## Passage 36: Going to Britain

- **ID:** `raw1_036` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Travel in Britain | **Sayfa:** 39, 40
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_036_going_to_britain.mp3` (*AU · en-AU-NatashaNeural · Hız -8%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. In Britain, there are one hundred ____ in a pound. *(İpucu: plural noun)*

2. You can only buy ____ in post offices, which are often inside small shops. *(İpucu: plural noun)*

3. Long-distance buses between towns and cities are called ____ and are cheaper than trains. *(İpucu: plural noun)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Which options does the passage recommend as cheaper alternatives to hotels?

   - A) Five-star city hotels

   - B) Private apartments near London stations

   - C) A 'bed and breakfast', youth hostels, or campsites

   - D) Only tourist offices and post offices

2. What advice does the passage give about the London underground?

   - A) Learn about how it works before using it

   - B) Buy a special railcard at a post office

   - C) Take a coach inside the underground stations

   - D) Avoid using it if you are under 24

3. Where can travelers find meals that are both good and cheap?

   - A) Pubs in small villages

   - B) Fish and chip shops and hamburger shops

   - C) Traditional hotel restaurants

   - D) Indian and Chinese restaurants


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Every bank in Britain changes foreign money.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Travelling by coach between towns and cities costs less than travelling by train.


### 2. Reading

Money
There are one hundred pence (100p) in a pound (£1). People sometimes say "p" instead of "pence", for example, "eighty p". Not all banks change foreign money, but you can usually find at least one bank in each town that will do it.

Where To Stay
Hotels are very expensive in Britain. A cheaper solution is a 'bed and breakfast' in someone's home. Information centres or tourist offices can help you to find these. There are also youth hostels and campsites in many places.

Writing Home
Stamps can only be bought in post offices; but nearly every village (or part of a town) has a post office. Often it is inside a small shop.

Getting Around
Trains are quite good in Britain. If you are under 24 or over 65, or if you are travelling with a family, ask about 'railcards' for cheaper fares on the train. There are also coaches (long-distance buses) between some towns and cities; these are cheaper than trains. In towns and cities, there are usually buses, and in London there is also an underground. But the underground is not easy to use, so you should learn about it before you use it. You can get information about the system in all the stations in London.

Eating Out
Restaurants are often expensive, and you cannot be sure the food will be good. But Indian and Chinese restaurants serve good meals, which are cheap. Pubs sometimes serve good food but it is usually quite expensive. Fast food shops - fish and chip shops, hamburger shops - are cheap, but the food is not always very good.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A*

1. Complete the sentence: In each town, you can find a bank which changes ____.

2. Complete the sentence: If you don't have much money, you can stay at a 'bed and breakfast' place, at a ____ or at a ____.

3. Complete the sentence: Travelling in London by ____ is not easy. You can find out about it in all the London stations.

4. Complete the sentence: Two examples of fast food shops are ____ and ____.


*B*

5. Where can you ask about a cheap place to stay?

6. Who can get 'railcards'? Complete: a) People ____. b) People over 65. c) People ____.

7. Where can you have a good and cheap meal?

8. In most villages, where is the post office?


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the main purpose of this passage?

   - A) British history and royal traditions

   - B) Practical travel advice for visitors to Britain on a budget

   - C) How to apply for a job in a British post office

   - D) A comparison between British and American food

2. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In the "Getting Around" section, the word "fares" is closest in meaning to ____.

   - A) schedules

   - B) stations

   - C) luggage limits

   - D) ticket prices

3. *[Inference]* Based on the passage, where should a budget traveler go if they want a meal that is both affordable and tasty?

   - A) An Indian or Chinese restaurant

   - B) A traditional pub

   - C) A hamburger shop

   - D) An expensive hotel restaurant


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Guided Summary: Budget Travel Tips for Britain** (70-90 words): Summarize the main budget travel tips for a tourist visiting Britain based on the passage.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: Planning a Trip Abroad** (80-110 words): When you visit a new city or country, what is the most important thing to plan before you go (money, transport, accommodation, or food)? Write a short paragraph explaining your opinion.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **bed and breakfast** (*oda ve kahvalti hizmeti veren kucuk konukevi*): We stayed at a cozy bed and breakfast near the coast.

- **youth hostel** (*genclik hosteli, ogrenci konukevi*): Students often stay in youth hostels because they are cheap and social.

- **coach** (*sehirlerarasi otobus*): It is cheaper to travel between the two cities by coach than by train.

- **fare** (*yolculuk ucreti, bilet ucreti*): Bus fares have increased slightly this year.

- **underground** (*metro, yeralti treni*): The London underground is fast, though it can be confusing at first.

- **foreign money** (*yabanci para, doviz*): You need to change some foreign money before paying in cash.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Para, konaklama, ulasim ve yemek basliklarindaki temel bilgileri ozetledim mi?

- [ ] Tavsiye verirken should / can yapilarini dogru kullandim mi?

- [ ] Seyahatle ilgili yeni kelimeleri (fare, coach, youth hostel vb.) dogru kullandim mi?

- [ ] Cumlelerin anlasilir ve dilbilgisi kurallarina uygun oldugunu kontrol ettim mi?

## Passage 37: Transportation in Turkey

- **ID:** `raw1_037` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Transportation & Economics | **Sayfa:** 41, 42
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_037_transportation_in_turkey.mp3` (*UK · en-GB-SoniaNeural · Hız -8%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. Highway vehicles transport ____ of the passengers in Turkey. *(İpucu: percentage)*

2. We need only 30 ____ to transport 1500 tons of cargo. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*

3. Trains use ____, which is less expensive than petrol. *(İpucu: noun)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. What percentage of the cargo in Turkey is transported by highway vehicles?

   - A) 5 percent

   - B) 25 percent

   - C) 75 percent

   - D) 90 percent

2. How much cargo can one train wagon carry?

   - A) 10 tons

   - B) 50 tons

   - C) 150 tons

   - D) 1500 tons

3. Why are trains described as more economical than trucks?

   - A) They use cheaper fuel and last longer.

   - B) They carry 90 percent of all passengers.

   - C) Each wagon costs much less than a locomotive.

   - D) They can travel faster on highways.


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Trains carry 25 percent of the cargo in Turkey.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — A truck can be used for more years than a train wagon.


### 2. Reading

There are two important types of transportation in Turkey: railway transportation and highway transportation. Highway vehicles (trucks, buses, cars, etc.) transport 90% of the passengers and 75% of the cargo in Turkey; but trains carry only 5% of the passengers and 25% of the cargo.

Now, we can compare the costs of trucks and trains. We want to transport 1500 tons of cargo. One truck can carry 10 tons, but one train wagon can carry 50 tons. We need 150 trucks but only 30 train wagons for 1500 tons of cargo.

1 train wagon can carry 50 tons. 1 truck can carry 10 tons.
1 train wagon costs $30,000.
1 truck costs $30,000.
1 locomotive costs $2,000,000.
1 locomotive + 30 wagons cost $2,900,000. 150 trucks cost $3,600,000.

Now, you can see which costs less and which costs more. There is another advantage of railway transport. We can use a train wagon for more years than we can use a truck.

We have to think about the cost of fuel for these vehicles, too. Trains use coal but trucks use petrol. We all know petrol is more expensive than coal. That's why trains are more economical than trucks.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*Comprehension*

1. Compare railway and highway vehicles in Turkey: a) Which carry more passengers?

2. Compare railway and highway vehicles in Turkey: b) Which carry less cargo?

3. Compare a train wagon (W) and a truck (T): a) Which is cheaper?

   - a) Train wagon (W)

   - b) Truck (T)

4. Compare a train wagon (W) and a truck (T): b) Which is more expensive?

   - a) Train wagon (W)

   - b) Truck (T)

5. Compare a train wagon (W) and a truck (T): c) Which can carry more cargo?

   - a) Train wagon (W)

   - b) Truck (T)

6. Compare a train wagon (W) and a truck (T): d) Which can carry less cargo?

   - a) Train wagon (W)

   - b) Truck (T)

7. Compare a train wagon (W) and a truck (T): e) Which can we use longer?

   - a) Train wagon (W)

   - b) Truck (T)

8. a) Which is cheaper, petrol or coal?

9. b) Which is more economical, a train or a truck?

10. What are the advantages of railway transportation?


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Author's Purpose]* What is the main purpose of the passage?

   - A) To describe the history of railways in Turkey

   - B) To explain how to build a locomotive and wagons

   - C) To compare the costs and efficiency of railway and highway transport

   - D) To encourage tourists to travel by bus instead of train

2. *[Detail]* Which of the following can be concluded from the passage?

   - A) Buying 150 trucks is cheaper than buying a train for 1500 tons of cargo.

   - B) Transporting 1500 tons of cargo by train requires a smaller total vehicle cost than by truck.

   - C) Trains carry most of the passengers in Turkey because they are cheaper.

   - D) One truck can carry five times as much cargo as one train wagon.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In the last sentence, "That's why trains are more economical than trucks," the word "economical" is closest in meaning to:

   - A) fast and modern

   - B) safe and comfortable

   - C) heavy and powerful

   - D) cost-effective and money-saving


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Guided Summary: Trains vs. Trucks** (60-80 words): Write a short summary comparing highway and railway transportation in Turkey based on the passage. Mention cargo capacity, lifespan, and fuel cost.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: Investing in Railways** (80-110 words): Do you think a country should invest more in railways or highways for transporting goods? Give at least two reasons to support your opinion.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **transportation** (*ulaşım, taşımacılık*): Public transportation is very important in big cities.

- **cargo** (*yük, kargo*): The ship carries heavy cargo across the sea.

- **compare** (*karşılaştırmak*): Let's compare the prices of these two buses.

- **advantage** (*avantaj, üstünlük*): One advantage of traveling by train is the comfort.

- **economical** (*ekonomik, tasarruflu*): Small cars are usually more economical than large trucks.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Demiryolu ve karayolu ulaşımı arasındaki en az iki farkı doğru belirttim mi?

- [ ] Karşılaştırma sıfatlarını (cheaper, more expensive, more economical) doğru kullandım mı?

- [ ] Metinden aldığım sayısal verileri ve birimleri (ton, yüzde, dolar) hatasız yazdım mı?

- [ ] Cümlelerimi hedef kelime aralığında, açık ve dilbilgisi kurallarına uygun yazdım mı?

- **ID:** `raw1_038` | **Seviye:** `A1` | **Konu:** Dining & Restaurants | **Sayfa:** 42
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_038_amy_s_restaurant.mp3` (*US · en-US-GuyNeural · Hız -8%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. Amy's Restaurant is a perfect place for ____ to eat lunch and dinner. *(İpucu: plural noun)*

2. It is clean, the service is fast, and the prices are ____. *(İpucu: adjective)*

3. Amy's has a different ____ every day. *(İpucu: noun)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Where is Amy's Restaurant located?

   - A) Near the train station

   - B) In Greenwich Village

   - C) Inside a university library

   - D) Next to a coffee factory

2. Which of the following is NOT included in the daily special?

   - A) Bread and butter

   - B) A mixed salad

   - C) Tomato soup

   - D) Coffee or tea

3. What does the speaker say about the other items on Amy's menu?

   - A) Only the daily special is good.

   - B) The menu items are very expensive.

   - C) The restaurant serves only breakfast.

   - D) All menu items give good value for the price.


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — The service at Amy's Restaurant is fast.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Amy's Restaurant is open 24 hours a day.


### 2. Reading

Amy's Restaurant is an example of a good place to eat in Greenwich Village. It's a perfect place for students to eat lunch and dinner. It's clean, the service is fast and the prices are reasonable. Amy's has a different special every day. The special includes fish or meat, vegetables, a mixed salad, bread and butter, dessert and coffee or tea - all for $3.45. The special and the other items on Amy's menu are always excellent for the prices.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*Comprehension*

1. Where can you eat in Greenwich Village?

2. What can you say about Amy's Restaurant? (Write only one sentence.)

3. How much is a special meal at Amy's?


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the main idea of the passage?

   - A) Amy's Restaurant offers good, affordable meals for students.

   - B) Greenwich Village has the most expensive restaurants in the city.

   - C) Students prefer cooking at home rather than eating out.

   - D) Amy's Restaurant only sells coffee, tea, and desserts.

2. *[Inference]* What can be inferred about students who eat at Amy's Restaurant?

   - A) Students work as waiters at Amy's Restaurant.

   - B) Students only eat fish and vegetables for dinner.

   - C) Students probably like places that are quick and inexpensive.

   - D) Students cannot eat lunch in Greenwich Village on weekends.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In the sentence "It's clean, the service is fast and the prices are reasonable," what does "reasonable" mean?

   - A) very high

   - B) fair and affordable

   - C) difficult to understand

   - D) different every hour


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Guided Response: Recommending Amy's Restaurant** (60-80 words): Write a short description of Amy's Restaurant for a friend who is a student. Include its location, service, prices, and what is in the daily special.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: My Favorite Student Cafe** (80-110 words): Write a short paragraph about a good restaurant or cafe for students near your school or home. Explain why you like it and what you can eat there.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **clean** (*temiz*): We always eat in a clean restaurant near campus.

- **reasonable** (*makul, uygun (fiyat)*): The prices at this cafe are very reasonable for students.

- **service** (*servis, hizmet*): The waiter brings our food quickly because the service is fast.

- **special** (*günün özel yemeği*): Today's special includes soup, chicken, and salad.

- **dessert** (*tatlı*): I like having fruit or cake for dessert after dinner.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Restoranın nerede olduğunu ve kimler için uygun olduğunu yazdım mı?

- [ ] Günlük özel menünün fiyatını ve içindekileri doğru belirttim mi?

- [ ] Geniş zaman (Simple Present) yapısını doğru kullandım mı?

- [ ] Yazım ve noktalama hatalarını kontrol ettim mi?

## Passage 39: Small Cars

- **ID:** `raw1_039` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Transportation & Environment | **Sayfa:** 43
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_039_small_cars.mp3` (*US · en-US-AriaNeural · Hız -8%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. Small cars are safer because they cannot go more than ____ per hour. *(İpucu: distance/speed)*

2. In the future, small cars will probably use ____. *(İpucu: noun)*

3. Using electric small cars will lead to less ____ in cities. *(İpucu: noun)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. How are the streets described at the beginning of the passage?

   - A) Quiet and empty

   - B) Always very crowded

   - C) Closed to buses and trucks

   - D) Clean and free of traffic

2. Why are small cars more economical?

   - A) You save petrol and spend less money.

   - B) They can carry heavy trucks on the road.

   - C) They can travel faster than 100 kilometers per hour.

   - D) They do not need any energy source at all.

3. What is a likely benefit of small cars in the future?

   - A) They will make streets completely empty.

   - B) They will travel much faster on highways.

   - C) They will help reduce pollution in cities.

   - D) They will replace all trains and buses.


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Small cars can easily travel faster than 100 kilometers per hour.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — The speaker predicts that many people will probably use small cars in the future.


### 2. Reading

When you go out, you can see a lot of cars, buses and trucks on the roads. The streets are always very crowded. Today, many people would rather use small cars. Small cars are cheaper and they are more economical because you can save petrol and you spend less money. These small cars are also safer because they can't go more than 100 kilometers per hour.

In the future, a lot of people will probably use small cars, and probably these cars will use electricity so there will be less pollution in cities.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*Comprehension*

1. Why would people rather use small cars? (Give 3 reasons.)

2. Why are small cars more economical than big cars? (Give 2 reasons.)

3. What would you rather have, a small car or a big car? Why?


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the passage mainly about?

   - A) Why buses and trucks are better than cars

   - B) How to drive safely at high speeds

   - C) The benefits of small cars today and in the future

   - D) Why cities should ban all types of vehicles

2. *[Inference]* What can be inferred from the second paragraph?

   - A) Electric cars are already the only cars on crowded streets.

   - B) Petrol-powered cars contribute to air pollution in cities.

   - C) Small cars will never be popular among city drivers.

   - D) Speeds over 100 kilometers per hour use less petrol.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In the sentence "Today, many people would rather use small cars," the phrase "would rather" is closest in meaning to:

   - A) must

   - B) never

   - C) used to

   - D) prefer to


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Guided Summary: Why Choose Small Cars?** (60-80 words): Summarize why people prefer small cars today and how small cars may change in the future.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: Small vs. Big Cars** (80-110 words): Would you rather have a small car or a big car in a city? Explain your choice with two or three reasons.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **crowded** (*kalabalık*): The streets are very crowded during rush hour.

- **would rather** (*tercih etmek*): I would rather take the bus than drive in heavy traffic.

- **save** (*tasarruf etmek, biriktirmek*): Walking to work helps you save money and stay healthy.

- **electricity** (*elektrik*): Modern trains use electricity instead of coal.

- **pollution** (*kirlilik*): Fewer cars on the road mean less air pollution.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Küçük arabaların tercih edilmesinin üç nedenini (ucuzluk, tasarruf, güvenlik) özetledim mi?

- [ ] Gelecek zaman ifadelerini (will probably) doğru kullandım mı?

- [ ] Kendi fikrimi nedenleriyle (because, so) destekledim mi?

- [ ] Kelime sayısı sınırına uydum mu ve yazım hatası yapmadım mı?

## Passage 40: Home Sweet Home

- **ID:** `raw1_040` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Housing & Lifestyle | **Sayfa:** 43, 44
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_040_home_sweet_home.mp3` (*UK · en-GB-RyanNeural · Hız -8%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. In Britain, ____ of the population does not live in rented housing. *(İpucu: percentage)*

2. Many young people live in small ____ or in their parents' house. *(İpucu: plural noun)*

3. People who buy old houses have to spend a lot of time and money on ____ and decoration. *(İpucu: plural noun)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. What does the passage say about housing in Britain?

   - A) Almost nobody in Britain wants to own a house.

   - B) All married couples live in big, new houses.

   - C) Most British people do not live in rented housing.

   - D) Only 40 percent of British people live in houses or flats.

2. Where are house prices quite low?

   - A) In the north of England and in Scotland

   - B) In the southeast of England

   - C) In the center of big cities only

   - D) Near the coast in southern England

3. Why do young people often buy old houses?

   - A) They are larger than modern flats in Scotland.

   - B) They are less expensive to buy.

   - C) They never need any repairs or decoration.

   - D) They are only found in the southeast of England.


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Some married young people in Britain still live in their parents' house.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Old houses in Britain are cheap and require no repairs.


### 2. Reading

For many British people, a house is the most important thing in their lives. In fact, 60% of the population doesn't live in rented housing. They would rather have their own houses, but prices are going up all the time, and many young people have not got enough money to buy a house. They live in small flats or in their parents' house (even when they are married).

Prices are especially high in the southeast of England. In the north, and in Scotland, prices are quite low. Young people often buy old houses, perhaps more than a hundred years old because they are not so expensive. But they have to spend a lot of time and money on repairs and decoration.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*Comprehension*

1. What is very important for British people?

2. How much of the population lives in rented housing?

3. a) Can many young people buy houses easily?

4. b) Why not?

5. c) What do most young people do?

6. Compare house prices in the southeast and in the north.

7. a) Why do most young people buy old houses?

8. b) What do they have to do then?


*Reference*

9. What does 'They' refer to in "They would rather have their own houses"?

10. What does 'They' refer to in "They live in small flats or in their parents' house"?

11. What does 'they' refer to in "because they are not so expensive"?

12. What does 'they' refer to in "But they have to spend a lot of time and money on repairs and decoration"?


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the main topic of the passage?

   - A) How to decorate a hundred-year-old British flat

   - B) Home ownership and housing challenges for young people in Britain

   - C) Why British families prefer renting flats in Scotland

   - D) The history of house construction in the southeast of England

2. *[Detail]* Based on the passage, approximately what percentage of the British population lives in rented housing?

   - A) 10%

   - B) 20%

   - C) 60%

   - D) 40%

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In the first paragraph, the phrase "prices are going up all the time" means that prices are:

   - A) increasing

   - B) falling

   - C) staying the same

   - D) stopping


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Guided Summary: Housing in Britain** (60-80 words): Summarize the housing situation for young people in Britain based on the passage. Mention prices, regions, and old houses.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: Buying an Old House** (80-110 words): Do you think buying an old house that needs repairs is a good idea for young people? Why or why not?


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **rented housing** (*kiralık konut*): Many students live in rented housing near the university.

- **go up** (*artmak, yükselmek*): Food and housing prices are going up every year.

- **flat** (*daire, apartman dairesi*): Most young couples start their lives in small flats.

- **repair** (*tamirat, onarım*): The old roof needs some urgent repairs before winter.

- **decoration** (*dekorasyon, süsleme*): They spent the weekend working on the decoration of their living room.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] İngiltere'deki gençlerin ev alma konusunda yaşadığı zorlukları açıkladım mı?

- [ ] Güneydoğu ile kuzey/İskoçya arasındaki fiyat farkına değindim mi?

- [ ] Eski ev almanın avantajını ve dezavantajını (tamirat/dekorasyon) belirttim mi?

- [ ] Zamirleri (they, their) ve bağlaçları (but, because) doğru kullandım mı?

## Passage 41: A Meeting

- **ID:** `raw1_041` | **Seviye:** `A1` | **Konu:** Social Introductions | **Sayfa:** 45
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_041_a_meeting.mp3` (*US · en-US-JennyNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. Dr. Keller is at a ____ at the United Nations. *(İpucu: noun)*

2. Dr. Keller has to return to ____ on Friday. *(İpucu: city name)*

3. The UN University does not have a ____, but it has offices in Tokyo. *(İpucu: noun)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Who introduces Dr. Keller and Mr. Hakada?

   - A) Dr. Keller's colleague from Geneva

   - B) A professor from the UN University

   - C) The host of the party

   - D) A speaker at the Toronto conference

2. Where will the World Health Conference take place this weekend?

   - A) Tokyo

   - B) Toronto

   - C) Geneva

   - D) New York

3. What does Mr. Hakada invite Dr. Keller to do?

   - A) To attend the conference in Toronto

   - B) To move to Tokyo for research

   - C) To return to Geneva on Friday

   - D) To visit the university offices in Tokyo


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Dr. Keller already knows a lot about the United Nations University.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Dr. Keller lives in New York.


### 2. Reading

Dr. Keller is at a party at the United Nations.

Host: Dr. Keller, I'd like you to meet Mr. Hakada. Mr. Hakada is the director of the United Nations University in Tokyo. Mr. Hakada, this is Dr. Keller. She works for the World Health Organisation.

Mr. Hakada: How do you do, Dr. Keller?

Dr. Keller: It's a pleasure to meet you, Mr. Hakada.

Mr. Hakada: Are you going to attend the World Health Conference in Toronto this weekend?

Dr. Keller: No. I have to return to Geneva on Friday. Please tell me about the UN University, Mr. Hakada. I know very little about it.

Mr. Hakada: We do a lot of research at the UN University. We do not have a campus but we have offices in Tokyo. Have you visited them?

Dr. Keller: No, I haven't.

Mr. Hakada: Would you like to see them when you come to Tokyo?

Dr. Keller: Yes, I'd like to. Thank you for the invitation.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*Comprehension Questions*

1. What does Mr. Hakada do?

2. What organisation does Dr. Keller work for?

3. Why isn't Dr. Keller going to attend the conference?

4. Where is the United Nations University?

5. What do they do at the UN University?

6. Does the university have a campus?

7. Has Dr. Keller visited the UN University?


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Detail]* Where is this conversation taking place?

   - A) At a university campus in Tokyo

   - B) At a conference hall in Toronto

   - C) At a party at the United Nations

   - D) At an office in Geneva

2. *[Inference]* What can be inferred about Dr. Keller's schedule this weekend?

   - A) She will be in Geneva instead of Toronto during the weekend.

   - B) She has already visited the UN University offices in Tokyo.

   - C) She plans to start working for the UN University soon.

   - D) She does not want to meet Mr. Hakada again.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* What does the word "attend" mean in Mr. Hakada's question about the World Health Conference?

   - A) organize

   - B) go to

   - C) cancel

   - D) pay for


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Guided Summary: Two Professionals Meet** (60-80 words): Write a short summary describing who Dr. Keller and Mr. Hakada are, why they are talking, and what Mr. Hakada invites Dr. Keller to do.

- **Task 2 — Meeting New People** (70-100 words): Write a short paragraph about how you introduce yourself to new people at a school or work event and what questions you ask them.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **director** (*mudur, yonetici*): She is the director of a large language school.

- **attend** (*katilmak*): I am going to attend a meeting tomorrow morning.

- **conference** (*konferans*): Doctors from many countries come to this medical conference.

- **research** (*arastirma*): Scientists do a lot of research in this laboratory.

- **campus** (*yerleske, kampus*): Our university has a green campus outside the city.

- **invitation** (*davet*): Thank you for your kind invitation to dinner.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Metindeki iki kisinin gorevlerini ve calistiklari kurumlari dogru yazdim mi?

- [ ] Basit simdiki ve gelecek zaman yapilarini (is, works, has to, going to) dogru kullandim mi?

- [ ] Noktalama isaretlerini ve buyuk harf kullanimini kontrol ettim mi?

- [ ] Verilen kelime sayisi sinirina uydum mu?

## Passage 42: Laura

- **ID:** `raw1_042` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Music & Biography | **Sayfa:** 46, 47
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_042_laura.mp3` (*AU · en-AU-NatashaNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. Laura's father worked as a ____ on the Bristol docks. *(İpucu: job title)*

2. At Bristol University, Laura studied ____ and economics. *(İpucu: academic subject)*

3. Laura and Tony have two children, a boy and a ____. *(İpucu: noun)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Where did Laura's parents originally come from?

   - A) Bristol

   - B) Ireland

   - C) Los Angeles

   - D) London

2. When did Laura start earning money from her music?

   - A) While she was still at comprehensive school

   - B) Right after she wrote her first song at university

   - C) After she finished university and joined another group

   - D) After she moved to Los Angeles with her husband

3. What did Laura and Tony decide to do a few days ago?

   - A) Buy a farmhouse in Ireland

   - B) Return to Bristol University

   - C) Open a music school in Bristol

   - D) Move from Britain to Los Angeles


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Laura has two brothers and a sister.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Laura and Tony bought a farmhouse in Bristol when they got married.


### 2. Reading

Laura Denison, famous singer and star of Top Twenty, was born in Bristol in 1953. Her parents originally came from Ireland. Laura's father was a customs officer on the Bristol docks. Unfortunately, he died a few years ago.

Laura, who is an only child, grew up in the suburbs of Bristol. She started her education at a local school and then moved to a large, modern comprehensive school in another part of the city.

After leaving school when she was 18, Laura went to Bristol University to study history and economics. She stayed there for three years. When she was at the university, she joined a university music group, and started to sing. It was at this time that she wrote her famous song 'The Price of Peace'. When she finished university she joined another group and began to earn money with her performances and songs.

When she was 21, she married Tony Harper, who was a student at that time. They have two children - a boy and a girl. When they got married they rented a beautiful farmhouse in a suburb of Bristol. They are still there but only a few days ago they decided to leave Britain and move to Los Angeles.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*Comprehension Questions*

1. When did Laura go to Bristol University?

2. Did she write her first song after she finished university?

3. How old was she when she married Tony Harper?

4. How many children have Laura and Tony got?

5. Where is their house?

6. Have they just decided to leave for Los Angeles?


*Reference Questions*

7. What does 'there' refer to in "She stayed there for three years."?

8. What does 'who' refer to in "When she was 21, she married Tony Harper, who was a student at that time."?


*Guided Questions*

9. Ask and answer if Laura has ever lived in Ireland.

10. Ask and answer how long Laura has lived in Bristol.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the main topic of the passage?

   - A) How to become a famous pop singer in Britain

   - B) Why Laura's parents left Ireland for Bristol

   - C) The life and career of singer Laura Denison

   - D) The history of music groups at Bristol University

2. *[Inference]* What can be inferred about the timing of Laura's marriage to Tony Harper?

   - A) She married Tony Harper before starting university.

   - B) She married Tony Harper around the time she finished university.

   - C) She moved to Los Angeles immediately after getting married.

   - D) She stopped singing completely after getting married.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In the passage, what does the word "suburb" refer to?

   - A) a residential area outside the city center

   - B) a busy port where ships arrive

   - C) a small village in another country

   - D) a famous concert hall for singers


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Biography Summary: Laura Denison** (70-90 words): Summarize the main events in Laura Denison's life from her childhood to her recent plans.

- **Task 2 — Moving to a New Country** (80-110 words): Laura and her family decided to leave Britain and move to Los Angeles. Do you think moving to a new country is a good idea for a family? Give your reasons.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **only child** (*tek cocuk*): He is an only child, so he spends a lot of time reading alone.

- **suburb** (*banliyo, dis mahalle*): Many families prefer to live in the quiet suburbs outside the city.

- **performance** (*performans, gosteri*): The singer gave a wonderful performance at the concert last night.

- **rent** (*kiralamak*): We rented a small flat near the university for one year.

- **earn money** (*para kazanmak*): The band hopes to earn money by playing at summer festivals.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Laura'nin hayatiyla ilgili olaylari kronolojik sirayla ozetledim mi?

- [ ] Past Simple zaman yapisini (was born, grew up, studied, married) dogru kullandim mi?

- [ ] Gorus paragrafimda net nedenler ve ornekler verdim mi?

- [ ] Cumle baslangiclarini, noktalama isaretlerini ve kelime sayisini kontrol ettim mi?

## Passage 43: Letter from Wendy

- **ID:** `raw1_043` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Travel & Invitations | **Sayfa:** 48
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_043_letter_from_wendy.mp3` (*UK · en-GB-SoniaNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. Wendy is writing the letter at Heathrow ____. *(İpucu: place)*

2. Tomorrow, Wendy has to take the tourists on a ____ of the city. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*

3. On Sunday, Wendy is free, but she must do some ____. *(İpucu: noun)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Why can't Wendy visit Janet in Torquay this weekend?

   - A) She already has plans to visit another friend in October.

   - B) She does not enjoy visiting Torquay in the summer.

   - C) She is very busy guiding tourists and doing housework.

   - D) She cannot find a flight from Heathrow Airport.

2. Where does Wendy invite Janet to come next week?

   - A) London

   - B) Torquay

   - C) Heathrow

   - D) Tokyo

3. When does Wendy think she can visit Janet?

   - A) Tomorrow

   - B) This Sunday

   - C) Next week

   - D) In October


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Wendy has to take the Japanese tourists to their hotel when they arrive.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Janet has already visited Wendy in London many times before.


### 2. Reading

8th August

Dear Janet,

Thanks very much for your invitation to come down to Torquay for the weekend. I'd love to come, but unfortunately August is a bad time for me to get away. We're very busy with the tourists here. At the moment, I'm waiting for a group of Japanese tourists. I'm actually writing this letter at Heathrow Airport! When they arrive, I have to take them to their hotel, and then tomorrow I have to take them on a bus tour of the city. I'm free on Sunday, but I must do some housework, so I'm afraid I can't come down to Torquay now. But I think I can visit you in October.

Wouldn't you like to come to London for a day or two instead? I'd love to see you. You can help me with the tourists! What about next week?

Wendy


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A. Complete the sentences.*

1. Wendy lives in ____ and Janet lives in ____.

2. Wendy is at the airport to meet ____.


*B. Mark the statements as True (T) or False (F).*

3. Wendy wrote the letter on Friday.

   - True

   - False

4. Wendy is a tourist guide.

   - True

   - False

5. Wendy is very busy in October.

   - True

   - False

6. Wendy is going to visit Janet next week.

   - True

   - False


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Author's Purpose]* What is Wendy's main purpose in writing this letter?

   - A) To complain about her job at Heathrow Airport

   - B) To decline an invitation politely and suggest another plan

   - C) To give Janet travel directions to Torquay

   - D) To ask Janet for money to visit Japan

2. *[Inference]* What can be inferred about Wendy's schedule next week?

   - A) Wendy plans to stop working next week.

   - B) Janet also works as a guide at Heathrow Airport.

   - C) Wendy will stay in Torquay all of next week.

   - D) Wendy expects to still be busy with tourists next week.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In the sentence "unfortunately August is a bad time for me to get away," what does "get away" mean?

   - A) leave for a short break or trip

   - B) find a new job

   - C) arrive at an airport

   - D) clean the house


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Guided Reply: Janet's Letter to Wendy** (60-80 words): Imagine you are Janet. Write a short reply to Wendy's letter thanking her, accepting her invitation to London next week, and mentioning October.

- **Task 2 — Working as a Tourist Guide** (80-110 words): Would you like to work as a tourist guide like Wendy? Write a paragraph explaining the good and difficult sides of the job and giving your opinion.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **get away** (*uzaklasmak, kacamak yapmak*): I need a quiet weekend to get away from the busy city.

- **do housework** (*ev isi yapmak*): Every Saturday morning, I have to do some housework before going out.

- **bus tour** (*otobus turu*): We took a bus tour around the historic centre of London.

- **I'm afraid** (*uzgunum ama, korkarim ki*): I'm afraid I can't join you for dinner tonight because I have to study.

- **instead** (*bunun yerine*): It rained on Saturday, so we watched a film at home instead.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Mektup formatina uygun hitap ve kapanis ifadeleri kullandim mi?

- [ ] Daveti nazikce reddedip alternatif bir plan sundum mu?

- [ ] Gelecek planlari ve zorunluluklar icin have to, must ve can yapilarini dogru kullandim mi?

- [ ] Yazim kurallarina ve kelime sinirina dikkat ettim mi?

## Passage 44: Education at Home (1)

- **ID:** `raw1_044` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Homeschooling & Education | **Sayfa:** 49
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_044_education_at_home_1.mp3` (*US · en-US-GuyNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. The Richardsons live in a big old house, 20 kilometres from ____. *(İpucu: city name)*

2. Both Marian and Roger are ____. *(İpucu: plural noun)*

3. Ginny says her parents are very good at music and ____. *(İpucu: plural noun)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. When did the reporter Jean Anderson visit the Richardson family?

   - A) On a Wednesday morning

   - B) On a Saturday afternoon

   - C) On a Friday evening

   - D) On a Sunday morning

2. Why does Marian Richardson dislike the schools in their town?

   - A) The schools are too far from their house.

   - B) The schools do not have any music rooms.

   - C) The teachers are weak and the students behave badly.

   - D) The schools are too expensive for five children.

3. Which musical instrument does Ginny play?

   - A) The guitar

   - B) The piano

   - C) The violin

   - D) The drums


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — There are five children in the Richardson family.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Donald is older than Ginny.


### 2. Reading

Some children in England don't go to school, but they study at home. Do you think it's right?

Our reporter, Jean Anderson, went to talk to some children. The Richardsons live in a big old house, 20 kilometres from London. The family is large - two girls and three boys. Our reporter visited them on a Wednesday morning. The children were at home. She heard music inside the house, a piano and a guitar. A normal family? A normal day? Yes, but in one way the Richardsons are very different. The children don't go to school. Their parents, Marian and Roger, teach them at home.

Jean Anderson spoke to Marian Richardson. Marian said: "My husband and I don't like the schools in our town. We don't think the teachers are very good. The children don't learn much. Some children at these schools can't read. It's terrible. Go to the schools and look: the children fight, smoke and drink. Roger and I are both teachers. We can teach them more at home."

Jean Anderson also spoke to Ginny. Ginny is one of the daughters of the family. She said: "We all love music. I play the piano and Donald plays the guitar. My parents are very good at music and languages. The schools here are very bad. We learn more at home. I think we're lucky."


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*Comprehension Questions*

1. What is Jean Anderson's job?

2. How many children have Marian and Roger got?

3. Why were the children at home on a Wednesday morning?

4. Who teaches them?

5. Where do the children study?


*6. Mark the statements as True (T) or False (F).*

6. Mrs. Richardson likes the teachers in the town.

   - True

   - False

7. Mrs. Richardson's children smoke and drink.

   - True

   - False


*Comprehension Questions*

8. What can't some of the children do at the schools in their town?


*8. Mark the statement as True (T) or False (F).*

9. Ginny is happy because she thinks she learns more at home.

   - True

   - False


*Comprehension Questions*

10. What does Ginny think about the schools in the town?


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is this passage mainly about?

   - A) How to become a good music teacher in England

   - B) Why reporter Jean Anderson dislikes local schools

   - C) A concert given by the five Richardson children

   - D) A family that chooses to educate their children at home

2. *[Author's Purpose]* Why does the reporter mention that she visited the family on a Wednesday morning?

   - A) Wednesday morning is normally a school day for children in England.

   - B) The Richardson family only studies music on Wednesdays.

   - C) The local schools are closed every Wednesday morning.

   - D) Jean Anderson only works on Wednesday mornings.

3. *[Detail]* How does Ginny feel about studying at home?

   - A) bored and lonely

   - B) worried about exams

   - C) pleased and fortunate

   - D) angry at her parents


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Guided Summary: The Richardson Family** (65-85 words): Summarize why Marian and Roger teach their children at home and how Ginny feels about it.

- **Task 2 — Home Education vs. School** (80-110 words): Some children study at home instead of going to school. Do you think studying at home is better than going to school? Explain your opinion.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **reporter** (*muhabir*): A newspaper reporter asked the students questions about their new school.

- **fight** (*kavga etmek*): The teacher told the boys not to fight in the playground.

- **be good at** (*bir konuda iyi olmak*): Our English teacher is very good at explaining difficult grammar rules.

- **lucky** (*sansli*): We feel very lucky to have such helpful neighbours.

- **language** (*dil, lisan*): Learning foreign languages helps you understand other cultures.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Ailenin cocuklari neden evde egittigini acikca ozetledim mi?

- [ ] Evde egitim hakkinda kendi gorusumu nedenleriyle yazdim mi?

- [ ] Parcalar icindeki kelimeleri (reporter, fight, lucky vb.) dogru anlamda kullandim mi?

- [ ] Dilbilgisi, yazim ve kelime sayisini kontrol ettim mi?

## Passage 45: Education at Home (2)

- **ID:** `raw1_045` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Homeschooling & Education | **Sayfa:** 50
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_045_education_at_home_2.mp3` (*US · en-US-AriaNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. About ____ families are teaching their children at home and doing very well. *(İpucu: number)*

2. Eve teaches her children writing and ____ in addition to reading books together. *(İpucu: noun)*

3. James says that when he and Eve were at school, everything was ____. *(İpucu: adjective)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. How do Stephen and Jason do their studies?

   - A) At a private school in Hertfordshire

   - B) With a professional tutor every morning

   - C) At home with their mother

   - D) In a group with their friends' children

2. How do the Tanner children practise writing and mathematics in daily life?

   - A) By studying a strict daily study programme

   - B) By writing shopping lists and paying at the shops

   - C) By taking online tests every weekend

   - D) By copying texts from library books

3. How do Eve and James' friends react to their decision?

   - A) They think home education is a bad idea.

   - B) They refuse to talk about school anymore.

   - C) All of them already teach their children at home.

   - D) Most are interested, and some are considering doing the same.


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — The Tanner parents believe outside activities like museums and sports are very important.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — James works as a teacher at a local school in Hertfordshire.


### 2. Reading

About 4000 families are teaching their children at home, and they are doing very well. One example is the Tanner family from Hertfordshire. Eve Tanner, 27, and her 29-year-old husband James do not send their children to school. The Tanner children, six-year-old Stephen and eight-year-old Jason, study at home with their mother. They have a bright, comfortable study in their home. All around there are lots of books and the children's paintings are on the wall.

James says, "Both Eve and I think we got nothing from school - only a diploma. We had no fun at all; in fact, everything was boring."

Some parents who are teaching their children at home have a study programme, but the Tanners don't. Eve says, "The children love books - they read all sorts of things. And, of course, we read story books together. I teach them writing and mathematics, too. They write shopping lists for me. Then they go shopping with me and pay for the things we buy."

Eve and James take their children to the library, to museums, to concerts, to swimming pools and to gymnastics. They think these activities are very important. What do friends think? Eve says, "Most of them are very interested. Some friends are also thinking about teaching their children at home."


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A*

1. The Tanners live in Hertfordshire.

   - True

   - False

2. Eve and James didn't go to school.

   - True

   - False

3. Eve and James don't have a study programme for their children.

   - True

   - False

4. The children study only reading and writing at home.

   - True

   - False

5. All Eve's and James' friends teach their children at home.

   - True

   - False


*B*

6. How many people are there in the Tanner family?

7. Why do Eve and James do lots of outside activities with their children?


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the main purpose of this passage?

   - A) To show how one family successfully educates their children at home

   - B) To argue that all schools in Hertfordshire should be closed

   - C) To explain how to get a teaching diploma without going to school

   - D) To compare public libraries with private study rooms

2. *[Inference]* What can be inferred about the Tanners' approach to education?

   - A) They follow a strict timetable from morning until evening.

   - B) They do not allow their children to read story books.

   - C) They prefer flexible, real-life learning over a formal curriculum.

   - D) They think sports and concerts are a waste of study time.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In the third paragraph, what does the phrase "all sorts of things" mean?

   - A) Only very short stories

   - B) Many different kinds of things

   - C) Expensive school textbooks

   - D) Books about mathematics only


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Summarize the Tanners' Home Education** (60-80 words): Write a short summary explaining how Eve and James Tanner educate their children at home and why they chose this way.

- **Task 2 — Home Education vs. Regular School** (80-110 words): Do you think it is better for children to study at home or go to a regular school? Give reasons for your opinion.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **study** (*çalışma odası*): The children do their homework in a bright and quiet study.

- **study programme** (*çalışma programı*): The students follow a weekly study programme at their school.

- **all sorts of things** (*her türlü şey*): We can buy all sorts of things at the big supermarket.

- **shopping list** (*alışveriş listesi*): Before going to the market, I always write a shopping list.

- **very important** (*çok önemli*): Reading books every day is very important for young learners.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Metinden aldığım bilgileri kendi cümlelerimle özetledim mi?

- [ ] Geniş zaman (Simple Present Tense) yapılarını doğru kullandım mı?

- [ ] Evde eğitim hakkındaki düşüncelerimi açık nedenlerle destekledim mi?

- [ ] Noktalama işaretlerini ve büyük/küçük harf kurallarını kontrol ettim mi?

## Passage 46: John Lennon

- **ID:** `raw1_046` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Music & Biography | **Sayfa:** 51, 52
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_046_john_lennon.mp3` (*UK · en-GB-RyanNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. At 15, while in art school, Lennon started his first rock group called the ____. *(İpucu: proper noun)*

2. When Pete Best left the band, ____ joined the Beatles as the drummer. *(İpucu: name)*

3. Every year thousands of people meet in New York's ____ to remember John Lennon. *(İpucu: place name)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Where was John Lennon born?

   - A) In Hamburg, Germany

   - B) In New York, the United States

   - C) In Liverpool, England

   - D) In London, Britain

2. When did the Beatles play in night clubs in Liverpool and Hamburg?

   - A) 1955 to 1958

   - B) 1958 to 1962

   - C) 1964 to 1967

   - D) 1970 to 1980

3. What did John Lennon do in 1967?

   - A) He acted in a film.

   - B) He wrote his first book.

   - C) He started the Silver Beatles.

   - D) He moved to Hamburg.


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Most of the Beatles' songs that Lennon wrote reached number one worldwide.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — John Lennon wrote both of his books in the same year.


### 2. Reading

A lot of people remember John Lennon for his music with the Beatles. He was serious and creative, and many young people of the 1960's and 1970's thought he spoke for them.

Lennon was born on October 9, 1940, in Liverpool, England. When he was a child, he listened to the music of rock and roll singers like Elvis Presley, Jerry Lee Lewis and Little Richard. At 15, when he was in art school, Lennon started his first rock-group, the Quarrymen. The members of the group were Paul McCartney, George Harrison and Pete Best, who played the drums.

The Quarrymen later changed their name to the Silver Beatles and then the Beatles. When Pete Best left the group, Ringo Starr joined the Beatles as the drummer. Between 1958 and 1962 the group played in night clubs in Liverpool and Hamburg, Germany. Then the Beatles made a record called 'I Wanna Hold Your Hand' and had their first big success in Britain. 'Please Please Me' came a short time after that and it became number one.

Lennon was both a singer and a songwriter. He wrote many of the Beatles' songs and most of these songs became number one all over the world. They were romantic and intelligent, and today's musicians still play them.

Lennon did not only make music and films with the Beatles. He also wrote two books, the first one in 1964 and the second one a year after that. In 1967 he acted in a film called How I Won The War.

On December 8, 1980 John Lennon died in New York, shot dead outside his apartment building. Every year thousands of people come together in New York's Central Park to remember him. For many people in the world, he was someone very special.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*Mark the best choice*

1. When he was very young, Lennon liked

   - a) the music of Little Richard

   - b) playing the drums

   - c) spending time in night clubs in Liverpool

   - d) New York's Central Park

2. ____ was the name of Lennon's first group.

   - a) The Silver Beatles

   - b) The Beatles

   - c) The Quarrymen

   - d) Please Please Me

3. The Beatles became famous

   - a) in 1958

   - b) when Lennon was 15 years old

   - c) with the song 'I Wanna Hold Your Hand'

   - d) in Hamburg

4. In 1965,

   - a) Lennon left for the United States

   - b) Lennon wrote his second book

   - c) Pete Best left the group

   - d) Lennon was an art student

5. Which of the following is a true statement?

   - a) How I Won The War is the name of a book.

   - b) Many musicians today don't know anything about the Beatles.

   - c) Lennon died in Britain.

   - d) Lennon had great success with his songs.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is a key idea about John Lennon shown in the passage?

   - A) John Lennon preferred acting in films to writing songs.

   - B) The Beatles became famous only after moving to New York.

   - C) John Lennon stopped writing music after 1967.

   - D) John Lennon was talented in several areas besides music.

2. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In the first paragraph, what does "he spoke for them" mean?

   - A) He was a famous radio newsreader for young people.

   - B) He expressed the thoughts and feelings of young people.

   - C) He taught young people how to speak English clearly.

   - D) He often gave speeches at art schools.

3. *[Inference]* What can be inferred from the passage about John Lennon's legacy?

   - A) People mostly remember him for his two books.

   - B) His music was popular only in Liverpool and Hamburg.

   - C) His music and memory remain influential many years after his death.

   - D) Today's musicians think his songs were too simple.


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Biography Summary: John Lennon** (60-80 words): Write a short summary of John Lennon's life and career based on the passage.

- **Task 2 — A Musician I Admire** (80-110 words): Write a paragraph about a famous musician or singer you admire and explain why people still remember or listen to their music.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **creative** (*yaratıcı*): She is a very creative artist who paints colourful pictures.

- **songwriter** (*şarkı yazarı*): Mozart was a famous composer, and Lennon was a great songwriter.

- **make a record** (*plak yapmak / kayıt yapmak*): The band made a record and it quickly became number one.

- **have big success** (*büyük başarı elde etmek*): Their new song had big success all over the country.

- **come together** (*bir araya gelmek*): Many people come together in the park every spring to celebrate music.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] John Lennon'ın hayatındaki olayları doğru tarih sırasıyla anlattım mı?

- [ ] Geçmiş zaman (Simple Past Tense) fiillerini doğru kullandım mı?

- [ ] Fikirlerimi açık ve kısa cümlelerle ifade ettim mi?

- [ ] Özel isimlerin ve şarkı/film adlarının yazımını kontrol ettim mi?

## Passage 47: Guide to Good Eating

- **ID:** `raw1_047` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Food & Restaurants | **Sayfa:** 53
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_047_guide_to_good_eating.mp3` (*US · en-US-JennyNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. Cornwell is the northern ____ of Bristol where about 14,000 people live. *(İpucu: noun)*

2. The Rajah Restaurant is quite small and has only ____. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*

3. The wonderful Indian meal at the restaurant cost only ____ for two people. *(İpucu: price)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. What did the writer think about restaurants in Cornwell before last Saturday?

   - A) Many famous Indian and Italian restaurants

   - B) Only some pizza houses and a few hamburger cafes

   - C) Expensive seafood restaurants near the post office

   - D) No places to eat at all

2. How did the writer and his friend get to the Rajah Restaurant?

   - A) By bus and then on foot

   - B) By car and then by taxi

   - C) By train to West Lane

   - D) By bicycle from central Bristol

3. Why does the writer advise readers to hurry if they want to try the restaurant?

   - A) Because the restaurant is closing next week

   - B) Because the food becomes cold very quickly

   - C) Because the restaurant is small and only has six tables

   - D) Because the prices will go up soon


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — The writer enjoyed all the food and wine they had at the Rajah Restaurant.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — The writer went to the restaurant with his old school friend George.


### 2. Reading

By Tom Cousin

A few days ago I was in Cornwell, the northern suburb of Bristol. It isn't big - about 14,000 people live there. I thought there weren't any good restaurants in Cornwell. I thought there were only some pizza houses and a few hamburger cafes.

But I was wrong. A friend took me out to dinner last Saturday. We got off the bus at Cornwell Central, and walked down George Road to the post office. Then we turned left into a little street called West Lane, we walked a few metres, and we entered a small restaurant.

The Rajah Restaurant has six tables, and there wasn't anyone there when we arrived. We had a wonderful Indian meal there. The Tandoori chicken was very tasty, and the curries were hot and delicious. The wine was good and cheap, the fruit salad was really exotic and the meal cost only £12 for two.

Try it - but hurry. You can't seat 14,000 people at only six tables!


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A*

1. Where can you eat in Cornwell? Complete the list: a) ____ b) ____ c) The Rajah Restaurant.

2. Where is the Rajah Restaurant in Cornwell?

3. Who went there last Saturday?

4. What did they have at the Rajah Restaurant?


*B*

5. Cornwell is a small place.

   - True

   - False

6. They drove to Cornwell.

   - True

   - False

7. The restaurant was crowded when they arrived.

   - True

   - False

8. The writer liked the meal but he thought it was a bit expensive.

   - True

   - False


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Author's Purpose]* What is the main purpose of Tom Cousin's article?

   - A) To complain about bad bus services in Bristol

   - B) To give directions to the Cornwell post office

   - C) To recommend a small and affordable Indian restaurant in Cornwell

   - D) To compare pizza houses with hamburger cafes

2. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In the third paragraph, what does the word "exotic" most likely mean?

   - A) Too sweet and salty

   - B) Very small and ordinary

   - C) Hot and spicy

   - D) Unusual and from a distant or tropical place

3. *[Inference]* Why does the writer joke, "You can't seat 14,000 people at only six tables!"?

   - A) He expects the restaurant to become popular once people hear about it.

   - B) He thinks the restaurant only serves customers on Saturdays.

   - C) He believes 14,000 people have already eaten there this week.

   - D) He thinks the restaurant should move to a bigger street.


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Restaurant Visit Summary** (60-80 words): Summarize Tom Cousin's visit to The Rajah Restaurant, including where it is, what he ate, and what he thought of it.

- **Task 2 — Write a Short Restaurant Review** (80-110 words): Write a short review of a good restaurant or cafe you know. Mention where it is, what kind of food it serves, the prices, and why you recommend it.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **suburb** (*banliyö, kenar mahalle*): We live in a quiet suburb outside the city centre.

- **take someone out to dinner** (*birini akşam yemeğine çıkarmak*): My friend took me out to dinner for my birthday.

- **get off the bus** (*otobüsten inmek*): Remember to get off the bus near the post office.

- **turn left** (*sola dönmek*): Go straight and then turn left into the next street.

- **tasty** (*lezzetli*): The homemade soup was very tasty and warm.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Restoranın yerini ve yemekleri doğru sıfatlarla tanımladım mı?

- [ ] Geçmiş zaman fiillerini (went, ate, walked) doğru kullandım mı?

- [ ] Tavsiyemi açıklarken yemek, fiyat ve ortam hakkında bilgi verdim mi?

- [ ] Cümleler arası bağlaçları (and, but, then) uygun şekilde kullandım mı?

## Passage 48: Radio Times Reader Holidays

- **ID:** `raw1_048` | **Seviye:** `B1` | **Konu:** Travel & Holidays | **Sayfa:** 54
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_048_radio_times_reader_holidays.mp3` (*AU · en-AU-NatashaNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. A one-week holiday in Malta costs from only ____. *(İpucu: price)*

2. The Egypt tour includes fantastic trips on the ____ and to the famous pyramids. *(İpucu: place name)*

3. Departures for the Egypt holiday are on ____ in November, February, and March. *(İpucu: day of the week)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Where do holidaymakers stay during the Malta holiday?

   - A) A 5-star hotel in Cairo

   - B) The 3-star San Pawl Hotel

   - C) A small guesthouse near the airport

   - D) A boat on the River Nile

2. Which of the following is included in the Malta holiday?

   - A) A full 7-day tour of Cairo and Alexandria

   - B) A trip on the River Nile

   - C) Scheduled flights from Heathrow every Thursday

   - D) A bottle of wine in the room on arrival

3. How are flights from Manchester offered for the Malta holiday?

   - A) Every Thursday at no extra cost

   - B) Only during November and February

   - C) At a supplement, in addition to weekly Heathrow flights

   - D) Only for guests booking a fortnight


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Visitors on both the Malta and Egypt holidays receive a travel bag.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — According to the brochure, Egypt usually has cloudy skies during most of the year.


### 2. Reading

MALTA

Warm winter sunshine on the friendly island of Malta - from only £149 for a week and £179 for a fortnight. And just look what is included in the price!

* Half board accommodation with English breakfast at the excellent 3-star San Pawl Hotel (supplements for singles)
* Special trips to Handicrafts Centre and other interesting places
* Bottle of wine in room on arrival
* Entertainment in hotel
* Services of our courier
* Superb travel bag

Malta - Clear blue seas, fascinating sights, warm weather, welcoming people (many speak English). Spectacular excursions and a great 3-star hotel as your base. Flights from Heathrow - every week on Friday from 3/11/95 to 29/3/96. Manchester flights also available at a supplement.

EGYPT

Visit Egypt to an exciting blend of ancient and modern, of beautiful wild deserts and vibrant towns and cities - all lying under a cloudless blue sky for most of the year!

Prices from only £385 for a full 7-day tour.

* Half board
* Visits to Cairo, Alexandria and many other famous places
* Fantastic trips on River Nile and to the famous pyramids
* Fully guided tours throughout your stay
* Scheduled flights from Heathrow and Manchester
* Superb travel bag

Egypt - superb friendly extrovert people, a land with an unparalleled and fascinating history. This excellent tour will let you discover for yourself as much as possible of this unforgettable country. Departures are on Thursdays from 5 - 12 November and 18 February to 10 March.

Do join us on one of our superb Radio Times Reader Holidays.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*Mark the statements as True (T) or False (F)*

1. A two-week holiday in Egypt costs £385.

   - True

   - False

2. Both holidays offer sightseeing trips.

   - True

   - False

3. If you speak only English, you can have language problems in Malta.

   - True

   - False

4. On the Egypt holiday you can leave from Manchester Airport or Heathrow Airport.

   - True

   - False

5. There are flights to Malta every Friday from November 1995 until the end of March 1996.

   - True

   - False


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Detail]* How do the two holidays advertised in the brochure mainly differ?

   - A) Malta focuses on relaxing winter sunshine and value stays, while Egypt highlights guided historical sightseeing.

   - B) Malta is much more expensive per week than the 7-day tour in Egypt.

   - C) The Malta holiday includes fully guided tours to Cairo and the River Nile.

   - D) Flights to Egypt depart every Friday from November until the end of March.

2. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In the Malta section, what does the word "fortnight" mean?

   - A) Four nights

   - B) One month

   - C) Two weeks

   - D) Seven days

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In the Egypt section, what does "an unparalleled and fascinating history" mean?

   - A) Completely forgotten by modern historians

   - B) Greater or better than anything else; unmatched

   - C) Short and easy to understand

   - D) Similar to every other Mediterranean country


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Compare the Two Holiday Packages** (70-90 words): Compare the Malta and Egypt holiday packages in terms of price, length of stay, and included activities.

- **Task 2 — Choosing Your Ideal Winter Holiday** (120-160 words): Which of the two holidays (Malta or Egypt) would you prefer to take, and why? Support your choice with details from the brochure and your own reasons.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **fortnight** (*iki hafta, on dört gün*): We stayed in Malta for a fortnight last winter.

- **half board** (*yarım pansiyon (kahvaltı ve akşam yemeği dahil)*): The hotel offers half board, so breakfast and dinner are included.

- **supplement** (*ek ücret, ilave bedel*): Single travellers have to pay a small supplement for a private room.

- **excursion** (*kısa gezi, tur*): Our package includes three excursions to historic villages nearby.

- **unparalleled** (*eşsiz, benzersiz*): The museum exhibits give visitors an unparalleled view of ancient life.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] İki tatil paketini fiyat, süre ve sunulan hizmetler açısından karşılaştırdım mı?

- [ ] Kendi tatil tercihimi açık nedenlerle ve metinden örneklerle destekledim mi?

- [ ] Karşılaştırma bağlaçlarını (whereas, while, on the other hand) doğru kullandım mı?

- [ ] Seyahat ve konaklama terimlerini doğru yazdım mı?

## Passage 49: Seaford School & Bedgebury School

- **ID:** `raw1_049` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Summer Schools & Facilities | **Sayfa:** 55
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_049_seaford_school_bedgebury_school.mp3` (*UK · en-GB-SoniaNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. At Seaford School, the large open-air heated swimming pool has a ____. *(İpucu: noun)*

2. Bedgebury School has large playing fields for football, hockey, and ____. *(İpucu: noun (sport))*

3. At Bedgebury School, boys and girls have rooms on ____. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. What age group is the summer course at Seaford School for?

   - A) 13 to 16 years old

   - B) 10 to 15 years old

   - C) 8 to 12 years old

   - D) 15 to 18 years old

2. How far is Bedgebury School from London?

   - A) 115 kilometres from London

   - B) 15 kilometres from London

   - C) 55 kilometres from London

   - D) 75 kilometres from London

3. Which other activities are offered at Seaford School besides swimming, tennis, and football?

   - A) Computers and riding

   - B) Golf and sailing

   - C) Music and drama

   - D) Fishing and cycling


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Bedgebury School has a lake where students can do watersports.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Students at Bedgebury School stay in private single bedrooms.


### 2. Reading

SEAFORD SCHOOL
1 3/4 hours by train from London (115 kms)
Ages: 10-15 | 25 July - 14 August

The seaside town of Seaford is on England's south coast. It is an excellent location from which to explore the South Downs and such towns as Hastings. As a Summer School centre, it offers an ideal combination of sea and country.

In the grounds of the main school are the large, open-air heated swimming pool (with lifeguard), six tennis courts and football fields. A combination of sea air and energetic activities makes for healthy appetites!

Other Activities:
* Computers
* Riding

BEDGEBURY SCHOOL
(Ages 13 - 16) Residential

Bedgebury School is situated in a beautiful town 55 kms from London. It is a private school with large grounds and superb 'Action' facilities which include:
* Large playing fields (football, hockey, volleyball)
* A lake for watersports
* A modern indoor sports centre
* Hard tennis courts

All these are located within the private grounds of the school.

The students sleep in simply furnished rooms with 5 beds. Boys and girls have rooms on different floors and are under the supervision of resident teachers.

Special Features:
* For sports enthusiasts - excellent facilities.
* A wealth of interesting places to visit nearby: cathedrals, unusual museums and country parks.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A*

1. Bedgebury School is in London.

   - True

   - False

2. The course at Seaford School is in the summer.

   - True

   - False

3. Bedgebury School is for boys only.

   - True

   - False

4. Five students stay in one room at Bedgebury School.

   - True

   - False

5. You can go to Seaford only by car or by bus.

   - True

   - False


*B*

6. Where is Seaford in England?

7. What sort of sports can you do at Seaford School?


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Detail]* What makes Seaford's location especially attractive for a summer school?

   - A) It has a modern indoor sports centre and a lake.

   - B) It is only 55 kilometres away from London.

   - C) It combines the seaside with access to the countryside.

   - D) It is famous for its cathedrals and unusual museums.

2. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In the Bedgebury School description, what does 'supervision' mean?

   - A) Strict punishment

   - B) Care and watching over by adults

   - C) Extra language lessons

   - D) Free weekend travel

3. *[Author's Purpose]* What is the main purpose of these two texts?

   - A) To advertise two residential or summer school programmes for young people

   - B) To compare the academic exam results of two British schools

   - C) To explain how to travel by train along England's south coast

   - D) To complain about the lack of sports facilities in London schools


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Comparing Two Schools** (65-85 words): Summarize the main features of Seaford School and Bedgebury School, including their locations, age groups, and activities.

- **Task 2 — My Preferred Summer School** (80-110 words): Which school would you prefer to attend, Seaford School or Bedgebury School? Give two or three reasons for your choice.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **be situated in** (*... konumlanmış, ... yer alan*): Our hotel is situated in a quiet village near the sea.

- **facilities** (*imkanlar, tesisler*): The university has excellent sports facilities for students.

- **supervision** (*gözetim, denetim*): Young children should always swim under adult supervision.

- **combination** (*birleşim, kombinasyon*): This coastal town offers a great combination of history and nature.

- **enthusiast** (*meraklı, tutkun, hayran*): Football enthusiasts will love visiting this famous stadium.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Her iki okulun konumunu, yaş aralığını ve temel spor imkanlarını doğru bir şekilde karşılaştırdım.

- [ ] Yazımda 'situated in', 'supervision' veya 'facilities' gibi metinden öğrendiğim en az 3 kelimeyi kullandım.

- [ ] Cümlelerimde tekil-çoğul uyumunu ve 'there is / there are' yapılarını hatasız kurdum.

- [ ] Yazımı tamamladıktan sonra noktalama işaretlerini ve yazım kurallarını gözden geçirdim.
