## Passage 50: Letter from George

- **ID:** `raw1_050` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Road Accident Narrative | **Sayfa:** 56, 57
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_050_letter_from_george.mp3` (*US · en-US-GuyNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. George and his family were looking forward to their ____ in France. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*

2. When they entered the Dartford Tunnel, they were driving in the ____. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*

3. When George woke up and saw a bright light, a ____ was standing over him. *(İpucu: noun (profession))*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Why did George not say much when Tim visited him in the hospital?

   - A) He was angry about the accident.

   - B) His throat hurt too much to speak.

   - C) The painkillers made him sleepy.

   - D) The doctor told him not to talk.

2. When was their ferry from Dover supposed to leave?

   - A) Half-past five

   - B) Midnight

   - C) Eight o'clock

   - D) Quarter past nine

3. Why did George fail to see the red lights of the coach in front of him in time?

   - A) He looked down to get 60 pence from his coat pocket.

   - B) He fell asleep at the wheel because he was tired.

   - C) He turned around to talk to the children in the back.

   - D) A lorry hit them from the side and pushed them off the road.


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — The weather and road conditions were wet, and the traffic was heavy that evening.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — George's car hit the coach first, and then the lorry hit them from behind.


### 2. Reading

2 Parkway Street
Clair
Suffolk
27th August, 1996

Dear Tim,

Thanks for your letter and for coming to the hospital. I'm sorry I didn't say much - the painkillers made me sleepy. I came home last weekend. My legs still hurt, but I'm walking round the garden as much as I can, so it's getting better.

It was my first accident, you know, and I can tell you it was a dreadful shock. Everyone thinks it'll never happen to them, don't they? I suppose I was tired that afternoon. I finished work and we left here at about half-past five. The children were excited and we were all looking forward to our camping holiday in France. The ferry from Dover didn't leave until midnight, so we thought we had plenty of time. But the traffic was very heavy and there was a lot of water on the road. In fact, we didn't get to the motorway until nearly 8 o'clock.

Perhaps I was driving too fast, I don't know, but everything seemed to be okay. It was a quarter past nine when we went into the Dartford Tunnel. We were in the outside lane. I asked Anne for 60p to pay at the other end of the Tunnel. She hadn't got any change, so I took my hand off the wheel and felt in my coat pocket. I looked down at the money. It was only for a split second, but that was long enough. I just didn't see the red lights of the coach in front of us until it was too late. Of course I slammed on the brakes and we skidded on the wet road. I can remember two crashes - I think the one from behind was first - the lorry. It pushed us into the coach. Then everything went black.

The next thing I saw was a bright light. A fireman was standing over me. He said, "Lie still. We'll soon cut you free." Then a doctor gave me an injection and everything went black again.

George


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A*

1. Why was George in hospital last week?

2. Who visited him in hospital?

3. What's the matter with George now?

4. Which country were George and his family going to for their holiday?

5. What sort of holiday was it?

6. How did they travel?

7. What time did they get to the motorway?

8. How much does it cost for a car to use the Dartford Tunnel?

9. Who did George see first when he opened his eyes?


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Inference]* What does George mean when he writes 'everything went black'?

   - A) It became dark because the tunnel lights went out.

   - B) He lost consciousness and could not see or remember anything.

   - C) The car headlights broke during the crash.

   - D) He closed his eyes to avoid seeing the bright light.

2. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In paragraph 3, what does 'a split second' mean?

   - A) Several minutes

   - B) About half an hour

   - C) Until the end of the tunnel

   - D) A very brief moment

3. *[Inference]* What can we infer about the end of the accident from what the fireman said?

   - A) George fell out of the car onto the motorway.

   - B) The family managed to catch the midnight ferry on time.

   - C) George was trapped inside the vehicle after the crash.

   - D) Anne was driving the car when they entered the tunnel.


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Summarizing George's Accident** (70-90 words): Summarize how George's accident happened and how he is recovering now.

- **Task 2 — Staying Safe on the Road** (80-110 words): What lessons can drivers learn from George's accident to stay safe on the road? Give two or three practical tips.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **look forward to** (*dört gözle beklemek*): The children are really looking forward to the summer holidays.

- **dreadful** (*korkunç, berbat*): It was a dreadful storm that damaged many roofs.

- **skid** (*kaymak, savrulmak (tekerlek/araç)*): The car skidded on the icy road and hit a fence.

- **change** (*bozuk para*): I didn't have enough change to pay for the bus ticket.

- **painkiller** (*ağrı kesici*): The doctor gave me painkillers after the operation.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Kazanın nasıl gerçekleştiğini kronolojik sırayla (saat ve olay akışına göre) özetledim.

- [ ] Geçmiş zaman (Past Simple) fiillerini ve zaman bağlaçlarını (first, then, until) doğru kullandım.

- [ ] Metinden öğrendiğim 'look forward to', 'skid' veya 'dreadful' gibi en az 3 kelimeyi cümlelerimde kullandım.

- [ ] Yazımda noktalama ve büyük/küçük harf hatalarını kontrol ettim.

## Passage 51: Nottingham

- **ID:** `raw1_051` | **Seviye:** `B1` | **Konu:** British Towns & Culture | **Sayfa:** 58
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_051_nottingham.mp3` (*US · en-US-AriaNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. To meet the cultural needs of the town, Nottingham has ____, several cinemas, a large university, and museums. *(İpucu: number + noun)*

2. Bangor is still clean and quiet because it is small and has little or no ____. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*

3. For people who enjoy sports, Bangor offers an 18-hole ____ and a large modern swimming pool. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Why is Nottingham an important industrial town in the Midlands?

   - A) It is near one of Britain's richest coalfields.

   - B) It is the largest port city in North Wales.

   - C) It has the oldest cathedral in England.

   - D) It has no heavy industry at all.

2. What is the population of Bangor?

   - A) Almost 300,000 people

   - B) About 130,000 people

   - C) About 30,000 people

   - D) Only 13,000 people

3. What can visitors find inside or at Penrhyn Castle near Bangor?

   - A) A water sports centre

   - B) A cricket ground

   - C) A railway museum

   - D) A football stadium


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Nottingham is described as an ugly and dirty city because of its factories.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Trent Bridge is a famous place to watch or play cricket in Nottingham.


### 2. Reading

Nottingham is a busy industrial, commercial and university town with almost 300,000 inhabitants. Close to the centre of one of Britain's richest coalfields, it is important in the Midlands for its many kinds of industry.

Nottingham is an industrial city, but it is not at all ugly or dirty. Nottingham lies on the side of a hill near the River Trent, only a short distance away from Sherwood Forest, the famous home of Robin Hood.

The city's castle is also quite famous, and Nottingham has three theatres, several cinemas, a very large university and various museums and galleries to look after the cultural needs of the town. Nottingham also enjoys some of the best sport in the country, with two professional football clubs, a first-class cricket ground (Trent Bridge) and the National Water Sports Centre in its large country park.

BANGOR

Centred round its university on the hill and its cathedral below, Bangor is one of the most attractive towns in North Wales. It is a small town with a population of only 13,000 and with little or no heavy industry. Therefore, it is still clean and quiet.

Bangor is an important cultural centre. There is a theatre and a cinema and visitors should not miss the Museum of Welsh Antiquities or Bangor's famous public garden, Gardd yr Esgob. A little further away, there is Penrhyn Castle with its railway museum.

For sports enthusiasts, Bangor has an 18-hole golf course and a large modern swimming-pool.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A*

1. Where is the home of Robin Hood?

2. Why is Bangor a clean and quiet town? (Give two reasons.)


*B*

3. Going to a cinema and playing golf are two things that you can do in your spare time in Bangor.

   - True

   - False

4. Nottingham is a clean and beautiful city.

   - True

   - False

5. Except for two football clubs and a cricket ground, there are no other sports facilities in Nottingham.

   - True

   - False


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Comparison / Detail]* What is the main difference between Nottingham and Bangor according to the passage?

   - A) Bangor has more professional football clubs than Nottingham.

   - B) Nottingham has no cultural places such as theatres or museums.

   - C) Nottingham is much larger and more industrial than Bangor.

   - D) Bangor is located in the Midlands near the River Trent.

2. *[Inference]* What does the writer mean by saying 'visitors should not miss the Museum of Welsh Antiquities'?

   - A) Visitors might get lost trying to find the museum.

   - B) The museum is highly recommended and worth seeing.

   - C) The museum is only open for a short time each day.

   - D) Visitors have to pay a high price to enter the museum.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In the first sentence, what does the word 'inhabitants' mean?

   - A) Residents living in the city

   - B) Tourists visiting the castle

   - C) Students at the university

   - D) Workers in the coalfields


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Comparing Nottingham and Bangor** (70-90 words): Compare Nottingham and Bangor in terms of their size, industry, cultural attractions, and sports facilities.

- **Task 2 — Big City vs. Small Town Living** (90-120 words): Would you prefer to live in a large, busy city like Nottingham or a small, quiet town like Bangor? Explain your reasons with examples.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **inhabitant** (*sakin, yaşayan kimse, nüfus*): London has millions of inhabitants from all over the world.

- **heavy industry** (*ağır sanayi*): Steel production and shipbuilding are examples of heavy industry.

- **attractive** (*çekici, güzel*): The historic streets make this village very attractive to tourists.

- **antiquities** (*tarihi eserler, antikalar*): The museum displays Roman antiquities found in the region.

- **gallery** (*sanat galerisi*): The city centre has several modern art galleries.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Nottingham ve Bangor şehirlerini nüfus, sanayi, kültür ve spor alanlarında açıkça karşılaştırdım.

- [ ] Metinden öğrendiğim 'inhabitants', 'heavy industry' veya 'attractive' gibi kelimeleri doğru bağlamda kullandım.

- [ ] 'Whereas', 'however' veya 'both' gibi karşılaştırma bağlaçlarıyla fikirlerimi birbirine bağladım.

- [ ] Yazım ve dil bilgisi kurallarını kontrol ederek metni düzenledim.

## Passage 52: A Language Teacher's Personal Opinion

- **ID:** `raw1_052` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Learning English | **Sayfa:** 59
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_052_a_language_teacher_s_personal_op.mp3` (*UK · en-GB-RyanNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. At ____, you can always hear English, and at the cinema you can watch films in English. *(İpucu: noun (plural))*

2. Advertisements often claim you can read Shakespeare or Dickens after going to classes for ____. *(İpucu: time period)*

3. In real life, we use language to talk to friends, neighbours, or a ____ in a restaurant. *(İpucu: noun (profession))*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Which language does a Mexican businessman usually speak when doing business with a Chinese businessman?

   - A) Spanish

   - B) English

   - C) Italian

   - D) Chinese

2. How does the writer feel about advertisements for short language courses and cassettes?

   - A) Helpful and honest

   - B) Very cheap and fun

   - C) Exciting for teachers

   - D) Awful and unrealistic

3. According to the teacher, what is actually necessary for learning a new language?

   - A) Buying expensive cassettes and listening at home

   - B) Reading Shakespeare plays in the first month

   - C) Talking to real people and listening to them over a long time

   - D) Taking a three-month course with a super teacher


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — International news reports between newspapers in different countries are often sent in English.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — The writer believes repeating sentences from a cassette is enough to speak English well.


### 2. Reading

Why is English such an important language today? There are several reasons. Many newspapers send their reports to other newspapers in different countries in English - not in Spanish or Italian. English is also the language of the business world. For example, when a Mexican businessman buys something from a Chinese businessman he usually speaks English. At airports you can always hear English, and at the cinema you can watch a film in English. Today a lot of people want to learn this language. They go to language schools in England or take English courses in their own countries.

Every day I see advertisements in the newspapers and on buses. They are about language courses. Sometimes they also advertise language cassettes in English. They say you can learn a second language very fast and easily. They often say you can read plays by Shakespeare or books by Charles Dickens after you listen to the cassettes or after you go to the classes for three months. These advertisements are awful. I am an English teacher and I know that it is difficult to learn a new language. But a lot of people think these advertisements are true. They want to go to these short courses or buy those expensive cassettes. I don't understand them. How can someone learn a new language in three months? Are 'super teachers' teaching their classes? I really don't know.

I don't think people can learn English from cassettes. When we speak a language we don't repeat sentences only. We use the language every day and everywhere. For example, we talk to our friends, our neighbours or to a waiter in a restaurant. You can't speak to a cassette. You can only repeat the questions and answers. We must talk to people and listen to them when we learn a new language. And this takes a long time. Not only three months!


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A*

1. Many businessmen can speak English.

   - True

   - False

2. The language cassettes are not cheap.

   - True

   - False

3. The writer likes the advertisements about language courses.

   - True

   - False

4. The writer is a 'super teacher'.

   - True

   - False

5. The writer thinks cassettes can teach you English very well.

   - True

   - False


*B*

6. Where can you hear a lot of English? (Write two things.)

7. Who do we talk to every day? (Write two things.)


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Author's Purpose]* Why does the writer ask, "Are 'super teachers' teaching their classes?" in paragraph 2?

   - A) To praise the teachers who work for language cassette companies

   - B) To show doubt that anyone can really teach a language in three months

   - C) To explain that English teachers should earn more money

   - D) To recommend special teachers to students who want to read Shakespeare

2. *[Main Idea]* What is the main idea of the final paragraph?

   - A) Watching English films is the only way to improve listening skills.

   - B) Shakespeare and Charles Dickens are too difficult even for English teachers.

   - C) Real communication with people over time is needed to learn a language.

   - D) Language schools in England are much better than courses in other countries.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* What does the word 'advertisements' mean in this passage?

   - A) Public notices trying to sell a product or service

   - B) Short books written for English learners

   - C) Daily news reports from foreign countries

   - D) School exams given after three months of study


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — The Truth About Learning English** (65-85 words): Summarize why English is important today and why the writer disagrees with language course advertisements.

- **Task 2 — How Should We Learn a New Language?** (80-110 words): Do you agree with the teacher that you cannot learn a language quickly from recordings or apps alone? Share your opinion and explain what helps you learn best.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **advertisement** (*reklam, ilan*): I saw an advertisement for a new English course on the bus.

- **business world** (*iş dünyası*): English is widely used across the international business world.

- **repeat** (*tekrar etmek*): Could you repeat that question more slowly, please?

- **second language** (*ikinci dil*): She speaks French as a second language at work.

- **report** (*haber, rapor*): Reporters send their news reports from around the world.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] İngilizcenin neden önemli olduğunu ve yazarın dil öğrenme hakkındaki görüşünü kendi cümlelerimle özetledim.

- [ ] Dil öğrenirken konuşma ve dinleme pratiğinin önemini örneklerle açıkladım.

- [ ] Metinden 'advertisement', 'business world' veya 'repeat' gibi en az 3 kelimeyi doğru şekilde kullandım.

- [ ] Cümlelerimin dil bilgisi, bağlaçlar ve noktalama açısından tutarlı olduğunu kontrol ettim.

## Passage 53: The Worlds of Walt Disney

- **ID:** `raw1_053` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Entertainment & Theme Parks | **Sayfa:** -
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_053_the_worlds_of_walt_disney.mp3` (*US · en-US-JennyNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. Walt Disney began making cartoon films with his brother Roy in ____. *(İpucu: year)*

2. Disney World was completed in 1971 and cost ____ dollars. *(İpucu: number)*

3. Unlike Disneyland, Disney World also has ____ and shopping centres so visitors can eat, sleep, and enjoy activities in one place. *(İpucu: plural noun)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. When did Disneyland open in California?

   - A) In 1923

   - B) In 1928

   - C) In 1955

   - D) In 1960

2. What did Walt Disney create in 1928?

   - A) Alice in Wonderland

   - B) Mickey Mouse

   - C) Adventureland

   - D) Lake Buena Vista

3. Why have people not moved into the second small city near Disney World yet?

   - A) It cost too much money

   - B) It is too far from Florida

   - C) It only has hotels and shops

   - D) It is still under construction


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Walt Disney lived to see the completion of Disney World in 1971.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Walt Disney's interest in technology helped make his work technically excellent.


### 2. Reading

Disneyland and Disney World are the two most famous entertainment parks in the world. Disneyland is in California and Disney World is in Florida, U.S.A. These were built by Walt Disney.

Walt Disney started to make cartoon films in 1923 with his brother Roy. First he made 'Alice in Wonderland' and then, in 1928, Mickey Mouse was created. He was very interested in technology so all his work was technically excellent and very enjoyable. He believed in providing good, clean entertainment and fun for all the family. He wanted a world which would give him a lot of fun when he was a boy and so he built it for others to enjoy in Disneyland, which was opened in Anaheim, California in 1955. It has four parts: Adventureland, Frontierland, Fantasyland and Tomorrowland.

In 1960 Walt Disney bought a big piece of land near Florida and started to build his second great entertainment park. This was called "Disney World". It was finished in 1971 and cost 400 million dollars. This place is a little different from Disneyland. It has got hotels and shopping centres as well as entertainment places. So people can eat, sleep and enjoy doing things in the same place.

A few years ago two other small cities were planned near Disney World. One of them is called Lake Buena Vista. This has been completed and people have started to move in. They are still building the second city so people will not move in until 1995.

Walt Disney died of cancer in 1966, but people still remember him when they visit his parks today.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the passage mainly about?

   - A) How to build modern theme parks cheaply

   - B) Walt Disney's career and his two famous entertainment parks

   - C) The history of cartoon films in California

   - D) Why tourists prefer Florida to California

2. *[Detail]* How does Disney World differ from Disneyland according to the passage?

   - A) Disneyland is much newer and more expensive than Disney World.

   - B) Disneyland has more hotels and shopping centres than Disney World.

   - C) Disney World offers accommodation and shopping in addition to entertainment.

   - D) Disney World was built before Walt Disney started making cartoons.

3. *[Inference]* What can be understood about the two small cities planned near Disney World?

   - A) One of the cities is finished, while the other is still being built.

   - B) Both cities were completely finished before 1971.

   - C) Neither city will allow people to live in it before 1995.

   - D) Both cities were designed by Walt Disney's brother Roy.


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Summary: Walt Disney and His Parks** (60-80 words): Write a short summary of Walt Disney's achievements and his two famous entertainment parks based on the passage.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: Family Holidays at Theme Parks** (80-110 words): Do you think entertainment parks are good places for family holidays? Give at least two reasons to support your opinion.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **entertainment** (*eğlence*): This city offers great entertainment for both young and old visitors.

- **cartoon film** (*çizgi film*): Children love watching colourful cartoon films on Saturday mornings.

- **technology** (*teknoloji*): Modern technology makes it easier to create amazing special effects.

- **complete** (*tamamlamak*): They will complete the new sports centre before next summer.

- **shopping centre** (*alışveriş merkezi*): The hotel is next to a large shopping centre in the city.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Parçada geçen tarih ve isim bilgilerini (1923, 1955, 1966, 1971) doğru şekilde kullandım mı?

- [ ] Geçmiş zaman (Past Simple) fiil çekimlerini doğru yazdım mı?

- [ ] Fikirlerimi basit bağlaçlarla (and, so, because, but) birbirine bağladım mı?

- [ ] Yazım, noktalama ve hedef kelime sayısı sınırına dikkat ettim mi?

## Passage 54: Letter from Family Friends Agency

- **ID:** `raw1_054` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Student Accommodation | **Sayfa:** 62, 63
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_054_letter_from_family_friends_agenc.mp3` (*AU · en-AU-NatashaNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. The Cranleys' house is near an ____, which makes it easy to reach any part of London. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*

2. Students who want to stay with Mrs. Eisley have to book at least ____ in advance. *(İpucu: time period)*

3. At Professor and Mrs. James Earl-Jones's house, there are two student bedrooms with ____ in each room. *(İpucu: number and noun)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Why do some students dislike staying with the Cranleys?

   - A) It is too far from the underground station.

   - B) The food there is not very good.

   - C) It can be too noisy because of the children and dogs.

   - D) Students have to share a bathroom with Mrs. Eisley.

2. What is special about Mrs. Eisley's accommodation?

   - A) Every student gets a private room with a washbasin and shower.

   - B) It is in the very centre of London near an underground station.

   - C) Students can live with a famous professor and an opera singer.

   - D) It is easy to find an empty room there at any time of the year.

3. What is the main disadvantage of staying with Professor and Mrs. James Earl-Jones?

   - A) Mrs. Jones practises singing opera every morning.

   - B) The garden is too small for students to enjoy.

   - C) Students are not allowed to eat meals with the family.

   - D) The house is quite far from central London.


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Each of the three families can take the same number of students.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Staying with Mrs. Eisley is more expensive than staying with the Cranleys.


### 2. Reading

John Bear is starting his university education in London, so he needs accommodation. He has written a letter to an agency and the following is the answer to his letter.

Family Friends Agency
London W1

Dear Mr. Bear,

Thank you for your letter of 17 October. You asked about good families for students to stay with in London. Here are three suggestions:

1) Mr. and Mrs. Cranley, Garden Street, London NW1
A large family with a large house. It can take up to four students. Everybody is part of the family. Good food. Good location (near an underground station), very central and easy to get to anywhere in London.

2) Mrs. Eisley, 3 Newton Avenue, Uxbridge
Mrs. Eisley is an old lady. There are five bedrooms for students. Everyone has his/her own room and each bedroom has a washbasin and shower. Another house on the same street has two more single bedrooms, but students eat with Mrs. Eisley.

3) Professor and Mrs. James Earl-Jones, Wimbledon
A famous family (Mr. Jones is a well-known professor, and his wife sings opera). The house is lovely, with a very large garden. There are two bedrooms for students, with two beds in each. Everyone who has stayed there says that the food is excellent.

Each of these places is very clean and friendly. Some students don't like Wimbledon because it's quite far from central London. Others love it. Some like staying with the Cranleys because they have five children and three dogs, but others don't like it because it's too noisy. Everyone likes Mrs. Eisley, so it's very difficult to get in. You must book at least six months in advance. I hope these suggestions are helpful.

Yours sincerely,
Family Friends Agency


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*Comprehension*

1. When did John write to the agency?

2. Where does he want to stay in London?

3. John loves children. Where can he stay?

4. How many students can eat with Mrs. Eisley?

5. John wants a shower for himself only. Where should he stay?

6. What does Mrs. Jones do?

7. Why don't some people like the Cranleys' house?

8. Why isn't it easy to find a place at Mrs. Eisley's house?

9. Why don't some students want to stay with Mr. and Mrs. Jones?

10. John can stay at the Cranleys'. Then how is he going to get to the university?

11. How many students can stay with Mr. and Mrs. Jones?


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Author's Purpose]* Why did Family Friends Agency write this letter to John Bear?

   - A) To invite John Bear to a university event in Wimbledon

   - B) To suggest three host families for John Bear in London

   - C) To complain about noisy students living in London NW1

   - D) To ask John Bear to pay a deposit for his accommodation

2. *[Inference]* What can be inferred about Mrs. Eisley's accommodation?

   - A) She only accepts students who study music.

   - B) Her house is currently being repaired.

   - C) Her rooms are very popular among students.

   - D) She lives too far away from any street or bus stop.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In the final paragraph, what does the phrase 'book at least six months in advance' mean?

   - A) reserve a place beforehand

   - B) buy textbooks for university

   - C) pay rent every six months

   - D) write a thank-you letter later


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Summary: Comparing Three Homestay Options** (60-80 words): Write a short summary comparing the main advantage and disadvantage of each of the three host families in the letter.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: Host Family vs. Dormitory** (80-110 words): Would you prefer to stay with a host family or live in a student dormitory when studying in another city? Explain your reasons.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **accommodation** (*konaklama, kalacak yer*): The university helps new students find comfortable accommodation near the campus.

- **suggestion** (*öneri*): Thank you for your helpful suggestions about places to visit in London.

- **location** (*konum, yer*): We live in a central location close to shops and buses.

- **noisy** (*gürültülü*): The street is too noisy at night because of the heavy traffic.

- **in advance** (*önceden, peşinen*): If you want cheap train tickets, you should buy them two weeks in advance.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Üç ev seçeneğinin de temel özelliklerini (oda, konum, avantaj/dezavantaj) doğru karşılaştırdım mı?

- [ ] Karşılaştırma yaparken because, however, on the other hand gibi ifadeler kullandım mı?

## Passage 55: Letter from Kathy

- **ID:** `raw1_055` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Moving to a New House | **Sayfa:** 64, 65
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_055_letter_from_kathy.mp3` (*UK · en-GB-SoniaNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. Kathy's family needs a new home because their current flat is too small now that the ____ are older. *(İpucu: plural noun)*

2. The country house is over ____ old, so they have to modernise it completely. *(İpucu: time period)*

3. Kathy and her family are planning to move into the country house ____. *(İpucu: time expression)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Why did Kathy reject the modern three-bedroomed flat in town?

   - A) It only had two small bedrooms.

   - B) It was too far from Peter's office.

   - C) It was on the eighth floor and on a busy main road.

   - D) None of their children's friends lived nearby.

2. Why was Peter unsure about the country house at first?

   - A) It was too expensive to buy.

   - B) It was a bit far from town.

   - C) It did not have a garden.

   - D) There were no schools near it.

3. How will Kathy's family get help with moving next month?

   - A) By hiring a big moving company

   - B) By asking their neighbours in the new village

   - C) With help from Bob and Carol

   - D) With help from several friends


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Peter and the children liked the modern flat in town.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Kathy learned about the country house from her husband's coworker.


### 2. Reading

54 New Street
Peterborough
Oct. 20th, 1996

Dear Bob and Carol,

I wanted to write to you some time ago, but we've been very busy this month with plans for a new home. Our flat's simply too small for us now that the children are older.

First we looked for a larger flat in town and found a modern 3-bedroomed flat quite near here. It wasn't far from Peter's office, so he liked it very much. The children were also happy because some of their friends lived next door. But when I saw the flat, I knew I didn't want it. It was on the eighth floor of a large block of flats and, you know, I'm afraid of high places. It was also on a busy main road.

Then I saw an advertisement in the evening paper for a really cheap house in the country. But it is a bit far from town, so Peter didn't like the idea at first. But we visited the place last weekend, and the children and I liked it immediately. It's over a hundred years old, so we must modernise it completely, but I don't mind that because it has got four large bedrooms. It's really big. The children loved it because there's a big garden to play in. There's a very good private school nearby, and I think we'll send the children there. The children and I were so enthusiastic that Peter finally agreed.

We're planning to move there next month. Luckily, several friends have offered to help us. I'm sure we'll be very happy there.

Hoping to see you in our new home soon.

Kathy


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A*

1. No one in the family liked the new flat on the 8th floor.

   - True

   - False

2. A friend offered to rent his country house to Peter and Kathy.

   - True

   - False

3. The children's education won't be a problem if Peter and Kathy buy the country house.

   - True

   - False


*B*

4. Why didn't Kathy like the new flat? (Give two reasons.)

5. Write two advantages (good points) of the country house.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the main point of Kathy's letter?

   - A) Kathy's family decided to buy an old country house instead of a city flat.

   - B) Peter forced his family to move closer to his office in town.

   - C) Kathy and her children disagreed about moving to the country.

   - D) Bob and Carol are helping Kathy buy a modern flat in Peterborough.

2. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In the third paragraph, what does Kathy mean by 'we must modernise it completely'?

   - A) sell it to someone else for more money

   - B) build a brand-new road next to it

   - C) update the old house so it is modern

   - D) paint only the outside garden fence

3. *[Inference]* What can be inferred about Peter's final decision on the country house?

   - A) He wanted to live more than a hundred years in the past.

   - B) He agreed mainly because his family loved the house so much.

   - C) He refused to move unless his office moved to the country too.

   - D) He was afraid of living on a quiet country road.


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Summary: Choosing a New Home** (60-80 words): Summarize why Kathy's family rejected the flat in town and chose the house in the country instead.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: Town Flat vs. Country House** (80-110 words): Would you prefer to live in a flat in town or a house in the country? Explain your choice with at least two reasons.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **advertisement** (*ilan, reklam*): She saw an advertisement for a cheap house in the evening newspaper.

- **modernise** (*modernize etmek, yenilemek*): They want to modernise their old kitchen before moving in.

- **enthusiastic** (*hevesli, istekli*): The students were very enthusiastic about the school trip to the mountains.

- **nearby** (*yakınlarda*): Is there a good supermarket nearby where we can buy fresh fruit?

- **offer to help** (*yardım teklif etmek*): Our neighbour offered to help us carry the heavy boxes upstairs.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Mektuptaki iki evin olumlu ve olumsuz yönlerini doğru özetledim mi?

- [ ] Şehir ve kırsal yaşam arasındaki farkları açıkça ifade ettim mi?

- [ ] Simple Past ve Future (be going to / will) yapılarını doğru kullandım mı?

- [ ] Hedef kelime sayısına ve noktalama kurallarına dikkat ettim mi?

## Passage 56: Single Fathers' Club

- **ID:** `raw1_056` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Support Groups & Parenting | **Sayfa:** 66, 67
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_056_single_fathers_club.mp3` (*US · en-US-GuyNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. The Single Fathers' Club began when a ____ named John McCarthy talked about his own experience on the air. *(İpucu: job)*

2. After six months, there were ____ in the Single Fathers' Club. *(İpucu: number and noun)*

3. The members of the club get together every ____ to discuss their problems. *(İpucu: day and time)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. What gave John McCarthy the idea to start a group for single fathers?

   - A) He read a newspaper article about single fathers.

   - B) He read a radio announcement for a single mothers' group.

   - C) He met Henry Mason at a school meeting.

   - D) His doctors told him to start a weekly club.

2. Who are the members of the Single Fathers' Club?

   - A) Only men who work in radio and television

   - B) Only rich businessmen and doctors

   - C) Men from many different jobs, as well as students and unemployed men

   - D) Only fathers who have two sons

3. What do the fathers say is the hardest part for men?

   - A) Finding enough money to buy healthy food

   - B) Remembering to come to meetings on Tuesday nights

   - C) Taking care of children with a high temperature

   - D) Learning to talk openly about personal topics with other men


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — John McCarthy started the Single Fathers' Club completely alone.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — The Single Fathers' Club later opened branches in Europe.


### 2. Reading

Self-help groups, i.e. groups of people with common problems, are very popular in many countries. These people have meetings and talk about their problems. They try to find answers for their problems together. For example, there are hundreds of groups of men and women who want to lose weight or give up drinking, who are single mothers or unmarried mothers. Now groups for single fathers are also starting to appear. This is the story of a group for single fathers.

It started when a radio announcer, John McCarthy, was reading an announcement for a single mothers' group. After he read the announcement, he said, "I'm a single father. I've got two sons. My wife died two years ago. I look after my children on my own and I have a lot of problems. I'm sure there are a lot of other men who have lost their wives. So we need a group, too". Two single fathers, Henry Mason and Paul Singer, heard his message on the radio and called him and they decided to meet. These three men started the first single fathers' group in the US on May 5th, 1988. After 6 months, there were 30 fathers in the group called "Single Fathers' Club". There are doctors, students, mechanics, a bus-driver, a television producer, a few businessmen and several men who are unemployed. They meet every Tuesday night.

They talk about problems, such as how to look after a child with a temperature, where to find milk at 6 a.m., how to help children who don't sleep well at night, or what foods are best for their children. The fathers feel these meetings help them. They say the difficult part for men is just learning to talk to other men about anything really personal.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A*

1. Members of self-help groups try to help the single or unmarried mothers in their area.

   - True

   - False

2. John McCarthy worked for a single mothers' group.

   - True

   - False

3. All the members of 'Single Fathers' Club' work and at the same time look after their children.

   - True

   - False

4. Men cannot easily talk about personal things.

   - True

   - False


*B*

5. What did McCarthy, Mason and Singer do on May 5th, 1988?

6. At their group meetings, fathers talk about their problems with their children. Write two of these problems.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the passage mainly about?

   - A) Why radio announcers often have family problems

   - B) How doctors help children who cannot sleep at night

   - C) The creation and purpose of a self-help group for single fathers

   - D) Why single mothers need more support than single fathers

2. *[Detail]* What can be learned about the Tuesday night meetings of the Single Fathers' Club?

   - A) They mostly discuss how to find better jobs.

   - B) They focus on practical everyday problems of raising children.

   - C) They spend most of their time complaining about their jobs.

   - D) They only invite doctors to speak about medical problems.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In the second paragraph, what does John McCarthy mean when he says 'I look after my children on my own'?

   - A) alone, without anyone else's help

   - B) with help from his radio listeners

   - C) only on Tuesday nights

   - D) with the help of a babysitter


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Summary: The Single Fathers' Club** (60-80 words): Summarize how the Single Fathers' Club was started and what the members do at their meetings.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: The Value of Self-Help Groups** (80-110 words): Do you think self-help groups are helpful for people with personal or family problems? Explain why or why not.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **common problems** (*ortak sorunlar*): People with common problems often find comfort in talking to one another.

- **give up** (*bırakmak, vazgeçmek*): He decided to give up drinking to improve his health.

- **look after** (*bakmak, ilgilenmek*): Working parents sometimes find it hard to look after young children alone.

- **unemployed** (*işsiz*): Several men in the club are currently unemployed and looking for work.

- **temperature** (*ateş (vücut ısısı)*): The baby woke up crying because he had a high temperature.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Kulübün nasıl kurulduğunu ve kimlerden oluştuğunu açıkça özetledim mi?

- [ ] Destek gruplarının faydalarına dair örnekler veya mantıklı gerekçeler sundum mu?

- [ ] Geniş zaman ve geçmiş zamanı bağlama göre doğru kullandım mı?

- [ ] Yazım hatalarını kontrol edip hedef kelime aralığında kaldım mı?

## Passage 57: Plane Crash in Peru

- **ID:** `raw1_057` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Survival & Aviation | **Sayfa:** 67, 68
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_057_plane_crash_in_peru.mp3` (*US · en-US-AriaNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. Juliana fell ____ metres while strapped in her seat. *(İpucu: number)*

2. When she woke up, she only found a small plastic bag of ____. *(İpucu: noun)*

3. Four ____ arrived at the village house and took her to a doctor. *(İpucu: noun)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Why did the plane crash?

   - A) It ran out of fuel over the ocean.

   - B) It hit a mountain during a terrible storm.

   - C) The pilot had a medical emergency.

   - D) It was damaged before taking off from Lima.

2. What happened when helicopters flew over the jungle?

   - A) She waved at them and they landed nearby.

   - B) She climbed a tree to signal them.

   - C) She heard them, but they could not see each other because of the trees.

   - D) They rescued three other passengers instead of her.

3. How long did Juliana walk and swim down the river?

   - A) Two days

   - B) Four days

   - C) Five days

   - D) Ten days


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Juliana's father worked at a bank in Pucallpa.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — The other three survivors stayed near the crashed plane until help arrived.


### 2. Reading

On Christmas Eve, 1971, Juliana Koepke, a seventeen-year-old German girl, and her mother left Lima by plane. They were going to Pucallpa, another town in Peru. They wanted to spend Christmas with Juliana's father, who was the manager of a bank in Pucallpa. Forty-five minutes later there was a terrible storm and the plane hit a mountain and crashed. Juliana fell 3,000 metres, strapped in her seat. She did not die when the seat hit the ground, but she was unconscious all night.

The next morning Juliana looked for pieces of the plane, and called for her mother. Nobody answered, and she only found a small plastic bag of sweets. Juliana's left arm was broken, one knee was badly hurt and she had deep cuts on her legs and arms. She had no shoes and she was wearing only a dress, which was badly torn. But she decided to try to get out of the jungle. She knew that she would die if she stayed there. She started to walk. She did not have anything to eat for two days, so she felt very weak. She heard helicopters, but could not see them above the trees, and of course they could not see her.

After four days she came to a river. She walked and swam down the river for another five days. At last she came to a small village house. Nobody was there, but that afternoon, four farmers arrived. They took her to a doctor in the next village.

Juliana learned afterwards that there were three other people who were not killed in the accident. But she was the only one who got out of the jungle. It took her ten days.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A*

1. Both Lima and Pucallpa are in Peru.

   - True

   - False

2. Juliana's father was on holiday in Pucallpa.

   - True

   - False

3. The plane hit a mountain a few hours after it left Lima.

   - True

   - False

4. After the accident, Juliana could not find her mother.

   - True

   - False

5. Juliana broke her leg in the accident.

   - True

   - False

6. She was wearing a dress but no shoes.

   - True

   - False

7. Juliana saw helicopters flying above the trees.

   - True

   - False

8. Some farmers found Juliana in the village house.

   - True

   - False

9. Juliana and three other people got out of the jungle.

   - True

   - False


*B*

10. Why were Juliana and her mother going to Pucallpa?

11. Why did Juliana feel very weak?

12. After Juliana came to the river, how long did it take her to arrive at the village house?


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the main topic of the passage?

   - A) The dangers of flying in Peru during December

   - B) How rescue helicopters operate in dense forests

   - C) A teenager's remarkable survival after a plane crash

   - D) The history of banking in Pucallpa, Peru

2. *[Inference]* What can be inferred about Juliana's decision to keep walking through the jungle?

   - A) She wanted to find her father's bank before Christmas.

   - B) She realized waiting at the crash site would lead to her death.

   - C) She was told by the other survivors to walk south.

   - D) She was looking for a larger town with a hospital.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* What does the word 'unconscious' most likely mean in the first paragraph?

   - A) Not awake or aware of anything

   - B) Unable to remember her name

   - C) Too frightened to move her legs

   - D) Completely unharmed by the fall


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Guided Summary: Juliana's Survival Story** (60-80 words): Write a short summary of how Juliana Koepke survived the plane crash and escaped from the jungle. Mention her injuries, how she found her way, and who helped her.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: Key to Survival** (80-110 words): In your opinion, what helped Juliana Koepke survive ten days in the jungle? Write a short paragraph explaining your ideas.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **strapped in** (*baglanmis, kemerle tutturulmus*): The passengers were strapped into their seats before takeoff.

- **unconscious** (*bilinci kapali, baygin*): He was unconscious for several minutes after hitting his head.

- **torn** (*yirtik, yirtilmis*): Her jacket was badly torn when she fell on the rocks.

- **jungle** (*tropik orman, balta girmemis orman*): It is easy to get lost in a thick jungle without a guide.

- **weak** (*gucsuz, halsiz*): I felt very weak after walking all day without lunch.

- **decide to** (*...meye karar vermek*): They decided to try to cross the river before dark.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Paragrafimdaki tum fiillerin gecmis zaman (Simple Past) yapisinda dogru kullanildigini kontrol ettim.

- [ ] Olaylari kronolojik siraya (once, sonra, sonunda) gore acikca bagladim.

- [ ] Metinden ogrendigim en az uc yeni kelimeyi veya kalibi yazimda kullandim.

- [ ] Noktalama isaretlerini, buyuk harfleri ve kelime sayisini gozden gecirdim.

## Passage 58: Inverness

- **ID:** `raw1_058` | **Seviye:** `B1` | **Konu:** Travel & Cities | **Sayfa:** 69, 70
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_058_inverness.mp3` (*UK · en-GB-RyanNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. The Caledonian Canal was opened in ____. *(İpucu: year)*

2. Visitors are encouraged to try local dishes like ____ and malt whisky. *(İpucu: noun)*

3. About ____ Scottish people speak the Gaelic language. *(İpucu: number)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. How can tourists travel to or around Inverness?

   - A) Only by boat along the Caledonian Canal

   - B) By train from Edinburgh only

   - C) By train, plane, and bus

   - D) By steamship from the Irish Sea

2. Why did people stop using the Caledonian Canal for commercial transport?

   - A) The water became too shallow for sailing boats.

   - B) Steamships replaced sailing boats and were too big for the canal.

   - C) The northern end near Inverness was closed for repairs.

   - D) Railways made boat travel unnecessary for tourists.

3. What is Inverness Castle used as today?

   - A) A courthouse

   - B) An art gallery

   - C) A language centre

   - D) A hotel for tourists


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — There are no old buildings remaining anywhere in Inverness.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — People who are not staying at a hotel in Inverness can still eat meals there.


### 2. Reading

Inverness is a small town situated in beautiful countryside in the north-east of Scotland. It is an important centre for visits to many parts of the Highlands, which is the famous mountainous area in Scotland. Inverness is a small town but it has got an excellent transport system. There are trains to all the main cities of Scotland (Inverness - Aberdeen 1.5 hours, Inverness - Dundee 3 hours, Inverness - Glasgow 4 hours, Inverness - Edinburgh 4.5 hours). Dalcross Airport (about 8 miles east of the town) offers flights to many parts of the country. The buses leaving from Faraline Park serve the town and the surrounding area. Many of the big banks have branches in Inverness so changing money is not a problem for tourists. As well as these essential services, the town has cultural and entertainment facilities. There are theatres and cinemas, and some hotels organise Highland entertainment during the summer months.

Inverness is an old town, but there are not many buildings left from the old times. However, there are still some buildings in the town which are very old, such as the houses on Douglas Row. And Church Street has some interesting old churches.

Inverness is situated at the northern end of the Caledonian Canal. The canal was opened in 1822. Sailing boats used the canal to travel between the Irish Sea and the North Sea. The canal provided a safe and fast journey for them. When steamships became popular instead of sailing boats, people stopped using the canal for transport because these steamships were too large and could not travel down the canal. Nowadays, there are sightseeing trips for tourists down the canal.

There are many good eating places in Inverness. There are plenty of restaurants and hotels which offer meals to non-residents. They have delicious local dishes at cheap prices; try some 'haggis' and a glass of malt whisky before you leave. There are also all kinds of seafood.

Places of Interest
Inverness Castle (1834) - It is now a court house.
Museum and Art Gallery - You can see the collection about the history of the Highlands in the Museum. In the Art Gallery artists exhibit their paintings, sculptures and photographs, which change frequently.
Abertaff House (1592) - The Highland Association uses this building now. This association tries to help people to learn Gaelic, the language which about 80,000 Scottish people speak. They are also interested in the country's national clothes, food and musical instruments.
St Andrew's Cathedral (1869) - It is richly decorated with fine windows and pillars.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A*

1. You can see old buildings on Douglas Row and Church Street.

   - True

   - False

2. The writer suggests that visitors to Inverness try some of the local food.

   - True

   - False

3. The Museum is used for art exhibitions.

   - True

   - False


*B*

4. Which essential services are there in Inverness according to the text?

   - a) Theatres and cinemas.

   - b) Inverness Castle and St. Andrew's Cathedral.

   - c) Hotels and restaurants.

   - d) Transport system and banks.

5. Which of the following is true about the Caledonian Canal?

   - a) No one uses the canal today.

   - b) The journey down the canal was quite dangerous for sailing boats.

   - c) Inverness is at one end of the canal.

   - d) Steamships which travelled between the Irish Sea and the North Sea used the canal.

6. Gaelic is

   - a) an interesting place in the old part of the town

   - b) the Scottish language

   - c) a national musical instrument of Scotland

   - d) a kind of local food


*C*

7. What is the Highlands?

8. You can travel to several Scottish cities from Inverness by train. Write the names of these cities.


*D*

9. Which of the following information can you find in the text? Items: a) what the weather's like, b) where Inverness is, c) hotel prices, d) the canal, e) what to eat and drink, f) population of Inverness, g) interesting places to see.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Author's Purpose]* What is the main purpose of this passage?

   - A) To compare Inverness with Edinburgh and Glasgow

   - B) To explain the economic history of Scottish canals

   - C) To promote learning the Gaelic language in Scotland

   - D) To inform visitors about what Inverness offers tourists

2. *[Detail]* What does the passage indicate about the Art Gallery in Inverness?

   - A) The museum collection is closed during the summer months.

   - B) Visitors can expect to see different artworks at different times of the year.

   - C) Only local Highland artists are allowed to display their work.

   - D) The Art Gallery is located inside Inverness Castle.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In the first paragraph, what does the phrase 'essential services' refer to?

   - A) Luxury hotels and fine dining restaurants

   - B) Traditional music and summer festivals

   - C) Basic, necessary practical services such as transport and banking

   - D) Guided sightseeing tours on historical waterways


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Summary: Visiting Inverness** (90-120 words): Summarize the main attractions and facilities that make Inverness appealing to tourists, based on the passage.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Essay: Small Towns vs. Big Cities** (150-180 words): Some travellers prefer visiting small historic towns like Inverness rather than big, crowded cities. Do you agree or disagree? Give reasons and examples to support your opinion.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **situated in** (*konumlanmis, yer alan*): The hotel is situated near the train station in the city centre.

- **essential services** (*temel hizmetler*): Clean water and reliable transport are essential services in any town.

- **sightseeing trip** (*gezi, cevre gezisi*): We took a sightseeing trip along the canal to see the old bridges.

- **local dishes** (*yerel yemekler*): The restaurant serves delicious local dishes at reasonable prices.

- **exhibit** (*sergilemek*): The art gallery exhibits modern paintings that change every month.

- **richly decorated** (*zengin bir sekilde suslenmis*): The palace is richly decorated with colourful glass and stone pillars.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Metnin ana bolumlerini (konum, ulasim, tarih, yemek ve gezilecek yerler) ozetledim.

- [ ] Kendi cumlelerimle ozgun ifadeler kurarak dogrudan kopyalamadan kacindim.

- [ ] Goruşlerimi destekleyen en az iki somut ornek verdim.

- [ ] Baglaclarin, zaman uyumunun ve kelime sayisinin sinirlar icinde kaldigini dogruladim.

## Passage 59: Lacrosse

- **ID:** `raw1_059` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Sports & History | **Sayfa:** 71, 72
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_059_lacrosse.mp3` (*US · en-US-JennyNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. The Native Americans originally used lacrosse to train for ____. *(İpucu: noun)*

2. A lacrosse field is ____ meters long. *(İpucu: number)*

3. Each player uses a special stick called a ____. *(İpucu: noun)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. When was lacrosse invented?

   - A) Before Columbus arrived in the New World

   - B) In the nineteenth century in Britain

   - C) Right after Canada became a country

   - D) When the first indoor stadiums were built

2. How much does a lacrosse ball weigh?

   - A) 70 grams

   - B) 21 grams

   - C) 140 grams

   - D) 210 grams

3. Why is lacrosse described as a very fast game?

   - A) The field is very short and narrow.

   - B) Players catch and pass the ball at high speed with their sticks.

   - C) There are no goalkeepers to stop the ball.

   - D) The players run without stopping for rest.


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Lacrosse is normally played inside a sports hall.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Lacrosse is played in Britain and Australia as well as in Canada.


### 2. Reading

Lacrosse is a popular sport in Canada. It is one of the oldest organized sports in America. The Indians in northern New York State and southern Ontario, Canada, invented it. They used it to train for war. They invented this game before Columbus arrived in the New World.

People play lacrosse outdoors. The lacrosse field is seventy meters long. At each end of the field there is a goal. The goal is a net. There are ten players on each team. Each player has a stick called a "crosse". The players hit a ball that is 21 centimetres around and weighs 140 grams. They try to hit the ball into the net as many times as possible. Lacrosse is a very fast game because the players can catch and pass the ball at a high speed with their sticks.

At one time lacrosse was the national summer sport in Canada. It is also popular in Britain and Australia.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A*

1. Which countries play lacrosse?

2. How many goals are there?

3. What does each player have?


*B*

4. ____ invented lacrosse.

   - a) Columbus

   - b) Indians

   - c) Canadians

   - d) A team

5. The ball weighs ____.

   - a) 21 centimetres

   - b) 70 grams

   - c) 140 grams

   - d) 70 metres

6. ____ people play in a lacrosse game.

   - a) Eight

   - b) Fifteen

   - c) Twenty

   - d) Ten

7. People play lacrosse ____.

   - a) in a building

   - b) on ice

   - c) on a field

   - d) indoors

8. The players hit ____.

   - a) each other

   - b) the net with a stick

   - c) the field

   - d) a ball with a stick

9. The players catch and pass the ball very ____.

   - a) quickly

   - b) loudly

   - c) slowly

   - d) quietly

10. Lacrosse was the national summer sport in ____.

   - a) Canada

   - b) England

   - c) Australia

   - d) New York State


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the main purpose of the passage?

   - A) To compare lacrosse with modern hockey and football

   - B) To explain why lacrosse is no longer played in Canada

   - C) To describe the history, rules, and popularity of lacrosse

   - D) To teach beginners how to make a lacrosse stick

2. *[Detail]* Which statement is supported by the information in the passage?

   - A) There are twenty players on the field during a standard game.

   - B) Every game ends when one team scores ten goals.

   - C) The ball is larger than a standard football.

   - D) Players are only allowed to pass the ball with their hands.

3. *[Inference]* What can be inferred about the history of lacrosse?

   - A) It was brought from Europe to North America by sailors.

   - B) It was originally invented as a winter game on ice.

   - C) It was created mainly for young children in schools.

   - D) It has existed in North America for many centuries.


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Guided Summary: What is Lacrosse?** (60-80 words): Write a short summary explaining the origins of lacrosse, how it is played, and where it is popular.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: Benefits of Team Sports** (80-110 words): Do you think fast team sports like lacrosse are good for young people? Write a short paragraph explaining your reasons.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **organized sport** (*duzenli, kurallari belirlenmis spor*): Football is an organized sport with strict international rules.

- **train for** (*egitim yapmak, hazirlanmak*): The soldiers had to train hard for months before the mission.

- **outdoors** (*acik havada, disarida*): We prefer to play basketball outdoors when the weather is warm.

- **net** (*ag, file*): The striker kicked the ball into the net to win the match.

- **at a high speed** (*yuksek hizla*): The car was travelling at a high speed on the highway.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Sporun tarihcesini ve temel kurallarini net bir sekilde acikladim.

- [ ] Sayisal bilgileri (oyuncu sayisi, saha uzunlugu, top agirligi) dogru aktardim.

- [ ] Genis zaman (Simple Present) ve gecmis zaman (Simple Past) kullanimini dogru ayirdim.

- [ ] Yazimi imla, noktalama ve hedef kelime sayisi acisindan kontrol ettim.

## Passage 60: The Barasana

- **ID:** `raw1_060` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Indigenous Culture & Lifestyle | **Sayfa:** 73, 74
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_060_the_barasana.mp3` (*AU · en-AU-NatashaNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. About ____ people live in the Vaupés Region. *(İpucu: number)*

2. The Barasana use ____ from banana trees to serve food. *(İpucu: noun)*

3. Barasana children first learn their ____ language. *(İpucu: possessive noun)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. How long does it take to walk from one Barasana house to another?

   - A) Ten minutes

   - B) Half an hour

   - C) One hour

   - D) Two hours

2. Why are the Barasana such good language-learners?

   - A) They have to take language classes at school.

   - B) Men must marry women from houses with different languages.

   - C) They trade goods with neighbours every day.

   - D) Every child has to learn four different languages.

3. What do young Barasana boys usually do?

   - A) Swimming, fishing, and practising hunting

   - B) Working in the gardens and cooking food

   - C) Helping their mothers with the housework

   - D) Going to a school in a nearby village


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Several Barasana families live together in a single large house.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Barasana men spend more than four hours every day hunting animals.


### 2. Reading

Between Colombia and Brazil there is an area called 'The Vaupés Region'. About 15,000 people live in this area. They are the Amazonian Indians. These Amazonian Indians live in small groups. These groups have got different names like: Tukano, Desana, Cubeo and Barasana.

The Barasana have a very different life-style. They do not live in villages. Many families live together in one house. It's a very big house and they share everything in this house. These houses are very far away from each other. A person has to walk for one hour to get from one house to another. There are gardens for special plants behind the houses. The Barasana pick bananas from the banana trees and use the leaves of these trees to serve food.

The Barasana men and the Barasana women do different things. The women spend most of their time doing housework. They look after children, work in the gardens and prepare the food. The men go fishing and hunt animals for their meat.

The Barasana are marvellous language-learners. This is because a Barasana man has to marry a woman from a different house. The people in different houses speak different languages, so they must learn the other language to understand each other. The wife has to learn her husband's language and the husband has to learn his wife's language.

The children first learn their father's language and use it every day, but they also understand their mother's language. The children do not go to school. They play with other children, watch their parents and in this way they learn about life. Young girls have to help their mothers, but the boys don't. They usually swim in the rivers, go fishing and practise hunting animals.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A. Mark the statements as True (T) or False (F).*

1. 1. Tukano, Desana and Cubeo are names of cities in Colombia.

   - True

   - False

2. 2. 15,000 Barasana live in the Vaupés Region.

   - True

   - False

3. 3. A Barasana family shares a house with other families.

   - True

   - False

4. 4. The Barasana men and the Barasana women do the housework together.

   - True

   - False

5. 5. A Barasana man can't marry a woman from a different house.

   - True

   - False

6. 6. A Barasana child can understand his/her mother's and father's languages.

   - True

   - False

7. 7. Young Barasana boys and girls have to help their parents.

   - True

   - False


*B. Answer the questions.*

8. 1. Where do the Amazonian Indians live?

9. 2. Who doesn't live in villages?

10. 3. Who looks after the children?

11. 4. What do the Barasana children do to learn about life? (Write two things.)


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the main topic of the passage?

   - A) The history of trade between Colombia and Brazil

   - B) The daily life, customs, and family structure of the Barasana people

   - C) How Amazonian Indians build boats for river travel

   - D) The types of wild animals found in the Vaupés Region

2. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In paragraph 4, the word "marvellous" is closest in meaning to ____.

   - A) slow

   - B) unwilling

   - C) wonderful

   - D) careless

3. *[Inference]* What can be inferred about education among the Barasana?

   - A) Barasana children learn practical skills through daily life rather than formal schooling.

   - B) Barasana girls spend more time swimming in rivers than Barasana boys.

   - C) Barasana women are not allowed to speak their own languages after marriage.

   - D) Most Barasana houses are located close together along the main river.


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Guided Summary: Life Among the Barasana** (60-80 words): Write a short summary of how the Barasana people live, work, and learn languages based on the passage.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: Sharing a Home with Many Families** (80-110 words): Would you like to live in a big shared house with many families like the Barasana? Write a paragraph explaining the advantages and disadvantages of this lifestyle.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **share** (*paylaşmak*): Many families live together and share everything in one big house.

- **look after** (*bakmak, ilgilenmek*): Barasana women spend most of their time looking after the children.

- **prepare** (*hazırlamak*): The women prepare the food and serve it on banana leaves.

- **marry** (*evlenmek*): A Barasana man has to marry someone from a different house.

- **practise** (*pratik yapmak, alıştırma yapmak*): Young boys swim in the rivers and practise hunting animals.

- **far away** (*uzak, birbirinden uzakta*): These huge houses are very far away from each other.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Paragraflarım Barasana halkının yaşam tarzı, iş bölümü ve dil öğrenme alışkanlıklarını doğru özetliyor mu?

- [ ] Geniş zaman (Simple Present Tense) yapılarını ve özne-fiil uyumunu doğru kullandım mı?

- [ ] Parçadan en az 3 yeni kelimeyi (örneğin share, look after, practise) doğru bağlamda kullandım mı?

- [ ] Cümle başı büyük harfleri, noktalama işaretlerini ve hedef kelime sayısını kontrol ettim mi?

## Passage 61: The Motor Car Industry

- **ID:** `raw1_061` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Automotive History | **Sayfa:** 74
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_061_the_motor_car_industry.mp3` (*UK · en-GB-SoniaNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. At the end of the 19th century, cars were made in small ____ rather than factories. *(İpucu: plural noun)*

2. Henry Ford opened his factory in ____. *(İpucu: year)*

3. Ford's factory produced only one kind of car, called the ____. *(İpucu: car name)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Why were motor cars not popular for many years in the 19th century?

   - A) People preferred riding horses in cities.

   - B) Cars were too expensive for most people.

   - C) Factories produced too many cars at once.

   - D) The roads in Europe and America were too narrow.

2. How did Henry Ford make cars cheaper and faster to produce?

   - A) He hired more mechanics to work in small workshops.

   - B) He imported cheaper parts from Great Britain and Germany.

   - C) He opened a factory that used machines to make car parts.

   - D) He designed several different luxury models at once.

3. How many kinds of motor cars were made in Henry Ford's factory?

   - A) Only one kind of car

   - B) Two different kinds of cars

   - C) Four kinds of European cars

   - D) Many different kinds of trucks and cars


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — At the end of the 19th century, motor cars were made in both Europe and the United States.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Mechanics in 19th-century workshops used hundreds of fast machines to build cars.


### 2. Reading

At the end of the 19th century motor cars were made in Great Britain, France, Germany and the United States. They were not popular for many years because they were too expensive. They were very expensive because they were made in small workshops and not in factories. Mechanics had to make many small parts with the help of only a few machines.

At the beginning of the 20th century - in 1909 - the American engineer Henry Ford found a way to make cheaper cars very fast. He opened a factory. In his factory there were machines making the parts of the cars. This factory made one kind of motor car only - the famous Ford "Model T".


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A. Mark the best choice.*

1. 1. At the end of the 19th century cars were very expensive because

   - a) they were made in factories

   - b) mechanics made them

   - c) they were not small

   - d) they were not popular

2. 2. Henry Ford's factory

   - a) made many kinds of cheap cars

   - b) made machines to make parts of the cars

   - c) opened in the nineteen twenties

   - d) made the first Ford "Model T"


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the main focus of the passage?

   - A) The history of car racing in the United States

   - B) Why European cars were better than American cars

   - C) The life story of mechanics in Great Britain

   - D) How car production changed from expensive workshops to Ford's factory

2. *[Inference]* What can be concluded about Henry Ford's production method?

   - A) Using machines to make parts of a single car model lowered costs and increased speed.

   - B) Building cars in small workshops produced better quality vehicles.

   - C) Making many different car models at the same time was the fastest method.

   - D) Customers in 1909 wanted more expensive cars made by hand.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In paragraph 1, the word "popular" is closest in meaning to ____.

   - A) dangerous to drive

   - B) easy to repair

   - C) widely liked and bought

   - D) made of metal


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Summary: From Workshops to Ford's Factory** (60-80 words): Summarize how early motor cars were made and how Henry Ford changed the industry in 1909.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: How Affordable Cars Changed Life** (80-110 words): How did cheaper cars like the Ford Model T change people's daily lives? Write a short paragraph explaining your opinion.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **workshop** (*atölye*): Early cars were built in small workshops instead of large factories.

- **factory** (*fabrika*): Henry Ford opened a factory to produce cars faster.

- **mechanic** (*tamirci, mekaniker*): Skilled mechanics had to build many small parts by hand.

- **engineer** (*mühendis*): Ford was an American engineer who changed car manufacturing.

- **part** (*parça*): Machines made the parts of the cars very quickly.

- **expensive** (*pahalı*): Before 1909, motor cars were too expensive for most people.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] 19. yüzyıl sonu ile 1909 yılındaki araba üretimi arasındaki farkı açıkça anlattım mı?

- [ ] Geçmiş zaman (Simple Past Tense) fiillerini doğru biçimde kullandım mı?

- [ ] Parçadan workshop, factory, engineer, part gibi kelimeleri doğru kullandım mı?

- [ ] Yazım, noktalama ve kelime sayısı sınırına dikkat ettim mi?

## Passage 62: The Ostrich

- **ID:** `raw1_062` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Wildlife & Birds | **Sayfa:** 75
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_062_the_ostrich.mp3` (*US · en-US-GuyNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. Even though an ostrich has wings, they are too ____ for it to fly. *(İpucu: adjective)*

2. An ostrich can run at a speed of about ____ miles an hour. *(İpucu: number)*

3. The female ostrich watches the eggs during the ____. *(İpucu: noun)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Where is the natural home of the ostrich?

   - A) Europe and South America

   - B) Australia and North America

   - C) Africa and Arabia

   - D) India and China

2. Which description best matches the body of an ostrich?

   - A) A large head and short neck

   - B) A small, flat head and strong, thick legs

   - C) Long wings and very thin legs

   - D) A round head and short, weak legs

3. Where do ostriches leave their eggs?

   - A) In nests high in trees

   - B) Inside caves near mountains

   - C) Under bushes near rivers

   - D) In holes in the sand


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — No other bird lays bigger eggs than the ostrich.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Ostriches usually live for about 30 years in the wild.


### 2. Reading

The ostrich is the largest bird in the world. Africa and Arabia are the home of the ostrich, but we can see one in almost every zoo around the world. It has got wings but they are very small, so it cannot fly. Some male ostriches can be 2.5m in height and weigh from 90 to 135 kg. An ostrich has a small, flat head and a very long neck. Its legs are strong and thick. It is an excellent runner. It can run very fast - about 40 miles an hour. It is faster than a horse, but it is not the fastest animal. There are faster animals than the ostrich - for example, the antelope. The food of the ostrich is plants and some insects.

Ostriches leave their eggs into holes in the sand. Sometimes there are 20 eggs in one hole. They are white and the largest eggs in the world. The female ostrich looks after the eggs in the daytime and the male ostrich at night.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*Mark the statements as True (T) or False (F).*

1. 1. There are larger birds than the ostrich in the world.

   - True

   - False

2. 2. Ostriches live in zoos only.

   - True

   - False

3. 3. An ostrich can't fly.

   - True

   - False

4. 4. An antelope is faster than a horse.

   - True

   - False

5. 5. An ostrich can weigh 125 kg.

   - True

   - False

6. 6. Ostriches like eating insects and large eggs of other birds.

   - True

   - False

7. 7. The male and the female ostriches look after the eggs together at night.

   - True

   - False


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Detail]* According to the passage, which of these animals is the fastest?

   - A) The ostrich

   - B) The antelope

   - C) The horse

   - D) They all run at the same speed

2. *[Inference]* What can be inferred about ostrich parents from the second paragraph?

   - A) Only female ostriches care about their eggs.

   - B) Ostriches always lay exactly 20 eggs in every hole.

   - C) Both male and female ostriches share the job of protecting their eggs.

   - D) Ostriches hide their eggs in trees so insects cannot reach them.

3. *[Author's Purpose]* What is the author's main purpose in this passage?

   - A) To give basic facts about the ostrich's body, habits, and eggs

   - B) To explain why ostriches are dangerous to zoo visitors

   - C) To compare how fast different African birds can fly

   - D) To persuade people to protect ostriches in Arabia


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Summary: Facts About the Ostrich** (60-80 words): Write a short summary describing the ostrich's appearance, abilities, diet, and how it takes care of its eggs.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: Survival Without Flying** (80-110 words): Most birds can fly, but the ostrich cannot. How do its other abilities help it survive in nature? Write a short paragraph giving your reasons.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **wing** (*kanat*): An ostrich has got wings, but they are too small for flying.

- **weigh** (*ağırlığında olmak, tartmak*): Large male ostriches can weigh up to 135 kilograms.

- **excellent** (*mükemmel, harika*): Because of its strong legs, the ostrich is an excellent runner.

- **insect** (*böcek*): Ostriches eat green plants and some small insects.

- **daytime** (*gündüz vakti*): The female bird looks after the eggs in the daytime.

- **height** (*boy, yükseklik*): Some male ostriches can reach 2.5 meters in height.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Devekuşunun fiziksel özelliklerini, beslenmesini ve yumurtalarına nasıl baktığını özetledim mi?

- [ ] Karşılaştırma sıfatlarını (largest, faster than, fastest) doğru kullandım mı?

- [ ] Parçadan öğrendiğim en az 3 kelimeyi (örneğin wing, weigh, daytime) doğru cümle içinde kullandım mı?

- [ ] Yazım kurallarını, noktalamayı ve kelime sayısını kontrol ettim mi?
