## Passage 86: Manchester

- **ID:** `raw1_086` | **Seviye:** `B1` | **Konu:** Industrial History & Trade | **Sayfa:** 121, 122, 123
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_086_manchester.mp3` (*US · en-US-GuyNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. For centuries, Manchester has been the centre of the ____. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*

2. The Manchester Ship Canal goes all the way from Liverpool to Manchester, a distance of ____. *(İpucu: distance)*

3. On January 1st, 1894, ____ ships sailed up the canal to Manchester for the first time. *(İpucu: number)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. What happens to cotton in Lancashire?

   - A) It is grown in large farms around Manchester.

   - B) It is imported from London by train.

   - C) It is made into cloth in Lancashire and exported worldwide.

   - D) It is sold only to towns inside Britain.

2. Why was exporting goods difficult before the Manchester Ship Canal was built?

   - A) Ships from Liverpool refused to carry cotton.

   - B) Transporting goods overland to Liverpool increased costs and reduced profits.

   - C) Manchester's factories did not produce enough machinery.

   - D) Liverpool's docks were too small for large cargo ships.

3. Which engineering challenges are mentioned in the passage?

   - A) Finding enough ships to test the waterway

   - B) Deepening the mouth of the River Mersey

   - C) Convincing Queen Victoria to attend the opening

   - D) Building bridges for railway lines and controlling rivers and streams


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Ships can load and unload cargo at the port of Manchester only during the daytime.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Queen Victoria officially opened the canal several months after the first ships sailed through it.


### 2. Reading

The City of Manchester is situated in the heart of a huge industrial area of Lancashire. For centuries this inland city has been the centre of the cotton trade. Cotton is not grown in Lancashire, of course, but it is made into cloth there, and the finished material is exported all over the world. All around Manchester are many smaller towns where the cotton industry has developed and where soap, chemicals, dyes, rubber goods and paper goods are manufactured. Manchester itself is now famous not only for the production of machinery of all kinds, but as a great trade centre of England, second only to London.

It was not always so. Until the building of the Manchester Ship Canal the cotton goods had to be transported to Liverpool, over thirty miles away, and there loaded onto cargo ships which carried them all over the world. Naturally, this increased the cost of the goods and reduced the profits of Manchester's trade.

Why is this no longer necessary? Because this famous canal goes all the way from Liverpool to Manchester, a distance of thirty-five miles. It is wide and deep enough to carry large ships easily and safely. It has transformed Manchester from an inland city into one of Britain's greatest ports. Huge docks and warehouses, stores and factories have sprung up along its banks (sides) and, every hour of the day and night, great ships from all over the world are loading or unloading cargo at the port.

The Ship Canal was opened to traffic on January 1st, 1894. It is a very good example of engineering skill and perseverance. Five railway lines crossed the route chosen for the canal and bridges had to be built for them. Rivers and streams also stood in the way. They could not be allowed to flow into the canal because they would have interfered with the water-level. These were only a few of the problems the engineers had to solve.

At last, however, the work was finished. On January 1st, 1894, seventy-one ships sailed for the first time from the mouth of the River Mersey where Liverpool stands, right up to the City of Manchester. On May 21st of the same year Queen Victoria herself sailed up this wonderful waterway to perform the official opening ceremony.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*Comprehension*

1. The City of Manchester is

   - a) surrounded by a large agricultural area

   - b) on the mouth of the River Mersey

   - c) more than thirty miles away from Liverpool

   - d) in Yorkshire

2. The people living in Lancashire are mainly employed in

   - a) growing cotton

   - b) working on the railway

   - c) sailing ships up the canal

   - d) working in factories

3. The phrase "a great trade centre of England, second only to London" means that Manchester

   - a) is not so large in area as London

   - b) has a smaller population than London

   - c) is the most important city for buying and selling goods after London

   - d) has fewer factories than London

4. Before the canal was built, Manchester's export cotton goods were

   - a) sent to London

   - b) sent to all the smaller towns nearby

   - c) carried in large ships to Liverpool

   - d) transported to Liverpool to be loaded on ships

5. Which of these statements is true?

   - a) Queen Victoria's ship was the first to sail up to Manchester.

   - b) The Manchester Ship Canal is about fifty-three miles long.

   - c) The port can be used only during daylight.

   - d) There are many industrial towns in Lancashire.

6. The sentence "It is a very good example of engineering skill and perseverance" means that

   - a) the engineers who built it were so skilful that they found the work easy

   - b) the engineers worked hard until they were skilful

   - c) anybody who wants to build a canal must use this as their model

   - d) the engineers who built it were skilful and they managed to deal with the serious problems they met

7. The Manchester Ship Canal is very beneficial to the city because

   - a) it allows large ships to sail into the port of Manchester

   - b) it was opened in 1894

   - c) it is thirty-five miles long

   - d) it has five railway bridges over it

8. Liverpool

   - a) is an inland city

   - b) is nearer the sea than Manchester

   - c) is famous for its cotton-mills

   - d) was not a port until 1894


*Reference*

9. What does 'them' refer to in "Until the building of the Manchester Ship Canal the cotton goods had to be transported to Liverpool, over thirty miles away, and there loaded onto cargo ships which carried them all over the world"?

10. What does 'its' refer to in "Huge docks and warehouses, stores and factories have sprung up along its banks (sides)"?


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the main purpose of the passage?

   - A) How cotton farming developed across northern England

   - B) How the Manchester Ship Canal transformed Manchester's trade and industry

   - C) Why Liverpool lost its importance as a coastal city

   - D) The biography of Queen Victoria and her industrial projects

2. *[Inference]* What can be inferred from the engineers' refusal to let rivers and streams flow into the canal?

   - A) The canal water was used as drinking water for Lancashire towns.

   - B) The rivers were too polluted with factory chemicals and dyes.

   - C) Keeping a controlled water level was essential for the canal to operate safely.

   - D) The engineers wanted to redirect the rivers toward Liverpool's docks.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In paragraph 3, the phrase "sprung up" is closest in meaning to ____.

   - A) appeared and grew rapidly

   - B) collapsed due to flooding

   - C) were moved away from the water

   - D) remained unchanged for centuries


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Summary: The Impact of the Manchester Ship Canal** (70-90 words): Summarize how the Manchester Ship Canal changed Manchester's economy and solved the city's earlier transport problems.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: Investing in Transportation Infrastructure** (100-130 words): Why are large transportation projects (such as canals, bridges, or railways) important for a country's economy? Explain your opinion with reasons and examples.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **be situated in** (*konumlanmak, yer almak*): The city is situated in the heart of a busy industrial region.

- **transform ... into** (*...i ...e dönüştürmek*): New roads have transformed the small village into a commercial hub.

- **interfere with** (*engellemek, aksatmak, müdahale etmek*): Heavy rain could interfere with the outdoor construction schedule.

- **perseverance** (*azim, sebat, kararlılık*): Building the tunnel through the mountain required great engineering perseverance.

- **spring up** (*aniden ortaya çıkmak, hızla kurulmak*): Many cafes have sprung up near the new university campus.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Paragrafta Manchester'ın kanal öncesi ve sonrası durumunu açıkça karşılaştırdım.

- [ ] Metinden en az 3 faydalı kelime veya kalıbı doğru bağlamda kullandım.

- [ ] Cümlelerim arasında neden-sonuç bağlaçlarına (because, therefore, however) yer verdim.

- [ ] Yazım ve noktalama kurallarını (özellikle büyük harf ve tarih yazımını) kontrol ettim.

## Passage 87: 'SEN' OR 'SİZ'?

- **ID:** `raw1_087` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Language & Politeness | **Sayfa:** 123, 124
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_087_sen_or_si_z.mp3` (*US · en-US-AriaNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. In many countries, calling older people by their ____ is considered very impolite. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*

2. A few hundred years ago, English also had the expression ____. *(İpucu: pronoun)*

3. English speakers never have this difficulty because they don't have ____. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. How do many older people react when young children call them by their first names?

   - A) They prefer to be called by their nicknames.

   - B) They do not remember the children's names.

   - C) They consider it very impolite and feel uncomfortable.

   - D) They think it is only acceptable in France.

2. Which pair of words represents the informal form of 'you' in Turkish and French?

   - A) 'sen' and 'tu'

   - B) 'siz' and 'vous'

   - C) 'sen' and 'vous'

   - D) 'thou' and 'you'

3. What does it mean in France when speakers change from 'vous' to 'tu'?

   - A) They are speaking to an older stranger.

   - B) They want to make the conversation more polite.

   - C) They have forgotten the formal word.

   - D) Their relationship has changed from formal to informal.


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Young people in Turkey and France normally use the informal form when speaking with one another.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Americans and Australians must carefully choose between formal and informal words for 'you'.


### 2. Reading

What do you call your parents? Mother? Father? Mum? Dad? Or do you call them by their first names? These days, many young children call their parents, and their parents' friends, by their first names. However, in many countries this kind of familiarity is very impolite, and as a result many of the older people become uncomfortable.

In some languages, such as French and Turkish, there are two forms for the word 'you'. In Turkish, 'sen' is the informal form and 'siz' the formal. In French, the familiar word is 'tu' and the more formal one is 'vous'. In English, there is only one form, 'you', although a few hundred years ago there was also the formal expression 'thou'.

In Turkey and France, the two forms of the word are part of everyday life. In both countries it is often unacceptable for an older person to be called by the word 'sen' or 'tu' when he is talking to a younger person, but the young people usually use the informal form when they are talking to each other. In France, the change from 'vous' to 'tu' shows a change in the relationship - from a formal one to an informal one. English speaking people - Australians, Americans and the British - never have this difficulty because they don't have another choice.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A. Reference*

1. What does 'them' refer to in "What do you call your parents? Mother? Father? Mum? Dad? Or do you call them by their first names?"

2. What does 'their' refer to in "These days, many young children call their parents, and their parents' friends, by their first names"?

3. What does 'the word' refer to in "In Turkey and France, the two forms of the word are part of everyday life"?

4. What does 'both countries' refer to in "In both countries it is often unacceptable for an older person to be called by the word 'sen' or 'tu'"?

5. What does 'they' refer to in "English speaking people - Australians, Americans and the British - never have this difficulty because they don't have another choice"?


*B. True / False*

6. In many countries calling older people by their first names is impolite.

   - True

   - False

7. Two forms of the word 'you' exist in most languages.

   - True

   - False

8. English speakers have to choose between the two forms of the word 'you'.

   - True

   - False


*C. Comprehension Questions*

9. When was 'thou' a part of the English language?

10. When is it acceptable to use the informal form of 'you' in Turkish and French?

11. How does a relationship change when a French speaker uses 'vous' instead of 'tu'?


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the passage mainly about?

   - A) Why young children dislike their parents' friends

   - B) How different languages and cultures handle formal and informal ways of addressing people

   - C) How the English word 'thou' entered the French language

   - D) Why Turkish and French have similar grammar rules

2. *[Detail]* According to the passage, when speaking to an older person in Turkey or France, a younger person is expected to use ____.

   - A) a formal form of 'you' such as 'siz' or 'vous'

   - B) the person's first name without a title

   - C) the informal form 'sen' or 'tu'

   - D) the older English word 'thou'

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In paragraph 1, the word "familiarity" refers to ____.

   - A) a detailed knowledge of a foreign language

   - B) a strong fear of meeting strangers

   - C) informal and close behaviour that may seem disrespectful

   - D) a traditional rule for naming newborn children


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Summary: Formal and Informal Forms of Address** (55-75 words): Write a short summary explaining how Turkish, French, and English differ in the way speakers address other people.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: Formal vs. Informal 'You'** (70-100 words): Do you think it is better for a language to have two forms of 'you' (formal and informal) like Turkish, or just one form like English? Give your reasons.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **familiarity** (*aşırı samimiyet, teklifsizlik*): Calling a teacher by their first name shows too much familiarity in some schools.

- **uncomfortable** (*rahatsız, huzursuz*): He felt uncomfortable when everyone stared at him.

- **impolite** (*kaba, nezaketsiz*): It is impolite to interrupt someone while they are speaking.

- **unacceptable** (*kabul edilemez*): Shouting in a library is unacceptable behaviour.

- **choice** (*seçenek, tercih*): We had no choice but to wait for the next train.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] İngilizce, Türkçe ve Fransızcadaki 'you' kullanım farklarını doğru özetledim.

- [ ] Saygı ve samimiyet bildiren ifadelere net örnekler verdim.

- [ ] Kendi görüşümü açıklarken günlük hayattan somut bir örnek ekledim.

- [ ] Cümlelerdeki zaman uyumunu ve noktalama işaretlerini kontrol ettim.

## Passage 88: Vive la Difference!

- **ID:** `raw1_088` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Culture & Travel Customs | **Sayfa:** 125, 126
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_088_vive_la_difference.mp3` (*UK · en-GB-RyanNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. There are more than ____ in the world, from very large ones to very small ones. *(İpucu: number and noun)*

2. When greeting friends in France, people kiss one another on ____. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*

3. In Saudi Arabia, people race ____ in their spare time. *(İpucu: plural noun)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Which differences can a visitor notice right away in a new country?

   - A) What people do in their spare time

   - B) The money people use and the food they eat

   - C) How people greet close friends and strangers

   - D) The beliefs and traditions of the people

2. What eating custom in the United States is described in the passage?

   - A) People never use knives at the table.

   - B) People think it is impolite to cut meat with a fork.

   - C) People cut their meat into small pieces first and then eat only with a fork.

   - D) People always say 'Guten Appetit' before eating.

3. What does the passage say about cricket?

   - A) Cricket is popular in Britain, though not in the rest of Europe.

   - B) Cricket is the most popular sport across all of Europe.

   - C) People in Japan watch cricket in their spare time.

   - D) Sumo wrestling is a traditional sport in Britain.


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — In Germany, people believe it is polite to cut potatoes with a knife.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Some cultural differences can only be understood after getting to know local people well.


### 2. Reading

There are more than 150 countries in the world. Some of them are very big, with millions of people. Others are smaller than some cities, with only a few thousand people. People in different countries have their own customs (i.e. social habits), traditions, languages and beliefs.

There are, of course, very obvious differences. A visitor immediately sees what people look like, the different money they use and the different food they eat. However, there are some differences you cannot see immediately. You learn about these when you get to know the people of the country well.

One difference between different countries is the way people greet their friends. In France, for example, people kiss one another on both cheeks. In Britain, people usually only kiss close friends and relatives, and only shake hands with people they meet for the first time.

There are also a lot of different customs linked with eating. For instance, in many countries people say something to each other before they start eating, such as 'Guten Appetit', but in Britain this is not the custom. In the United States people cut their meat into small pieces before they start to eat, and then they eat only with a fork. In Germany, it is impolite to cut potatoes with a knife, and in many countries people don't eat with knives or forks at all.

What people do in their spare time in the evening or at the weekend can also be very different. Sport is an example of this. Cricket is a popular sport in Britain but not in the rest of Europe. People race camels in Saudi Arabia and watch Sumo wrestling in Japan.

All these differences between countries make travel interesting.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A. Reference*

1. What does 'Others' refer to in "Others are smaller than some cities, with only a few thousand people"? (Complete: "other ____")

2. What does 'these' refer to in "However, there are some differences you cannot see immediately. You learn about these when you get to know the people of the country well"?

3. What does 'this' refer to in "in many countries people say something to each other before they start eating, such as 'Guten Appetit', but in Britain this is not the custom"?

4. What does 'their' refer to in "What people do in their spare time in the evening or at the weekend can also be very different"?


*B. True / False*

5. There are more than 150 large countries in the world.

   - True

   - False

6. Social habits, traditions, languages and beliefs change from country to country.

   - True

   - False

7. British people do not kiss people they meet for the first time.

   - True

   - False

8. In the United States, a knife is never used during a meal.

   - True

   - False

9. There are countries where people eat without knives and forks.

   - True

   - False

10. Cricket is popular in all European countries.

   - True

   - False

11. Travelling is interesting because of the differences between countries.

   - True

   - False


*C. Comprehension Questions*

12. What three differences between countries are easy to see?

13. Three differences between countries cannot be seen immediately. What are these differences in?

14. What is the main idea of this passage?


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Author's Purpose]* Why does the author mention 'Guten Appetit', cutting potatoes, and eating without knives or forks in paragraph 4?

   - A) To explain how to cook traditional meals in Germany and Britain

   - B) To argue that eating with a fork is better than eating with a knife

   - C) To give examples of how table manners and eating habits differ around the world

   - D) To show that British people have no polite customs before meals

2. *[Inference]* Why does the passage say some differences "cannot be seen immediately"?

   - A) Some countries are too dark to see clearly at night.

   - B) Visitors are usually not allowed to watch local sports.

   - C) Foreign languages are too difficult for tourists to understand.

   - D) Understanding social customs takes time and closer contact with local people.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In paragraph 5, the phrase "spare time" is closest in meaning to ____.

   - A) working hours

   - B) free time

   - C) meal times

   - D) travel time


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Summary: Cultural Differences Around the World** (60-80 words): Summarize the obvious and less obvious cultural differences mentioned in the passage.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: Respecting Local Customs While Travelling** (80-110 words): Why is it useful to learn about a country's customs before you visit it? Give examples from your own culture or another culture.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **custom** (*gelenek, adet, alışkanlık*): Every region has its own local customs and festivals.

- **obvious** (*açık, bariz, belli*): It is obvious that she enjoys learning about new cultures.

- **greet** (*selamlamak, karşılamak*): People greet each other differently depending on where they live.

- **spare time** (*boş zaman*): In his spare time, he likes reading travel books and playing chess.

- **linked with** (*...ile bağlantılı, ilişkili*): Many daily habits are closely linked with local traditions.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Görünür olan ve hemen fark edilmeyen kültürel farklılıkları net bir şekilde ayırdım.

- [ ] Metinden en az iki farklı ülke ve gelenek örneğine yer verdim.

- [ ] Kendi ülkeme ait gelenekleri açık ve anlaşılır İngilizce cümlelerle anlattım.

- [ ] Tekil-çoğul uyumunu ve geniş zaman (Simple Present) yapısını kontrol ettim.

## Passage 89: Tornadoes

- **ID:** `raw1_089` | **Seviye:** `B1` | **Konu:** Weather & Natural Disasters | **Sayfa:** 127, 128
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_089_tornadoes.mp3` (*US · en-US-JennyNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. Like a giant ____, a tornado sucks up anything in its path. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*

2. The severe damage a tornado causes in a single location lasts only about ____. *(İpucu: time duration)*

3. The destructive tornado of March 18, 1925 killed ____ people across three states. *(İpucu: number)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. How fast can the wind inside a tornado blow?

   - A) About 96 kilometres per hour

   - B) About 354 kilometres per hour

   - C) More than 800 kilometres per hour

   - D) About 200 kilometres per hour

2. In what kind of weather do tornadoes normally occur?

   - A) Cold, dry winter days

   - B) Hot, humid days

   - C) Cool, windy autumn nights

   - D) Rainy spring mornings only

3. Where is the safest place to stay if a tornado is likely to strike?

   - A) Inside a freight train

   - B) On the top floor of a building

   - C) Behind a large wooden post

   - D) In an underground cellar or basement


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Anyone lifted by a tornado always suffers serious physical injuries.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Over two hundred tornadoes hit the United States annually.


### 2. Reading

The great power of tornadoes is almost unbelievable. The speed of this whirling funnel-shaped wind may be more than 800 kilometres per hour. Like a giant vacuum cleaner, it sucks up anything in its path.

There are many interesting stories about the strange things that tornadoes have done in the United States. Common wheat straw has been driven several centimetres into posts and trees. Buildings have been turned completely around on their foundations and have remained intact. People and animals have been carried hundreds of metres, often suffering no physical harm. Feathers have been removed from chickens. Cars, trucks, and even whole freight trains have been carried away.

Fortunately, a tornado does not last long, about 20 to 30 minutes on the average. Usually, it destroys an area about 26 kilometres long, and the great damage that it does in one place lasts only about 30 seconds. Tornadoes normally occur on hot, humid days but not necessarily in the summer. The biggest and most destructive tornado in the United States struck on March 18, 1925. Roaring along at a speed of 96 kilometres per hour, it swept clean a path 2 kilometres wide across the states of Missouri, Illinois, and Indiana. In its 354-kilometre long journey across these three states, the tornado killed 689 people.

More than 200 tornadoes strike in the United States every year. It is not possible to predict when a tornado will strike although the U.S. Weather Bureau gives storm warnings when conditions are right to cause a tornado. The safest place to be if a tornado seems likely is in some underground area such as a cellar or a basement.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A*

1. How long does a tornado last?

2. What weather conditions are suitable for a tornado?

3. What is the best place to hide in case of a tornado?

4. Give two examples of the damage tornadoes can cause.


*B*

5. The largest recorded tornado speed in the world was 96 kilometres per hour.

   - True

   - False

6. Tornadoes occur only during the summer months.

   - True

   - False

7. Tornadoes have enough force to move buildings.

   - True

   - False

8. Fortunately, tornadoes do not occur in the United States frequently.

   - True

   - False

9. The most destructive tornado to strike the United States took place in the spring.

   - True

   - False

10. Meteorologists are still not very successful in their tornado predictions.

   - True

   - False


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Author's Purpose]* Why does the author mention wheat straw, chickens, and freight trains in the second paragraph?

   - A) To prove that tornadoes only happen in rural farming areas

   - B) To explain how the U.S. Weather Bureau measures wind speed

   - C) To illustrate the extraordinary and strange force of tornadoes

   - D) To show that modern buildings cannot survive strong storms

2. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In paragraph 2, the word "intact" is closest in meaning to ____.

   - A) Completely destroyed

   - B) Undamaged and in one piece

   - C) Buried underground

   - D) Empty of people

3. *[Inference]* What can be inferred about tornado forecasting in the United States?

   - A) Officials can tell when conditions favour a tornado, not the exact moment it will hit

   - B) The U.S. Weather Bureau can predict tornadoes several days before they form

   - C) Storm warnings are only issued after a tornado has already caused damage

   - D) Tornadoes always follow a predictable path across three states


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Summarizing Tornado Facts** (70-90 words): Summarize the main characteristics, effects, and safety precautions related to tornadoes based on the passage.

- **Task 2 — Preparedness Against Unpredictable Storms** (120-150 words): Do you think storm warnings are useful even if scientists cannot predict the exact time a tornado will strike? Give reasons to support your opinion.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **whirling** (*dönen, fırıl fırıl dönen*): The whirling winds lifted dust high into the sky.

- **intact** (*sağlam, bozulmamış*): Surprisingly, the glass vase remained intact after falling off the table.

- **damage** (*hasar, zarar*): The coastal town suffered heavy damage during the hurricane.

- **predict** (*tahmin etmek, öngörmek*): Experts still cannot predict earthquakes with complete accuracy.

- **warning** (*uyarı, ikaz*): The city issued flood warnings before the heavy rain arrived.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Parçada geçen ana bilgileri kendi cümlelerimle özetledim.

- [ ] Geçmiş olaylar ile genel gerçekler için uygun fiil zamanlarını kullandım.

- [ ] Fikirlerimi bağlaçlarla (however, although, therefore vb.) akıcı şekilde bağladım.

- [ ] Hedef kelime sayısına uydum ve yazım/noktalama kontrolü yaptım.

## Passage 90: Industrial Medicine and Safety

- **ID:** `raw1_090` | **Seviye:** `B1` | **Konu:** Workplace Health & Safety | **Sayfa:** 129, 130
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_090_industrial_medicine_and_safety.mp3` (*AU · en-AU-NatashaNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. After working in coal mines for years, most coal miners develop ____. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*

2. Exposure to loud industrial noise can sometimes lead to ____. *(İpucu: noun)*

3. When working outside near airplanes, airport workers are required to ____. *(İpucu: verb phrase)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Who educates workers about the dangers of their jobs in large companies?

   - A) Only company managers without medical training

   - B) Industrial doctors and safety engineers

   - C) Government inspectors from outside the company

   - D) Experienced farm and construction workers

2. Which parts of the body can be harmed by new toxic chemicals?

   - A) Only the eyes and ears

   - B) Bones and muscles in the legs

   - C) The skin, lungs, and internal organs

   - D) Teeth and hair

3. Which items are mentioned as examples of protective clothing?

   - A) Eye goggles, hard hats, and safety shoes

   - B) Heavy coats, umbrellas, and scarves

   - C) Leather jackets and sunglasses

   - D) Cotton gloves and running shoes


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Lead, mercury, and silica were only discovered recently in modern factories.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Small businesses are required by law to employ at least one safety engineer.


### 2. Reading

People who work together often get the same diseases (illnesses). For example, lung cancer is common among asbestos workers and miners. Many people who work with X-rays get skin cancer. Most coal miners develop black lung disease after years of work in coal mines. These people probably get these diseases because of their work. In addition, accidents on the job may injure or kill workers. For instance, farm machines sometimes injure farm workers. Construction workers sometimes fall from buildings. These injuries can be fatal. People who work with electricity may get electric shocks. Fires sometimes kill fire fighters.

Many large companies employ both industrial doctors and safety engineers to protect workers from accidents and illness on the job. They do this in three ways. First, doctors study the workers and their diseases. They know that certain groups of workers often have the same diseases. At the same time, safety engineers study accidents and their causes. Second, doctors and safety engineers work together to change the machines or the job because they want to prevent accidents or illnesses. Third, they educate workers about the dangers of their jobs. They teach the workers about safety on the job. Education is necessary for prevention.

There are many causes of accidents and illness at work. Chemicals, noise, and radiation are a few. For example, gases in mines cause accidents and poison miners. Toxic chemicals, such as lead (Pb), mercury (Hg), and silica (SiO2), made people sick in the past, and they make people sick today. In addition, there are new chemicals, and some of them are toxic. They harm the skin, the lungs, and other internal organs such as the heart, liver, or kidney. Industrial noise sometimes causes deafness. Many things in the workplace can cause illness or injury.

Industrial physicians and safety engineers often prevent these problems. After they find the causes, they plan and design new machines to prevent accidents in the future. They design different ways to store and transport toxic chemicals. They measure and control gases in the air. They set standards for work in dangerous situations. For example, airport workers must cover their ears near the airplanes outside. Some workers must wear protective clothing, like eye goggles, hard hats, and safety shoes.

After careful study, both doctors and safety engineers do many things to decrease the danger of injury and illness on the job. They make the work area a safe place.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A*

1. In "Many large companies employ both industrial doctors and safety engineers to protect workers from accidents and illness on the job. They do this in three ways.", what does "They do this" refer to?

   - a) Companies employ industrial doctors and safety engineers

   - b) Workers have accidents and illnesses at work

   - c) Industrial doctors and safety engineers study the causes of illnesses and accidents

   - d) Companies protect workers from accidents and illness

2. In "There are many causes of accidents and illness at work. Chemicals, noise, and radiation are a few.", what does "a few" refer to?

   - a) causes

   - b) accidents

   - c) gases in mines

   - d) chemicals

3. In "They harm the skin, the lungs, and other internal organs such as the heart, liver, or kidney.", when something harms you, it ____ you.

   - a) provides

   - b) heats

   - c) hurts

   - d) decreases

4. In "Industrial noise sometimes causes deafness.", a person who suffers from deafness cannot ____.

   - a) see

   - b) speak

   - c) hear

   - d) walk


*B*

5. Coal miners and asbestos workers have health problems because of their work.

   - True

   - False

6. Fatal accidents sometimes occur on farms and at construction sites.

   - True

   - False

7. Safety engineers are interested in job-related diseases.

   - True

   - False

8. Job-safety training can help prevent future accidents.

   - True

   - False

9. Lead, mercury and silica are some of the new industrial chemicals.

   - True

   - False

10. Safety engineers design new machines in order to find the causes of accidents.

   - True

   - False

11. The storage and transportation of toxic substances are important in making the workplace safe.

   - True

   - False

12. Everybody who works in large companies should wear protective clothing.

   - True

   - False


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the main purpose of the passage?

   - A) To argue that mining and construction jobs should be banned

   - B) To compare medical treatments for lung cancer and skin cancer

   - C) To explain workplace health risks and how specialists prevent them

   - D) To describe the history of chemical discoveries in industry

2. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In paragraph 1, the word "fatal" is closest in meaning to ____.

   - A) Very painful but temporary

   - B) Causing death

   - C) Easy to prevent

   - D) Caused by chemicals

3. *[Detail]* How do industrial doctors and safety engineers divide and combine their work?

   - A) Doctors treat injuries only after a factory closes down

   - B) Safety engineers are mainly responsible for curing lung diseases

   - C) Doctors and safety engineers work completely separately without sharing ideas

   - D) Doctors focus on work-related illnesses while safety engineers focus on accidents, and both cooperate on prevention


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Protecting Workers from Occupational Hazards** (65-85 words): Summarize the main workplace dangers mentioned in the passage and the three ways specialists protect workers.

- **Task 2 — Rules vs. Education in Workplace Safety** (120-150 words): Which is more important for workplace safety: strict company rules and equipment, or educating workers? Explain your view with examples.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **fatal** (*ölümcül*): Wearing seat belts can prevent fatal injuries in car crashes.

- **toxic** (*zehirli, toksik*): The factory stores toxic waste in sealed containers.

- **prevention** (*önleme, engelleme*): Regular handwashing helps with the prevention of infections.

- **protective clothing** (*koruyucu giysi*): Construction workers must wear protective clothing on site.

- **decrease** (*azaltmak, düşürmek*): New safety rules aim to decrease accidents in mines.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] İş sağlığı risklerini ve önleme yöntemlerini net biçimde özetledim.

- [ ] Neden-sonuç ilişkilerini kurarken uygun yapılar (because, in order to, after vb.) kullandım.

- [ ] Parçadaki teknik terimleri doğru anlamlarıyla kullandım.

- [ ] Yazımı kelime sınırı ve dil bilgisi açısından gözden geçirdim.

## Passage 91: Learning About Computers

- **ID:** `raw1_091` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Computers & Technology | **Sayfa:** 131, 132
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_091_learning_about_computers.mp3` (*UK · en-GB-SoniaNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. The CPU is made up of billions of ____. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*

2. Computers typically store information on disks that resemble ____. *(İpucu: plural noun)*

3. Instead of programming a computer ourselves, we only need to learn how to use an ____. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Which part is described as the heart of the computer?

   - A) The CPU

   - B) The monitor

   - C) The keyboard

   - D) The printer

2. What does the monitor do?

   - A) It prints documents onto paper

   - B) It translates information for the processor

   - C) It displays typed text or saved information from memory

   - D) It stores disks inside a connected drive

3. What two examples of computer programmes are mentioned in the script?

   - A) Repairing hardware and printing photos

   - B) Teaching French and playing games

   - C) Typing letters and cleaning records

   - D) Building CPUs and connecting drives


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Practical use is the only way to learn how to use a computer.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Everyone must learn how to write computer programmes before using a computer.


### 2. Reading

A computer is a giant brain which can help us learn fast, work successfully, or just have a good time. It can also store, or keep, very large amounts of information for easy reference. The only way to learn to use a computer is to do so. But before we start, we should know what the different parts of a computer do.

The Basic Parts of a Computer System

HARDWARE - The hardware is the computer machinery and consists of the following parts:

CPU - The CPU (central processing unit) is the heart of the computer. It contains the processor, which changes information into a form that we can understand, and the memory, which saves information you want to keep. The CPU consists of billions of on and off switches.

KEYBOARD - We put information into the computer using the keyboard. It has keys that look like those on a typewriter.

DISKS and DISK DRIVE - Most computers store information on disks, which look like records. We put the disks into a disk drive, which is connected to the CPU.

MONITOR - The monitor looks like a television screen. It shows either new information which we have typed or information which we have asked the computer to give us from the memory.

PRINTER - The printer prints any information which we choose to have on paper.

SOFTWARE - The software is the computer programme. A programme is a set of codes which tells the computer how to work, so we need different programmes to do different tasks. For example, some programmes can teach us French, and some are games. There are thousands of different programmes. However, it is not necessary to know how to programme a computer. This is the job of specialists. All we have to learn is how to use an existing programme.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A*

1. In what ways can a computer help us? (Write two.)

2. What does the memory do?


*B*

3. The computer machinery is called the software.

   - True

   - False

4. The CPU consists of keys which are like the keys of a typewriter.

   - True

   - False

5. We can see information on the monitor.

   - True

   - False

6. The printer is a part of the CPU.

   - True

   - False

7. There are computer programmes which can teach us a foreign language.

   - True

   - False

8. Only a specialist can use a computer.

   - True

   - False


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Detail]* What is the function of the processor inside the CPU?

   - A) To display typed words on a television-like screen

   - B) To change information into a form people can understand

   - C) To print selected documents onto paper

   - D) To hold disks that look like records

2. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In paragraph 1, the word "store" means to ____.

   - A) Buy

   - B) Delete

   - C) Print

   - D) Keep

3. *[Main Idea]* What is the main difference between hardware and software according to the passage?

   - A) Hardware refers to the physical machine parts, while software refers to the programmes that direct them

   - B) Hardware is used only by specialists, while software is used by beginners

   - C) Hardware includes the keyboard and monitor, while software includes the CPU and printer

   - D) Hardware teaches languages like French, while software saves typed data on disks


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Hardware and Software Basics** (60-80 words): Write a short summary explaining the difference between hardware and software and naming the main hardware parts.

- **Task 2 — Using Computers for Learning** (80-110 words): How can a computer help students learn better? Give two reasons and examples.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **reference** (*başvuru, kaynak olarak kullanma*): She keeps all her study notes in a folder for easy reference.

- **store** (*depolamak, saklamak*): Computers can store thousands of photos and documents.

- **machinery** (*makineler, aksam*): The factory replaced its old machinery with modern equipment.

- **existing** (*mevcut, var olan*): This programme helps beginners learn basic vocabulary.

- **specialist** (*uzman*): You should ask an IT specialist to fix the network problem.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Donanım ve yazılım parçalarını açık ve doğru biçimde açıkladım.

- [ ] Basit ve anlaşılır cümle yapıları kullandım.

- [ ] Kendi düşüncemi destekleyen somut örnekler verdim.

- [ ] Kelime sayısını ve noktalama işaretlerini kontrol ettim.

## Passage 92: Seat Belts

- **ID:** `raw1_092` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Road Safety Laws | **Sayfa:** 132, 133
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_092_seat_belts.mp3` (*US · en-US-GuyNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. If you are 14 or over and do not wear a seat belt, you can be fined up to ____. *(İpucu: amount of money)*

2. The driver must make sure that ____ under 14 wear a seat belt in the front. *(İpucu: noun (plural))*

3. You do not have to wear a seat belt when you are ____ your vehicle. *(İpucu: verb (-ing))*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. When two passengers sit in the front of a vehicle with a bench seat, who does NOT have to wear a seat belt?

   - A) The driver of the vehicle

   - B) The passenger sitting next to the window

   - C) The person sitting in the middle front seat

   - D) Any passenger under the age of 14

2. What should people with certain health conditions do if they should not wear a seat belt?

   - A) Get permission from a police officer

   - B) Get a medical certificate from a doctor

   - C) Drive only at low speeds

   - D) Sit in the middle of a bench seat

3. What should you do after receiving a medical certificate from your doctor?

   - A) Leave it at the doctor's office

   - B) Send it to the vehicle company

   - C) Attach it to the front windshield

   - D) Keep it and show it to the police if asked


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — If only one passenger sits in the front on a bench seat, they do not have to wear a seat belt.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Passengers sitting in the back seat can also be fined 50 pounds if they do not wear a seat belt.


### 2. Reading

Who has to wear a seat belt

Drivers or front seat passengers in most vehicles have to wear a seat belt. If you are 14 or over, it will be your responsibility to wear your belt. If you do not, you can be fined up to £50. The driver will not have to make you wear your belt. But he will have to make sure that children under 14 do not ride in the front if they are not wearing a seat belt.

A few vehicles have a middle front seat between the front passenger seat and the driver's seat; for example, a bench seat. Your vehicle may be one of them. If just one passenger sits in the front, he must wear a seat belt. But if two passengers sit in the front, the person sitting in the middle will not have to wear a belt.

When you do not have to wear a seat belt

You do not have to wear a seat belt on certain conditions. One of these is when you are backing your vehicle. Another is if you have a medical certificate. Certain people should not wear a seat belt because of their health. It may be worse for them to wear a belt than to be in a road accident without one. They will not have to wear a belt if they get a medical certificate from a doctor. If you think you are one of these people, go and talk to your doctor as soon as possible. Keep the certificate. If the police ask you why you are not wearing a seat belt, you should show them the certificate.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A*

1. In "A few vehicles have a middle front seat between the front passenger seat and the driver's seat; for example, a bench seat. Your vehicle may be one of them.", 'them' refers to vehicles with ____.

   - a) bench seats in the front and the back

   - b) a middle front seat

   - c) a middle seat in the back

   - d) a front passenger seat

2. In "If you think you are one of these people, go and talk to your doctor as soon as possible.", 'these people' refers to people who ____.

   - a) are backing their vehicles

   - b) have road accidents without wearing seat belts

   - c) give medical certificates

   - d) shouldn't wear seat belts because of their health


*B*

3. It is a driver's responsibility to make all the front seat passengers wear seat belts.

   - True

   - False

4. People who talk to a doctor don't have to wear seat belts.

   - True

   - False


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the main topic of the passage?

   - A) How to repair broken seat belts in old vehicles

   - B) Why bench seats are safer than regular seats

   - C) Rules and exceptions for wearing seat belts in vehicles

   - D) How police officers test drivers for road safety

2. *[Inference]* What happens if a 16-year-old passenger rides in the front passenger seat without a seat belt?

   - A) The driver must pay a £50 fine for the passenger.

   - B) The 16-year-old passenger can be fined up to £50.

   - C) Nobody is fined because the passenger is under 18.

   - D) The passenger must sit in the middle seat instead.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* What does the phrase "backing your vehicle" mean in the passage?

   - A) Driving your vehicle backwards

   - B) Stopping your vehicle by the road

   - C) Loading heavy bags into your vehicle

   - D) Following another vehicle closely


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Seat Belt Rules Summary** (60-80 words): Summarize the main rules and exceptions for wearing seat belts mentioned in the passage.

- **Task 2 — Should Everyone Wear a Seat Belt?** (80-110 words): Do you think wearing a seat belt should be mandatory for all passengers, including those in the back seat? Explain your opinion with simple reasons.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **seat belt** (*emniyet kemeri*): Drivers must wear a seat belt for their own safety.

- **responsibility** (*sorumluluk*): It is your responsibility to lock the door before leaving.

- **be fined** (*para cezasi almak*): You can be fined if you park your car here.

- **medical certificate** (*saglik raporu*): You need a medical certificate to skip sports class.

- **back a vehicle** (*araci geri surmek*): Always check your mirrors when backing your vehicle.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Emniyet kemeri takma kurallarini ve yas sinirini (14 yas) dogru acikladim mi?

- [ ] Emniyet kemeri takmanin zorunlu olmadigi istisnalari (geri gitme, saglik raporu, orta koltuk) belirttim mi?

- [ ] 'have to', 'must' ve 'should' gibi zorunluluk kiplerini dogru kullandim mi?

- [ ] Cumlelerimi basit ve akici baglaclarla ('but', 'because', 'if') birbirine bagladim mi?

## Passage 93: James Dean

- **ID:** `raw1_093` | **Seviye:** `B1` | **Konu:** Cinema & Biography | **Sayfa:** 133, 134
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_093_james_dean.mp3` (*US · en-US-AriaNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. When he had very little money, Dean sometimes mixed his cereals with ____. *(İpucu: noun (food))*

2. When Dean went to parties, he often brought his ____ with him. *(İpucu: noun phrase (musical instrument))*

3. ____ copies of the book Jimmy Dean Returns were sold. *(İpucu: quantity / number phrase)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. How did director Elia Kazan discover James Dean?

   - A) While Dean was eating in a Hollywood restaurant

   - B) At a party where Dean was playing drums

   - C) While Dean was acting in a play in New York

   - D) During a car race in California

2. Why did Rock Hudson dislike working with James Dean on the film Giant?

   - A) He found Dean angry and difficult to be with.

   - B) He thought Dean was too shy to speak on camera.

   - C) He was jealous of Dean's fast sports cars.

   - D) He disliked the script of the film Giant.

3. What caused James Dean's fatal car accident in 1955?

   - A) His new Porsche had broken brakes.

   - B) The light was poor and he was not wearing his glasses.

   - C) He fell asleep while driving home from a party.

   - D) Another driver pushed his car off the main road.


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — At parties, James Dean often avoided talking to people and took off his glasses.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — James Dean won several professional sports car races before he died.


### 2. Reading

James Dean, the famous American actor, was not very tall - less than medium height - and had thick fair hair, and intense blue eyes. He was short-sighted - he couldn't really see anything without his glasses.

James came to Hollywood when he was eighteen, hoping to get into films. At first he was not at all successful and for a time he had to live on very little money. There were days when he only ate cereals, which he sometimes mixed with marmalade. Then he went to New York, the centre of live theatre in the United States. He got a part in a play and was seen by the great director, Elia Kazan. Kazan was planning the film East of Eden and realised Dean would be perfect for one of the main parts.

Dean was difficult to work with. He was also an eccentric person. He always wore the same overcoat and jeans, rarely shaved and had only one or two friends. He sometimes didn't talk to anybody for days. When he went to parties, he often brought his bongo drums with him. Then he sat in a corner, took off his glasses because he didn't want to see anybody, and played the drums all night without saying a word.

He went back to Hollywood to make East of Eden and then made two more films, Rebel Without a Cause and Giant. His co-star in the last film, Rock Hudson, later said, "I didn't like Dean particularly. I didn't like working with him, either. He was hard to be with. He was always angry and he never smiled."

Although he had poor eyesight, he loved riding on fast motorbikes and driving even faster sports cars. A few days after he finished Giant, on 25 September 1955, Dean went for a drive in his new Porsche. The sun was going down so the light was poor. And unfortunately, Dean was not wearing his glasses. Suddenly, he saw a car in front of him. It was slowing down to turn into a side road. Dean tried to slow down, too, but it was too late. He hit the car at high speed and was killed immediately.

Two German girls killed themselves when they heard the news because they believed it was impossible to live without him. A few years later, a New York shopgirl wrote a book called Jimmy Dean Returns, in which she wrote that she was communicating with Dean from the other world. Half a million copies of the book were sold. It was only the beginning of a strange legend that goes on, even today.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A*

1. In "He was short-sighted - he couldn't really see anything without his glasses.", a 'short-sighted' person ____.

   - a) is not tall

   - b) does not wear glasses

   - c) has trouble with his eyes

   - d) is fair-haired with blue eyes

2. In "He was also an eccentric person.", someone who is 'eccentric' ____.

   - a) has ideas and habits that other people find strange

   - b) plays a musical instrument, such as the bongo drums

   - c) is sociable and enjoys being with people

   - d) is an ordinary person


*B*

3. Dean was acting in a play in New York when Elia Kazan discovered him.

   - True

   - False

4. Rock Hudson believed that Dean was not an easy-going person.

   - True

   - False

5. As well as Dean, two girls were killed in the accident in 1955.

   - True

   - False

6. Until his death, Dean acted in four films - East of Eden, Rebel Without a Cause, Giant and Jimmy Dean Returns.

   - True

   - False


*C*

7. In which film(s) did Dean and Rock Hudson act together?

8. Why didn't Dean see the car in front of him? Give two reasons.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Detail]* What event marked the turning point in James Dean's acting career?

   - A) Moving to Hollywood at the age of eighteen

   - B) Buying a new Porsche after filming Giant

   - C) Meeting Rock Hudson on a film set

   - D) Getting a theatre role in New York and meeting Elia Kazan

2. *[Inference]* What does the final paragraph suggest about James Dean's popularity after his death?

   - A) Most fans quickly forgot about James Dean after 1955.

   - B) James Dean secretly wrote a book before his accident.

   - C) Dean's death inspired extreme reactions and an enduring legend among fans.

   - D) Hollywood directors refused to release Giant after Dean died.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In paragraph 2, what does the phrase "live on" mean in "he had to live on very little money"?

   - A) Rent an expensive apartment with

   - B) Survive and pay for basic needs with

   - C) Give away to close friends

   - D) Save in a bank account for


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Biography Summary: James Dean** (65-85 words): Write a short summary of James Dean's acting career, personality, and tragic death based on the passage.

- **Task 2 — The Legend of Young Stars** (120-150 words): Why do you think some famous actors or musicians become legends after dying young? Share your thoughts and give reasons.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **short-sighted** (*miyop, uzagi goremeyen*): She is short-sighted, so she wears glasses when driving.

- **live on** (*(belli bir parayla) gecinmek*): Students often have to live on a small budget.

- **eccentric** (*tuhaf, alisilmadik (kimse)*): Our eccentric neighbour paints his front door a different colour every month.

- **co-star** (*rol arkadasi*): He became close friends with his co-star during the filming.

- **at high speed** (*yuksek hizla*): Driving at high speed in bad weather is very dangerous.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

## Passage 94: Skyscrapers

- **ID:** `raw1_094` | **Seviye:** `B1` | **Konu:** Architecture & History | **Sayfa:** 135, 136
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_094_skyscrapers.mp3` (*UK · en-GB-RyanNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. The Home Insurance Company Building was built in Chicago in ____. *(İpucu: year / number)*

2. For the first time, the Reliance Building had ____ that covered almost the entire surface. *(İpucu: noun (plural))*

3. In 1956, Frank Lloyd Wright dreamed of building a skyscraper of ____ floors. *(İpucu: number)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Where was the first skyscraper built?

   - A) New York City

   - B) Chicago

   - C) London

   - D) Detroit

2. What happened to the Home Insurance Company Building?

   - A) It is now a luxury hotel.

   - B) It burned down in 1894.

   - C) It was pulled down in 1931.

   - D) Tourists can still visit its top floor.

3. Besides technology, what two things are needed to build even taller skyscrapers today?

   - A) Faster elevators and stronger glass

   - B) Permission from Frank Lloyd Wright's family

   - C) More stone walls and wider streets

   - D) Enough money and protection of the environment


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Residents on different floors of the John Hancock Center can sometimes see different weather at the same time.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Frank Lloyd Wright built a 528-floor skyscraper near Lake Michigan in 1956.


### 2. Reading

If someone asked you where the skyscraper was born, what would you answer? Most people would probably say New York City, but they would be wrong. Chicago was the birthplace of the skyscraper.

Dictionaries define a skyscraper as "a building of great height which is constructed on a steel skeleton." The first building to fit that description was the Home Insurance Company Building. It was built in Chicago in 1884. It was 10 storeys, i.e. floors, high - a great height for that time. It had a strong framework (structure) of iron and steel instead of walls of stone to support it.

The Home Insurance Company Building does not exist any more, they pulled it down in 1931, but visitors to the city can still see other early Chicago skyscrapers. One of them is the 16-storey Reliance Building, which was completed in 1894. The Reliance Building had windows that, for the first time, covered almost the entire surface.

For many years, Chicago was behind New York in the construction of skyscrapers. It got back into the competition with buildings like the John Hancock Center, built in 1968. There are luxury flats on forty-nine of the Center's 100 floors. Sometimes people who live on a high floor look out on a sunny sky while those on the downstairs floors can watch the rain from their windows.

Architects and engineers have the technology to build even taller structures, but to do this, they must find the money for them and these new skyscrapers should not harm the environment.

Back in 1956, the famous architect Frank Lloyd Wright dreamed of a skyscraper of 528 floors. He planned to build it near the shore of Lake Michigan in Chicago. Wright's dream never became a reality, but who knows? Someday somebody may build his tower in the city where the skyscraper was born.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A*

1. In "visitors to the city can still see other early Chicago skyscrapers. One of them is the 16-storey Reliance Building", 'them' refers to ____.

   - a) visitors who come to Chicago

   - b) the Home Insurance Company Building and the Reliance Building

   - c) skyscrapers which were completed in 1894

   - d) the other early skyscrapers in Chicago

2. The Home Insurance Company Building ____.

   - a) was the first skyscraper in the U.S.A.

   - b) is a place which tourists still visit

   - c) was a high building of 100 floors

   - d) was built in 1984

3. People who live on different floors of the John Hancock Center ____.

   - a) moved into the flats before 1968

   - b) can experience different weather conditions

   - c) live in forty-nine luxury flats

   - d) don't have a chance to enjoy the sun


*B*

4. The Home Insurance Company Building had a framework which consisted of stone walls.

   - True

   - False

5. As well as technology, financial and environmental factors are also important in the construction of skyscrapers.

   - True

   - False

6. F.L. Wright's 528-floor skyscraper was built near Lake Michigan.

   - True

   - False

7. In the last paragraph, 'the city where the skyscraper was born' refers to Chicago.

   - True

   - False


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Detail]* What structural feature made the Home Insurance Company Building qualify as the first skyscraper?

   - A) Using windows that covered only a small area

   - B) Locating buildings directly on the shore of a lake

   - C) Supporting the building with an iron and steel framework instead of stone walls

   - D) Putting luxury flats on at least half of the floors

2. *[Author's Purpose]* Why does the author start the passage with a question about New York City and Chicago?

   - A) To correct a common misconception about where skyscrapers originated

   - B) To argue that New York buildings are less attractive than Chicago's

   - C) To explain why Chicago stopped building tall towers in 1931

   - D) To compare ticket prices for tourists in both cities

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In paragraph 3, what does "pulled it down" mean?

   - A) Moved it to another city

   - B) Demolished or tore it down

   - C) Repaired its iron skeleton

   - D) Closed its top floors to visitors


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — The Birth and Growth of Skyscrapers** (60-80 words): Summarize the history and development of skyscrapers in Chicago based on the passage.

- **Task 2 — Should Cities Build Taller Skyscrapers?** (120-150 words): Do you think modern cities should continue building taller and taller skyscrapers? Discuss the advantages and disadvantages.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **steel skeleton** (*celik iskelet*): The tower is built around a strong steel skeleton.

- **pull down** (*yikmak (bina)*): They pulled down the old factory to build a park.

- **framework** (*iskelet, yapi catisi*): The building's iron framework supports all twenty floors.

- **entire surface** (*tum yuzey*): Glass panels cover the entire surface of the modern museum.

- **harm the environment** (*cevreye zarar vermek*): Factories must use clean energy so they do not harm the environment.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Gokdelenlerin dogdugu sehri ve ilk orneklerin ozelliklerini dogru ozetledim mi?

- [ ] Tarihsel olaylari anlatirken edilgen yapiyi ('was built', 'was completed', 'was pulled down') dogru kullandim mi?

- [ ] Gorus yazimda gokdelenlerin hem avantajlarina hem de cevresel/mali zorluklarina degindim mi?

- [ ] Paragraf duzenine, noktalama isaretlerine ve kelime secimine dikkat ettim mi?

## Passage 95: Temperate Climates

- **ID:** `raw1_095` | **Seviye:** `B1` | **Konu:** Climate & Geography | **Sayfa:** 136, 137
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_095_temperate_climates.mp3` (*US · en-US-JennyNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. In warm temperate zones, plants can grow all year round because the average temperature does not drop below ____. *(İpucu: temperature)*

2. The eastern or China type of warm temperate climate is influenced by ____ and is wetter. *(İpucu: plural noun)*

3. A cool temperate region with frequent changes in weather conditions is known as a ____. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. What characterizes the Mediterranean type of warm temperate climate?

   - A) It has humid summers and strong winds.

   - B) It has dry, hot summers and mild, wet winters.

   - C) It has five months below 6 degrees Celsius.

   - D) It is found only in eastern Asia.

2. For how long can temperatures drop below 6 degrees Celsius in a cool temperate zone?

   - A) Every month of the year

   - B) Only one coldest month

   - C) Up to five months a year

   - D) At least nine months a year

3. What kind of seasons does a continental region have?

   - A) Warmer summers and cold winters

   - B) Wet summers and mild winters

   - C) Constant rain throughout the year

   - D) Frequent daily weather shifts


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Temperate climates avoid extreme heat and extreme cold.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Most of the world's population lives in warm temperate zones.


### 2. Reading

There are many different types of climate in the world. This article discusses the various types of temperate climates, that is, the climates that are free from very high and very low temperatures.

The world's temperate regions are divided into warm and cool zones. In the warm temperate zones, the average temperature does not drop below 6°C in the coldest month of the year. This is the lowest possible temperature necessary for growing plants all year round. There are two different kinds of warm temperate regions. One is the western or Mediterranean type (dry, hot summers and mild, wet winters) found on the western coasts and across southern Europe. The other kind is called the eastern or China type. This kind of climate is influenced by winds and is wetter, with mild winters and hot, humid summers.

The cool temperate zones, on the other hand, have a cold season. There are as many as five months a year when the average temperature goes below 6°C. There are two different kinds of cool temperate zones. One type is called a maritime zone, with frequent changes in weather conditions. The other is called a continental region, with warmer summers and cold winters.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A*

1. What does 'This' refer to in "This is the lowest possible temperature necessary for growing plants all year round"?

2. What does 'This kind of climate' refer to in "This kind of climate is influenced by winds and is wetter"?


*B*

3. Complete the chart about the types of temperate climates by naming the two main zones and the two subtypes under each.


*C*

4. What is a temperate climate?

5. Where do we find the western (or Mediterranean) type of warm temperate climate?

6. How many months in each year can the average temperature drop below 6°C in a cool temperate zone?

7. Which cool temperate zone has frequent changes in weather conditions?

8. Describe the conditions in the continental type of cool temperate zone.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the main purpose of the passage?

   - A) To explain how winds cause extreme weather events

   - B) To describe how plants survive freezing winter months

   - C) To classify and describe the main types of temperate climates

   - D) To compare southern European farming with Chinese agriculture

2. *[Inference]* What can be inferred about plant growth in cool temperate zones?

   - A) Cool temperate zones receive too much summer rain for farming.

   - B) Plants cannot grow all year round in cool temperate zones.

   - C) Maritime zones are always warmer than Mediterranean zones.

   - D) Continental regions have milder winters than China-type climates.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* The word 'humid' in paragraph 2 is closest in meaning to ____.

   - A) extremely cold

   - B) completely dry

   - C) very windy

   - D) damp and moist


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Summarizing Temperate Climate Zones** (75-95 words): Summarize the main divisions of temperate climates and their key characteristics in your own words.

- **Task 2 — Choosing Your Ideal Temperate Climate** (140-170 words): Which type of temperate climate described in the passage would you prefer to live in, and why? Support your opinion with reasons and details.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **temperate** (*ılıman*): Temperate climates are ideal for farming because they avoid extreme heat and cold.

- **average temperature** (*ortalama sıcaklık*): The average temperature in this coastal city rarely drops below freezing.

- **maritime** (*denizel, denize özgü*): Coastal areas have a maritime climate with frequent rain and changing winds.

- **be influenced by** (*... tarafından etkilenmek*): Our local climate is heavily influenced by warm ocean currents.

- **humid** (*nemli, rutubetli*): Tourists often find the humid summer weather exhausting.

- **all year round** (*yıl boyunca, bütün yıl*): Fresh herbs can be grown in a greenhouse all year round.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Ilıman iklimlerin temel özelliğini ve ana gruplarını kendi cümlelerimle özetledim.

- [ ] Sıcak ve soğuk ılıman kuşakların alt türlerini (Akdeniz, Çin, denizel, karasal) doğru eşleştirdim.

- [ ] Paragraf bütünlüğünü koruyarak geçiş bağlaçlarını (on the other hand, while, for example) uygun biçimde kullandım.

- [ ] Dil bilgisi, noktalama ve hedef kelime sayısı aralığını kontrol ettim.

## Passage 96: The Pen

- **ID:** `raw1_096` | **Seviye:** `B1` | **Konu:** History of Writing | **Sayfa:** 138, 139
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_096_the_pen.mp3` (*AU · en-AU-NatashaNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. The stylus was a sharp writing tool made of ____ that left marks on stone or wood. *(İpucu: materials)*

2. The Egyptians invented a pen with ink by sharpening one end of a ____ stem. *(İpucu: noun)*

3. In ____, an American named Lewis Waterman completed the fountain pen by putting ink inside the holder. *(İpucu: year)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Where does the word 'pen' come from?

   - A) The Greek word for ink

   - B) The Egyptian word for bamboo

   - C) The Latin word for feather

   - D) The French word for metal nib

2. What was the main drawback of the quill pen?

   - A) It needed to be sharpened quite often.

   - B) It could not hold enough ink inside.

   - C) It only worked on stone and wood surfaces.

   - D) It was too expensive for ordinary writers.

3. Which writing tool was introduced in 1943 and became even more popular than the fountain pen?

   - A) A machine-made metal nib

   - B) The Egyptian bamboo stem pen

   - C) Lewis Waterman's fountain pen

   - D) Laszlo Biro's ball-point pen


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — People used the stylus for thousands of years before ink pens replaced it.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — The metal nib had to be sharpened as frequently as a bird's feather.


### 2. Reading

Man has never been satisfied with simply talking. For some reason he has always considered his ideas to be so important that he has always tried to find ways to record them on paper. Man has developed many systems of writing; he also developed various tools with which to write.

One of the first instruments developed by man for writing was the stylus. It was a sharp instrument made of bone or metal, and with it the writer could make marks on stone or wood. It was used for thousands of years and was very practical.

The idea of using pen and ink was developed by the Egyptians. Their version of the pen was made from bamboo. They sharpened one end of the stem (or the body) to make a nib, that is, the point which you write with. Then they filled the empty part of the stem with ink and forced the ink onto the nib. It was a good idea, but when paper was later introduced, a much finer instrument was needed.

The problem was solved with the introduction of the quill pen. The quill is a large feather from the wing or tail of a bird. The quill also gave us the word 'pen' - feather in Latin is penna. Because this pen was a feather, however, it had to be sharpened quite often. A special instrument was made for this purpose, the penknife.

The quill was used from the 6th through the 18th century, but in the 19th century a machine was invented which successfully made a nib out of metal and which, thus, solved the problem of sharpening. The nib could be fitted into a holder and dipped into a bottle of ink. Finally, in 1884, a young American, Lewis Waterman, found a way of putting the ink into the holder, and the fountain pen as we know it was complete.

Although the fountain pen was very popular, a more popular kind of pen was introduced in 1943. This was the ball-point pen, invented by Laszlo Biro, an Argentinian.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*Mark the best choice*

1. What does 'them' refer to in "he has always tried to find ways to record them on paper"?

   - a) men

   - b) ways

   - c) ideas

   - d) systems

2. The quill pen was better than the stylus because people could

   - a) sharpen it frequently

   - b) write finely on paper

   - c) force ink into the nib

   - d) make clear signs on stone

3. The penknife was first used to

   - a) cut bamboo

   - b) cut feathers off a bird

   - c) fill ink into the quill

   - d) keep the end of the quill sharp

4. A nib made from metal was an advantage because

   - a) it was used for many centuries

   - b) the pens couldn't be filled with ink

   - c) it didn't need to be sharpened

   - d) people could fit it into a bottle of ink

5. The fountain pen was completed when

   - a) a metal nib was made

   - b) the nib was fitted into a holder

   - c) the nib was dipped into ink

   - d) the ink was put into the holder

6. Which statement is correct?

   - a) Using a stylus, man could write on paper.

   - b) With a pen made from bamboo, man could use ink.

   - c) The fountain pen was developed in the 6th century.

   - d) The quill pen was developed by Egyptians.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the passage mainly about?

   - A) How ancient Egyptians learned to manufacture paper

   - B) How writing tools evolved over time to meet new needs

   - C) Why Lewis Waterman became a famous businessman

   - D) How Latin words entered modern European languages

2. *[Inference]* What general pattern is shown in the history of the pen?

   - A) Writing tools rarely changed after the 6th century.

   - B) People stopped using ink once metal nibs were invented.

   - C) New writing tools were created to overcome the limits of older ones.

   - D) The ball-point pen was the first tool used on paper.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* The word 'practical' in paragraph 2 is closest in meaning to ____.

   - A) useful and effective

   - B) fragile and delicate

   - C) rare and expensive

   - D) slow and difficult


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Tracing the Evolution of the Pen** (80-100 words): Summarize the historical development of the pen from the stylus to the ball-point pen.

- **Task 2 — The Value of Handwriting in a Digital Age** (140-170 words): Today most people type on phones and computers instead of writing by hand. Do you think handwriting with a pen is still important in the modern world? Give reasons for your view.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **record** (*kaydetmek, yazıya geçirmek*): Early writers used sharp tools to record their stories for future generations.

- **practical** (*kullanışlı, pratik*): Carry a small notebook in your pocket—it is very practical during trips.

- **nib** (*kalem ucu*): Always dip the nib gently into the ink bottle to avoid spills.

- **sharpen** (*sivriltmek, açmak*): Artists must sharpen their pencils carefully before sketching fine details.

- **solve the problem** (*sorunu çözmek*): The invention of the printing press solved the problem of slow book copying.

- **dip into** (*batırmak, daldırmak*): She dipped the brush into blue paint before touching the canvas.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Yazı araçlarının tarihsel sırasını (stylus, bambu kalem, tüy kalem, dolma kalem, tükenmez kalem) doğru aktardım.

- [ ] Her icadın önceki aracın hangi sorununu çözdüğünü açıkça belirttim.

- [ ] Geçmiş zaman kiplerini ve edilgen yapıları (was invented, was used) doğru kullandım.

- [ ] Hedef kelime sayısına uyarak yazım ve noktalama hatalarını düzelttim.
