## Passage 97: Popular vs. Quality Newspapers

- **ID:** `raw1_097` | **Seviye:** `B1` | **Konu:** Media & Language Learning | **Sayfa:** 140, 141
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_097_popular_vs_quality_newspapers.mp3` (*UK · en-GB-SoniaNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. Editors of popular newspapers try to catch readers' attention with ____ in big black type. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*

2. Foreign readers are frequently confused by popular newspaper headlines because they use ____. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*

3. Because editors fear readers will not be patient enough for a ____, almost every sentence becomes a separate paragraph. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. What surprises many students of English as a foreign language about English newspapers?

   - A) Quality newspapers do not use any headlines.

   - B) Popular newspapers are written strictly for language teachers.

   - C) Popular newspapers are often more confusing for learners than quality ones.

   - D) Quality newspapers have one-sentence paragraphs.

2. Why do editors design puzzling headlines in popular newspapers?

   - A) To give a complete summary of the news story

   - B) To make readers curious enough to read the article

   - C) To help foreign students learn formal grammar

   - D) To save space on the front page

3. Why do editors make almost every sentence a separate paragraph in popular newspapers?

   - A) They assume readers lack the patience for long paragraphs.

   - B) They want to make the newspaper look longer than it is.

   - C) They want to teach foreign readers how to write short sentences.

   - D) They do not have enough news stories to fill each page.


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Readers of quality newspapers generally enjoy reading more than readers of popular newspapers.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Quality newspapers usually cost more money than popular newspapers.


### 2. Reading

Students of English as a foreign language often ask teachers which newspaper to read. They naturally suppose that reading an English newspaper will be a good way of improving their knowledge of the language. But they are frequently disappointed because they cannot understand popular newspapers and think the 'quality' newspapers will be even more difficult.

In fact, the opposite is usually the case. Popular newspapers are written for people who haven't got much time for reading and who, perhaps, do not read as fast or enjoy reading as much as people who buy 'quality' newspapers. So, the editors try to attract the readers' attention with sensational headlines in big black type. The headlines do not explain the content of the news item underneath. On the contrary, they are often designed to puzzle the reader so that he will be persuaded to read the item to find out the meaning of the headline! Apart from that, they use double meanings, so a foreign reader is usually confused by them. Editors are also afraid that the readers will not be patient enough to read a long paragraph. Therefore, almost every sentence becomes a paragraph in itself, which is also a disadvantage for the student of English as a foreign language.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*Mark the best choice*

1. Students of English as a foreign language want to read English newspapers because

   - a) they are disappointed in the difficulty of newspapers

   - b) they don't have much time to read

   - c) they think they will learn the language better in this way

   - d) None of the above is correct.

2. In the passage, "In fact, the opposite is usually the case" means

   - a) quality newspapers are less difficult to read than popular newspapers

   - b) students can read popular newspapers easily

   - c) people who read popular newspapers have a lot of time for reading

   - d) popular newspapers aren't written for people who do not have time for reading

3. Editors think that people who read popular newspapers

   - a) do not have much time to read

   - b) do not enjoy reading very much

   - c) cannot read very fast

   - d) All of the above are correct.

4. What does 'sensational' probably mean in "try to attract the readers' attention with sensational headlines in big black type"?

   - a) beautiful

   - b) interesting

   - c) serious

   - d) huge

5. What does 'they' refer to in "On the contrary, they are often designed to puzzle the reader"?

   - a) the readers

   - b) the editors

   - c) the headlines

   - d) the news items

6. What does 'they' refer to in "Apart from that, they use double meanings, so a foreign reader is usually confused by them"?

   - a) the readers

   - b) the editors

   - c) the headlines

   - d) the news items

7. Which of the following is not a disadvantage of a newspaper?

   - a) The sentences are long.

   - b) The headlines do not explain the content of the news item underneath.

   - c) Foreign readers are confused by the headlines.

   - d) The headlines are big and black.


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Author's Purpose]* What is the author's main purpose in writing this passage?

   - A) To advertise a specific British quality newspaper

   - B) To criticize students who do not read fast enough

   - C) To show how editors write long academic paragraphs

   - D) To explain why popular newspapers are harder for language learners than expected

2. *[Inference]* What can be inferred about 'quality' newspapers from the passage?

   - A) Quality newspapers have clearer headlines and more connected paragraphs.

   - B) Quality newspapers avoid writing about serious news topics.

   - C) Quality newspapers are intended for people who dislike reading.

   - D) Quality newspapers use larger type and shorter paragraphs than popular ones.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* The word 'persuaded' in paragraph 2 is closest in meaning to ____.

   - A) forced against his will

   - B) prevented from continuing

   - C) convinced or encouraged

   - D) warned to be careful


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Summarizing Challenges of Popular Newspapers** (70-90 words): Summarize why popular newspapers can be more difficult for English learners than quality newspapers.

- **Task 2 — Advice on Reading English News for Learners** (140-170 words): What advice would you give to a friend learning English about choosing and reading English news sources? Explain your recommendations.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **sensational** (*sansasyonel, dikkat çekici*): The magazine used sensational headlines on its cover to increase sales.

- **attract attention** (*dikkatini çekmek*): Bright posters were placed near the entrance to attract students' attention.

- **double meaning** (*çift anlam, kinaye*): The clever advertisement used a double meaning to make people laugh.

- **patient enough** (*yeterince sabırlı olmak*): Learning to be patient enough to read whole chapters takes regular practice.

- **disadvantage** (*dezavantaj, sakınca*): Lack of practice is a major disadvantage when learning to speak a new language.

- **be persuaded** (*ikna edilmek*): She was finally persuaded to join the school reading club.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Öğrencilerin popüler ve nitelikli gazeteler hakkındaki yanlış beklentisini açıkça özetledim.

- [ ] Popüler gazetelerin yabancı dil öğrencileri için oluşturduğu üç temel zorluğu (kafa karıştırıcı başlıklar, çift anlamlar, tek cümlelik paragraflar) belirttim.

- [ ] Neden-sonuç bağlaçlarını (because, therefore, so that) doğru ve akıcı kullandım.

- [ ] Kelime sayısını ve İngilizce dil bilgisi doğruluğunu kontrol ettim.

## Passage 98: Acid Rain (1)

- **ID:** `raw1_098` | **Seviye:** `B1` | **Konu:** Environment & Pollution | **Sayfa:** 142
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_098_acid_rain_1.mp3` (*US · en-US-GuyNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. Burning coal or oil releases ____ that are carried up into the atmosphere. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*

2. Over ____ research projects have been conducted to investigate acid rain. *(İpucu: number)*

3. Scandinavia and Central Europe started a diplomatic campaign to show that acid rain is a major ____ threat. *(İpucu: adjective)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Where have measures already been taken to stop pollution before it reaches the environment?

   - A) Western European research institutes

   - B) Eastern European industrial cities

   - C) Scandinavia and Central Europe

   - D) North American environmental groups

2. What is currently forcing politicians to take action against acid rain?

   - A) A drop in coal and oil supplies

   - B) Reported damage to large areas of forest and lakeland

   - C) Pressure from international diplomats

   - D) Public protests in Western European capitals

3. What is expected to happen in the near future?

   - A) A complete ban on burning coal and oil

   - B) A decrease in European research projects

   - C) New taxes on energy companies

   - D) A major international initiative to fight acid rain


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Acid rain was already treated as a serious issue five years ago.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Governments could be blamed within a few years for the death of forests if they fail to act.


### 2. Reading

Acid rain is caused by burning coal or oil. When either fuel is burned, it releases poisonous gases which are carried up into the atmosphere and sometimes transported long distances.

Over 3,000 research projects have been carried out to look into acid rain, and a decision to tackle the problem has been taken in most of the Western European countries. Measures have been taken in Scandinavia and in Central Europe to stop the pollution before it is dumped on the environment, and a diplomatic campaign has been launched to convince the other countries that the problem has to be considered as a major ecological threat.

"Five years ago this issue was not being treated seriously," says one leading environmental group, "but now that damage has been reported in large areas of forest and lakeland, our politicians are being forced to take action. This problem must be solved quickly: if governments do nothing, they will be faced in two or three years' time with the accusation that they have allowed our forests to die." A major international initiative to combat acid rain is expected in the near future.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A. Reference*

1. What does 'either fuel' refer to in "When either fuel is burned, it releases poisonous gases"?

2. What does 'which' refer to in "it releases poisonous gases which are carried up into the atmosphere"?

3. What does 'it' refer to in "to stop the pollution before it is dumped on the environment"?


*B. Comprehension Questions*

4. What causes acid rain?

5. What is the aim (purpose) of the diplomatic campaign which started in Scandinavia and Central Europe?

6. What are politicians forced to do and why?

7. Which problem must be solved quickly?

8. If the governments don't do anything, what will they be accused of doing?

9. What is expected in the near future?


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Main Idea]* What is the passage mainly about?

   - A) The economic benefits of coal and oil production in Europe

   - B) The causes of acid rain and growing international efforts to combat it

   - C) How Scandinavian forests recover naturally from air pollution

   - D) Why research projects have failed to explain acid rain

2. *[Inference]* Why did Scandinavia and Central Europe launch a diplomatic campaign to convince other countries?

   - A) Most countries do not have enough money to fund research projects

   - B) Western European governments refuse to share scientific data

   - C) Air pollution travels across borders, so international cooperation is needed

   - D) Environmental groups only operate in Scandinavia and Central Europe

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In paragraph 2, the word "tackle" in "a decision to tackle the problem" is closest in meaning to:

   - A) address and deal with

   - B) ignore completely

   - C) postpone until later

   - D) measure scientifically


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Summary: Causes and Responses to Acid Rain** (65-85 words): Summarize the causes of acid rain and how European countries are responding to the problem based on the passage.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: International Action on Pollution** (90-120 words): Do you think international cooperation is necessary to solve environmental problems like acid rain? Explain your opinion with reasons.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **poisonous gases** (*zehirli gazlar*): Burning fossil fuels releases poisonous gases into the air.

- **tackle the problem** (*sorunu ele almak, çözmeye çalışmak*): Governments must work together to tackle the problem of climate change.

- **diplomatic campaign** (*diplomatik kampanya*): The country launched a diplomatic campaign to gain regional support.

- **major ecological threat** (*büyük ekolojik tehdit*): Plastic waste in the oceans has become a major ecological threat.

- **take action** (*harekete geçmek*): Local leaders are being forced to take action after the recent floods.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Metindeki ana nedenleri ve Avrupa'daki girişimleri kendi cümlelerimle özetledim.

- [ ] Paragraflar arasında mantıklı geçiş ve bağlaçlar kullandım.

- [ ] Çevre ve kirlilikle ilgili temel sözcükleri doğru bağlamda kullandım.

- [ ] Dilbilgisi, zaman uyumu ve noktalama işaretlerini kontrol ettim.

## Passage 99: Acid Rain (2)

- **ID:** `raw1_099` | **Seviye:** `B1` | **Konu:** Environment & Energy | **Sayfa:** 143
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_099_acid_rain_2.mp3` (*US · en-US-AriaNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. Power plants that burn ____ to generate electricity are the main cause of acid rain. *(İpucu: noun)*

2. Acid rain can also contaminate ____, making it a danger to human health. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*

3. Developing the special ____ needed to cut power plant pollution will be very costly. *(İpucu: noun)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. How does acid rain cause fish to die?

   - A) It blocks sunlight from reaching the water

   - B) It heats up rivers and lakes dangerously

   - C) It increases the amount of acid in the water

   - D) It dries up lakes in industrial countries

2. According to environmentalists, what is the real reason governments delay passing laws against acid rain?

   - A) Economic costs

   - B) Lack of scientific evidence

   - C) Public opposition to electricity cuts

   - D) Shortages of coal supplies

3. Which regions are mentioned as already having contaminated lakes?

   - A) Germany, France, and Spain

   - B) China, Japan, and South Korea

   - C) Brazil, Argentina, and Chile

   - D) Canada, the northeastern United States, and Norway


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Most environmentalists agree with governments that the causes of acid rain remain unclear.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Acid rain can put human health at risk by polluting drinking water.


### 2. Reading

According to some scientists, one of the most serious problems for the environment is acid rain. Acid rain is caused mainly by power plants that burn coal to produce electricity. The smoke from these power plants contains acidic substances which later fall back to earth in rain or snow. As a result, the amount of acid in lakes and rivers increases, causing the fish to die. Already many lakes in Canada, the northeastern U.S., and Norway are contaminated by pollution from power plants. Scientists are also warning that acid rain can damage plants and trees and lead to the contamination of drinking water. It is, therefore, a danger to human health.

Many governments do not want to pass laws that will reduce the pollution which causes acid rain. They say that the causes of acid rain are still not completely clear. However, it is clear to the majority of environmentalists that economics is the real reason why governments do not take immediate action.

In most industrial countries, the majority of power plants use coal. It will be extremely expensive to develop the special equipment that is needed to reduce the pollution from these power plants.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A. Mark the best choice*

1. The main idea of the passage is that ____.

   - a) although acid rain is a serious problem for the environment, governments have still not taken the necessary steps to reduce it

   - b) many power plants burn coal to produce electricity and this increases the amount of acid in rivers and lakes

   - c) acid rain, which is a serious problem for the environment, has polluted many lakes in Canada, Norway and the United States

   - d) many governments will pass laws to reduce acid rain when its causes become clear


*B. True or False*

2. Power plants which burn coal to produce electricity pollute the environment.

   - True

   - False

3. Most environmentalists say that they do not really know the causes of acid rain.

   - True

   - False

4. It will not be cheap to reduce pollution from power plants.

   - True

   - False


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Author's Purpose]* Why does the author include both the governments' claim and the environmentalists' view in paragraph 2?

   - A) To describe how electricity is distributed in industrial cities

   - B) To contrast governments' stated excuse with what environmentalists believe is the real motive

   - C) To prove that coal is cheaper than all renewable energy sources

   - D) To show that scientists disagree about whether fish are dying in lakes

2. *[Inference]* What can be inferred about what would happen if industrial countries required special anti-pollution equipment in coal plants?

   - A) Power plants would produce too much electricity

   - B) Governments would have to close all rivers and lakes

   - C) Scientists would stop studying acid rain

   - D) Power companies and governments would face very high expenses

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In paragraph 1, the word "contaminated" is closest in meaning to:

   - A) protected

   - B) emptied

   - C) polluted

   - D) explored


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Summary: Effects of Acid Rain and Government Inaction** (65-85 words): Write a short summary explaining the effects of acid rain and why governments are reluctant to pass laws against it.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: Cost vs. Environmental Protection** (90-120 words): Should governments pass strict laws to reduce power plant pollution even if it is very expensive? Give reasons for your view.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **power plant** (*enerji santrali, elektrik santrali*): Coal-fired power plants supply electricity to millions of homes.

- **acidic substances** (*asidik maddeler*): Factory smoke often contains acidic substances that harm crops.

- **be contaminated by** (*kirlenmek, bulaşmak*): Several rivers were contaminated by chemical waste from nearby factories.

- **pass laws** (*yasa çıkarmak*): City officials decided to pass laws limiting vehicle emissions.

- **immediate action** (*acil önlem / hemen harekete geçme*): Doctors warned that immediate action is needed to prevent disease.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Asit yağmurunun doğaya ve insan sağlığına etkilerini eksiksiz özetledim.

- [ ] Hükümetlerin yasa çıkarmama gerekçesini ve çevrecilerin görüşünü karşılaştırdım.

- [ ] Akademik ve açık bir dil kullanarak yazım kurallarına dikkat ettim.

- [ ] Kelime sayısı hedefi içinde kaldım.

## Passage 100: The Kon-Tiki Voyage, 1947

- **ID:** `raw1_100` | **Seviye:** `A2` | **Konu:** Exploration & History | **Sayfa:** 144, 145
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_100_the_kon_tiki_voyage_1947.mp3` (*UK · en-GB-RyanNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. Easter Island is famous for its giant heads made of ____. *(İpucu: noun)*

2. Heyerdahl and ____ friends built a wooden raft modeled after those of ancient Peru. *(İpucu: number)*

3. The explorers reached Polynesia after ____ hard days at sea. *(İpucu: number)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Why did nobody believe Thor Heyerdahl's theory at first?

   - A) He had no experience sailing on the ocean

   - B) People knew ancient Peruvians only had wooden rafts

   - C) Easter Island was too close to the mainland

   - D) The Peru Current moved in the wrong direction

2. What difficulties did the crew face during their voyage across the Pacific?

   - A) Getting lost near the mainland

   - B) Running out of wooden materials

   - C) Bad weather and sharks

   - D) Arguments among the crew members

3. After whom or what was the raft Kon-Tiki named?

   - A) An ancient god

   - B) A Peruvian king

   - C) A Norwegian explorer

   - D) An ocean current


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Thor Heyerdahl was an explorer from ancient Peru.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Heyerdahl and his friends carefully planned their voyage before setting sail.


### 2. Reading

In the middle of the Pacific Ocean, thousands of miles from the nearest mainland, lie the islands of Polynesia. On some of the islands, there are ancient roads and buildings. These show that there have been ancient civilizations on the islands. The most mysterious of the Polynesian Islands is Easter Island, where there are giant heads made of stone. Some of them are as tall as a four-storey building. These are very old. Obviously men have been living on these islands for a long, long time. But how did the islanders first get to the islands so long ago, and how did they do it when there weren't any big ships? These are some of the questions which are difficult to answer.

Thor Heyerdahl, a Norwegian explorer, thought he had the answer to the mystery. He believed the people from Ancient Peru sailed more than 6,000 kilometres across the Pacific Ocean on their small rafts to start a new life on the islands of Polynesia.

Nobody believed Heyerdahl because they knew that the people of ancient Peru only had wooden rafts to sail on. How did the people sail from Peru to the islands? Heyerdahl wanted to show everybody that his theory was right. Together with four friends, he started to build a raft like those of the ancient Peruvians. When it was finished they called it Kon-Tiki, the name of an ancient god. They spent a long time planning their journey and deciding what to take with them.

The natural movement of the water (a current called the Peru Current) carried them west. Although they had lots of problems during their journey with the weather and sharks, the boat was fine—it didn't sink. After 102 hard days at sea, the explorers arrived on one of the Polynesian Islands. Heyerdahl proved it was possible.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*Reference & Comprehension*

1. What does 'These' refer to in "These show that there have been ancient civilizations on the islands"?

   - a) the roads

   - b) the ancient roads

   - c) the ancient roads and buildings

   - d) the buildings

2. What does 'them' refer to in "Some of them are as tall as a four-storey building"?

   - a) the Polynesian Islands

   - b) the giant stones

   - c) the islands

   - d) the giant heads made of stone

3. What does 'those' refer to in "he started to build a raft like those of the ancient Peruvians"?

   - a) the rafts

   - b) the friends

   - c) the ancient Peruvians

   - d) the islands

4. What does 'it' refer to in "Heyerdahl proved it was possible"?

   - a) building a raft that doesn't sink in stormy weather

   - b) the Polynesian Islands

   - c) sailing from Peru to the Polynesian Islands on a raft

   - d) spending 102 days at sea

5. Easter Island ____.

   - a) is in the Pacific Ocean

   - b) is part of the mainland

   - c) is not a Polynesian Island

   - d) has got many four-storey houses

6. Explorers know that people have been living on the Polynesian Islands for a very long time because ____.

   - a) people travelled there from Peru on rafts years ago

   - b) they have found ancient roads and buildings on these islands

   - c) the stone heads on Easter Island are very tall

   - d) all of the above

7. Heyerdahl believed that the first people who arrived on the islands ____.

   - a) went there after big ships were invented

   - b) were Polynesian

   - c) were originally from Easter Island

   - d) travelled there to start a new life

8. Kon-Tiki was the name of ____.

   - a) an ancient god

   - b) an island

   - c) a modern ship

   - d) the ancient Peruvians

9. Heyerdahl was able to arrive on the islands because ____.

   - a) they were not as far away from the mainland as he thought

   - b) the Peru Current carried the raft west towards the islands

   - c) the weather was fine

   - d) he had planned the journey very well


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Detail]* Why did Thor Heyerdahl decide to sail from Peru to Polynesia on a wooden raft?

   - A) To find gold hidden on Easter Island

   - B) To discover a new ocean current near Peru

   - C) To prove that ancient Peruvians could have sailed to Polynesia on rafts

   - D) To break a speed record across the Pacific Ocean

2. *[Inference]* Which words best describe Thor Heyerdahl based on the passage?

   - A) determined and adventurous

   - B) fearful and hesitant

   - C) careless and unprepared

   - D) dishonest and secretive

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In paragraph 4, what does the word "current" mean?

   - A) a wooden ship

   - B) a strong storm

   - C) an ancient map

   - D) a steady flow of water


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Guided Summary: The Kon-Tiki Expedition** (60-80 words): Write a short summary of Thor Heyerdahl's theory and how he tested it during the Kon-Tiki voyage.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: Would You Join an Expedition?** (80-110 words): Would you like to join a dangerous sea voyage like the Kon-Tiki expedition to test a historical theory? Why or why not?


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **ancient civilizations** (*eski uygarlıklar*): Archaeologists found evidence of ancient civilizations in the desert.

- **mysterious** (*gizemli*): The island is famous for its mysterious stone statues.

- **wooden raft** (*tahta sal*): They crossed the river on a small wooden raft.

- **theory** (*kuram, teori*): She wanted to test her theory through a real experiment.

- **ocean current** (*okyanus akıntısı*): A strong ocean current pushed the small boat toward the shore.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Heyerdahl'ın teorisini ve yolculuğunun sonucunu doğru aktardım.

- [ ] Geçmiş zaman (Simple Past) fiillerini doğru biçimde kullandım.

- [ ] Metindeki temel olayları mantıklı bir sırayla yazdım.

- [ ] Kelime sayısı sınırına uygun bir paragraf oluşturdum.

## Passage 101: U.S. Cities: Past and Present

- **ID:** `raw1_101` | **Seviye:** `B1` | **Konu:** Urban History & Problems | **Sayfa:** 146, 147, 148
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_101_u_s_cities_past_and_present.mp3` (*US · en-US-JennyNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. In the 19th century, American cities had a lot of pollution from ____ and other animals. *(İpucu: noun (plural))*

2. Dirty living conditions in 19th-century cities made ____ diseases like smallpox and cholera very dangerous. *(İpucu: adjective)*

3. Skilled ____ were able to quickly steal people's purses and wallets and run away. *(İpucu: noun (plural))*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. What problem did city governments face regarding water supplies in the last century?

   - A) They did not know that their water was polluted at all.

   - B) They knew water was polluted, but not how the pollution happened.

   - C) They refused to spend money on modern sewer pipes.

   - D) They blamed real estate developers for poisoning the rivers.

2. Why were cities and neighbourhoods poorly planned?

   - A) They lacked the materials needed to build durable homes.

   - B) They were strictly controlled by local city governments.

   - C) They cared only about earning more money.

   - D) They spent most of their funds on repairing old roads.

3. What commonly killed or seriously injured people in 19th-century city streets?

   - A) Runaway horses out of control

   - B) Careless butchers driving carts

   - C) Early automobiles without brakes

   - D) Crowded streetcars in narrow alleys


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Many children died in 19th-century American cities because of dirt and infectious diseases.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — The speaker believes it is impossible to improve the current problems of American cities.


### 2. Reading

The problems of the American cities - pollution, crime, a lack of planning, accidents - are bad, but they were worse in the so-called 'good old days'. Pollution from cars is one example. It is bad now but there was a lot of pollution from horses and other animals in American cities in the 19th century.

Most cities had their own slaughterhouses. Butchers were careless about the cows and pigs which they killed and they left the remains of the animals here and there. And the sewer system for waste water from the kitchens and bathrooms did almost not exist.

Pollution was a very important problem for city governments in the last century, especially the problem of water supplies. They knew that they polluted water, but they didn't know how it was polluted. Trying to get pure water was a big problem.

People like to talk about the good old days but, actually, the cities of the 19th century were dirtier than today. Because of the dirt, communicable diseases were a great problem. When one of these diseases was seen, the situation was dangerous for all of the people in that area. Examples of such diseases were smallpox, yellow fever, malaria, cholera and typhoid. The number of children who died was high.

Some of the problems that disturbed people who lived in the cities during that period are still with us - poor planning, crime and accidents. The planning of cities and their neighbourhoods was controlled by real estate developers who were only interested in earning more money. Actually, their real job was to build well-planned cities and provide the population with both durable housing and a good-looking neighbourhood.

Crime was a great concern then, too. The police had to work hard to stop it. Pickpockets, who were very skilled, were able to quickly steal the purses and wallets of the people and run away with them.

It is true that people were not being killed by cars. However, they were killed or seriously injured (hurt) by runaway horses, which were running around out of control. It was quite a common scene.

The situations and problems of American cities have not changed so much. We must look back at the past and try to learn from it so that developments can be made.


**A. Kitaptaki Orijinal Sorular**


*A. Fill in the blanks.*

1. The reasons for pollution in the streets in the 19th century were ____ and ____.

2. Cows and pigs were killed by ____.

3. Two of the problems which we had in the past and which we still have today are ____ and ____.

4. The city governments of the past had important problems with ____ because they were polluted and it was difficult to obtain pure water.

5. Purses and wallets were stolen by ____.

6. Real estate developers controlled ____ and ____.


*B. Mark the best choice.*

7. In "Most cities had their own slaughterhouses. Butchers were careless about the cows and pigs which they killed...", 'slaughterhouses' are places where ____.

   - a) animals are killed

   - b) butchers feed animals

   - c) butchers live

   - d) people buy meat

8. In "Because of the dirt, communicable diseases were a great problem. When one of these diseases was seen, the situation was dangerous for all of the people in that area.", 'communicable diseases' are ____.

   - a) diseases which one person can easily get from another person

   - b) a type of communication diseases

   - c) children's diseases

   - d) diseases for dangerous situations

9. In "However, they were killed or seriously injured (hurt) by runaway horses, which were running around out of control.", 'runaway horses' are horses which ____.

   - a) are killed by their owners

   - b) are injured

   - c) are no longer under the control of their owners

   - d) are used for transportation

10. The main idea of the first paragraph is that ____.

   - a) American cities are terrible places to live

   - b) things were better in the past in American cities

   - c) American cities were worse in the past than they are now

11. In "Crime was a great concern then, too. The police had to work hard to stop it.", 'Crime was a great concern then, too' means ____.

   - a) crime is still a big problem

   - b) crime is not a problem anymore

   - c) crime was a problem of the past only

12. According to the last paragraph, the writer thinks that ____.

   - a) things are getting worse

   - b) things are getting better

   - c) things can get better


*C. Answer the following questions.*

13. How were some people killed or injured in the streets in 19th-century cities?

14. Why did a lot of children die in the past?


**B. Ekstra Sınav Soruları (Proficiency / IELTS / TOEFL)**

1. *[Author's Purpose]* What is the author's main purpose in this passage?

   - A) To argue that modern cars cause worse pollution than 19th-century animals

   - B) To prove that 19th-century police departments completely eliminated street crime

   - C) To show that urban problems existed in an even worse form in the past and offer lessons for today

   - D) To describe how modern sewer systems were invented by real estate developers

2. *[Inference]* What can be inferred from the third paragraph about 19th-century American cities?

   - A) City officials intentionally hid the cause of water pollution from the public.

   - B) People in the 19th century did not yet understand how waste and dirt contaminated drinking water.

   - C) Pure water was unavailable because there were no rivers near American cities.

   - D) Real estate developers banned the construction of clean water pipelines.

3. *[Vocabulary in Context]* In the sentence "provide the population with both durable housing and a good-looking neighbourhood," the word "durable" is closest in meaning to ____.

   - A) crowded and inexpensive

   - B) recently painted

   - C) temporarily built

   - D) long-lasting and strong


### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Summary: Life in 19th-Century American Cities** (70-90 words): Write a short summary explaining why 19th-century American cities were actually worse than modern cities, mentioning the main problems described in the passage.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: The Most Urgent Urban Problem** (100-130 words): Which urban problem mentioned in the passage (pollution, poor planning, crime, or street accidents) do you think is the most urgent in modern cities today? Write a well-organized paragraph explaining your view with reasons and examples.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **communicable diseases** (*bulaşıcı hastalıklar*): Good hygiene helps prevent the spread of communicable diseases in crowded schools.

- **real estate developers** (*emlak geliştiricileri / müteahhitler*): Real estate developers are planning to build a new shopping centre near the river.

- **sewer system** (*kanalizasyon sistemi*): The city needs to upgrade its old sewer system before the heavy winter rains begin.

- **pickpockets** (*yankesiciler*): Tourists should watch out for pickpockets on crowded metro trains.

- **durable** (*dayanıklı, uzun ömürlü*): Parents should buy durable shoes for children who play outside every day.

- **a great concern** (*büyük bir endişe / kaygı kaynağı*): Air pollution has become a great concern for people living in industrial towns.


**Self-Assessment Checklist:**

- [ ] Geçmiş ve günümüz şehir yaşamı arasındaki benzerlik ve farkları açıkça karşılaştırdım mı?

- [ ] Paragrafta veya metinde akışı sağlamak için bağlaçları (however, as a result, therefore vb.) doğru kullandım mı?

- [ ] Yazımı dil bilgisi, noktalama ve kelime seçimi açısından kontrol ettim mi?

## Passage 102: Spas: Good or Bad?

- **ID:** `raw1_102` | **Seviye:** `B1` | **Konu:** Health & Spa Resorts | **Sayfa:** 148, 149, 150
- **Ses Dosyası:** `../audio/raw1/raw1_102_spas_good_or_bad.mp3` (*AU · en-AU-NatashaNeural · Hız -4%*)

### 1. Listening (Metni Okumadan Dinleyerek Çözün)

**A. Gap-Fill (Boşluk Doldurma)**

1. Every year, millions of visitors travel to spa resorts to drink and bathe in ____. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*

2. In Germany, France, and Austria, visitors' spa expenses are paid by the ____. *(İpucu: noun phrase)*

3. A British banker advised his staff not to visit spas because there is usually a ____ nearby. *(İpucu: noun)*


**B. Listening Multiple Choice**

1. Which resort is described as offering radioactive mud to its guests?

   - A) Baden Bein Wein in Austria

   - B) Baden Baden in Germany

   - C) Abano Terme in Italy

   - D) Vichy in France

2. What do visitors experience at the spa of Badgastein in Austria?

   - A) They swim across an underground cold lake wearing helmets.

   - B) They enter a mountain tunnel by truck and lie down naked in hot vapours.

   - C) They drink mineral water while hiking up an Austrian mountain.

   - D) They cover their bodies with cold mud inside a dark cave.

3. Why did a doctor express doubt about the usefulness of spas?

   - A) Spas are only effective for elderly patients with back pain.

   - B) The hot vapours in mountain tunnels can cause serious headaches.

   - C) Governments spend too much taxpayer money on luxury resorts.

   - D) A short stay at a spa cannot make you healthy if your overall lifestyle is unhealthy.


**C. True / False / Not Given**

1. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Water has been used as a medical treatment since ancient times.

2. [ ] True  [ ] False  [ ] Not Given — Visitors who tried the health tunnel in Badgastein complained that the experience was unpleasant.


### 2. Reading

Water is the oldest treatment in medical history. It has been used for its healing properties since the earliest days by men and women to soothe and heal the injured and hurting parts of their bodies. Thus, their ill bodies feel better and also more relaxed.

Today, spas, which use water and mud to heal people, are very famous in Europe. Millions of visitors each year go to their favourite spa resort to drink and bathe in mineral springs so that their backs, arms and legs will stop aching or their rough skin will feel smooth again. Some of the most famous resorts are Baden Bein Wein in Austria, Vichy in France, Marienbad in Czechoslovakia, Baden Baden in Germany, where guests put hot mud on their bodies, and Abano Terme in Italy, a resort which offers health-giving radioactive mud to its guests.

Some treatments provide bizarre and strange experiences. The spa of Badgastein in Austria, for instance, has an old miner's tunnel, now a 'health tunnel'. You drive into the mountain in a truck and wear a helmet to protect your head. When you reach the depths of the tunnel, you take off your clothes and lie down naked while rich vapours surround you. Because of the heat, a person sweats and can lose hundreds of calories in an hour and thus decrease in weight. Visitors who have had this experience are very pleased and say that spa treatments are very relaxing.

But are spa resorts really beneficial? That is, do people really get something good and useful for their money?

Many governments think they do. German, French and Austrian visitors' spa expenses are paid by the national health system. Obviously, these governments believe in the healing factor of spas or they wouldn't let the health services pay so much money.

Not all experts, however, think spas are helpful. One doctor, for instance, recently reported, "It is silly to think that you can become a healthy person in a week or two at a spa when you have an unhealthy lifestyle."

Some other sources also tell us that we should be careful about spas. Not long ago, a British banker told members of his staff not to go to spas. He said, "There is usually a casino nearby where you can lose a lot of money in card games and the like."

### 3. Writing

- **Task 1 — Summary: The Debate Over Spa Resorts** (70-90 words): Summarize the main arguments for and against visiting spa resorts based on the passage.

- **Task 2 — Opinion Paragraph: Spas vs. a Healthy Lifestyle** (100-130 words): Do you agree with the doctor who said a short stay at a spa cannot make someone with an unhealthy lifestyle healthy? Write a paragraph sharing your opinion with reasons.


**Useful Vocabulary & Collocations:**

- **healing properties** (*iyileştirici özellikler*): Aloe vera is famous for its natural healing properties on minor burns.

- **soothe** (*yatıştırmak, hafifletmek*): Warm herbal tea can soothe a sore throat before bedtime.

- **beneficial** (*faydalı, yararlı*): Regular exercise is beneficial for both physical energy and mental focus.

- **bizarre** (*tuhaf, acayip*): The museum exhibited some bizarre sculptures that surprised all the visitors.

- **national health system** (*ulusal sağlık sistemi*): The national health system covers basic hospital treatments for all citizens.

- **unhealthy lifestyle** (*sağlıksız yaşam tarzı*): Changing to an unhealthy lifestyle with fast food and little sleep can harm your heart.
